We will frequently reference this equation when we need to quantify a vector field of this type.

4.1.
Preliminary tame estimates

Given
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we define a bilinear form associated to
L_{m}
via

(4.1.1)

B_{m}:H^{m}(\Omega)\times H^{m}(\Omega)\to\mathbb{R}\text{ via }B_{m}(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{j}^{m}\varphi_{0}\partial_{j}^{m}\varphi_{1}.

We have recorded a number of basic properties of
B_{m}
in Appendix
C.2
. Our first result here considers an estimate for
B_{m}
in which we are able to a save a derivative thanks to integration by parts.

Lemma 4.1

(A bilinear estimate)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Suppose that
\varphi\in H^{m}(\Omega)
,
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(X\cdot e_{n})=0
as well as (
4.0.2
) with
r=1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
, and that
\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)\in H^{m}(\Omega)
. Then we have the estimates

(4.1.2)

|B_{m}(\varphi,\nabla\cdot(X\varphi))|\lesssim\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2}+\rho^{-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}

and

(4.1.3)

|B_{m}(\varphi,\nabla\cdot(X\varphi))|\lesssim\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{m}}+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

Here the implicit constants depend on
m
,
\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
, and the dimension.

Proof.

Since
\varphi,\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)\in H^{m}(\Omega)
and
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we can apply the Leibniz rule to see that

(4.1.4)

\nabla\cdot(X\partial_{j}^{m}\varphi)=\partial_{j}^{m}\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\binom{m}{k}\nabla\cdot(\partial_{j}^{k}X\partial_{j}^{m-k}\varphi)\in L^{2}(\Omega),

and so
\partial_{j}^{m}\varphi\in H^{0}_{X}(\Omega)
, the space defined by (
B.2.1
) in Appendix
B.2
. By introducing commutators and employing Proposition
B.9
, we obtain the identity

(4.1.5)

B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{\nabla\cdot X}{2}\partial_{j}^{m}\varphi+\nabla\cdot([\partial_{j}^{m},X]\varphi)\Big)\partial_{j}^{m}\varphi.

Hence, we have the estimate

(4.1.6)

|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|\lesssim\Big(\lVert DX\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},X]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\Big)\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}.

According to Corollary
D.8
, we have the estimate

(4.1.7)

\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},X_{0}]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}X_{0}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert\partial_{j}X_{0}\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m,\par\end{cases}

and

(4.1.8)

\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},X_{1}]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}X_{1}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\lVert\partial_{j}X_{1}\rVert_{W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Estimates (
4.1.7
) and (
4.1.8
) combine to show that

(4.1.9)

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},X]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

This establishes (
4.1.3
).

We now continue to prove (
4.1.2
). In the latter case of (
4.1.9
), we use interpolation and Young’s inequality to bound

(4.1.10)

\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}{m-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}^{\frac{m}{m-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
\lesssim_{\rho_{\mathrm{max}}}\rho^{-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}.

Together, (
4.1.9
) and (
4.1.10
) provide the bound

(4.1.11)

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},X]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\rho^{-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Now we return to (
4.1.6
) and plug in (
4.1.11
) to derive the estimate

(4.1.12)

|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|\lesssim\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2}+\rho^{-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}},

which is (
4.1.2
).
∎

For the next two results we use the notation
\nabla_{X}\varphi=X\cdot\nabla\varphi
. The following rather technical lemma considers traces of the normal derivatives of
\nabla_{X}\varphi
when
X
has vanishing normal trace and
\varphi
has some vanishing normal derivative traces. The point is that the higher norms on normal derivative traces depend only on
X
and a lower norm of
\varphi
.

Lemma 4.2

(A trace estimate)
.

Suppose that
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})

satisfies
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(X\cdot e_{n})=0
and decomposes as
X=X_{0}+X_{1}
with
DX_{0}\in H^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
DX_{1}\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
. Assume additionally that
\varphi\in H^{2m+1}(\Omega)
and satisfies the Neumann conditions

(4.1.13)

\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega.

The we have the following normal derivative estimates for
\ell\in\{{0,1,\dots,m-1}\}
:

(4.1.14)

\lVert\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell}\nabla_{X}\varphi)\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m,\end{cases}

and

(4.1.15)

\lVert\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell}\nabla_{X}\varphi)\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lesssim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell,\infty}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Here the implicit constants depend on the domain, the dimension,
m
and
\ell
.

Proof.

We begin by computing the argument of the trace in the stated estimate. Fix
\ell\in\{{0,1,\dots,m-1}\}
. According to the Leibniz rule, we have

(4.1.16)

\partial_{n}^{m+\ell}\nabla_{X}\varphi=\sum_{k=0}^{m+\ell}\binom{m+\ell}{k}\partial_{n}^{m+\ell-k}X\cdot\nabla\partial_{n}^{k}\varphi\\
=\sum_{k=0}^{m+\ell}\binom{m+\ell}{k}\partial_{n}^{m+\ell-k}X\cdot(\nabla_{\|},0)\partial_{n}^{k}\varphi+\sum_{k=0}^{m+\ell}\binom{m+\ell}{k}\partial_{n}^{m+k-\ell}(X\cdot e_{n})\partial_{n}^{k+1}\varphi.

Applying
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}
and using that
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0
and
X\cdot e_{n}=0
on
\partial\Omega
, we compute

(4.1.17)

\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell}\nabla_{X}\varphi)=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\binom{m+\ell}{k}\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell-k}X\cdot(\nabla_{\|},0)\partial_{n}^{k}\varphi)\\
+\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}\binom{m+\ell}{k}\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell-k}(X\cdot e_{n})\partial_{n}^{k+1}\varphi)+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\ell<m-1,\\
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(X\cdot e_{n}\partial_{n}^{2m}\varphi)&\text{if }\ell=m-1.\end{cases}\\
=\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}\binom{m+\ell}{k}\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell-k}X\cdot\nabla\partial_{n}^{k}\varphi)+\binom{m+\ell}{m-1}\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{\ell+1}X\cdot(\nabla_{\|},0)\partial_{n}^{m-1}\varphi)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

We then take
H^{-1/2}
-norms and handle
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
separately.

We bound
\mathbf{I}
via the embedding
H^{1/2}\hookrightarrow H^{-1/2}
and the
H^{1}\to H^{1/2}
continuity of the trace map:

(4.1.18)

\lVert\mathbf{I}\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}\lVert\partial_{n}^{m+\ell-k}X\cdot\nabla\partial_{n}^{k}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}\lVert D^{m+\ell-k}X\otimes D^{k+1}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
+\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\lVert D^{m+1+\ell-k}X\otimes D^{k+1}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}=\mathbf{III}+\mathbf{IV}.

For
\mathbf{III}
and
\mathbf{IV}
we employ the splitting
X=X_{0}+X_{1}
and Corollary
D.7
:

(4.1.19)

\mathbf{III}\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}\big(\lVert D^{m-1-k}(D^{1+\ell}X_{0})\otimes D^{k+1}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert D^{m-1-k}(D^{1+\ell}X_{1})\otimes D^{k+1}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\big)\\
\lesssim\lVert D^{1+\ell}X_{0},D^{1+\ell}X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor},W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert D^{1+\ell}X_{0},D^{1+\ell}X_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m,\end{cases}

and similarly,

(4.1.20)

\mathbf{IV}\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\big(\lVert D^{m-1-k}(D^{2+\ell}X_{0})\otimes D^{k+1}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert D^{m-1-k}(D^{2+\ell}X_{1})\otimes D^{k+1}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\big)\\
\lesssim\lVert D^{2+\ell}X_{0},D^{2+\ell}X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor},W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert D^{2+\ell}X_{0},D^{2+\ell}X_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

Hence, the estimate on
\mathbf{I}
we obtain is

(4.1.21)

\lVert\mathbf{I}\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

For
\mathbf{II}
we make a perfect divergence to exploit the negative norm in the
H^{-1/2}
space, i.e.

(4.1.22)

\partial_{n}^{\ell+1}X\cdot(\nabla_{\|},0)\partial_{n}^{m-1}\varphi=(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot(\partial_{n}^{\ell+1}X\partial_{n}^{m-1}\varphi)-(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot(\partial_{n}^{\ell+1}X)\partial_{n}^{m-1}\varphi.

Then again by the continuity of the trace map and Corollary
D.7
, we find that

(4.1.23)

\lVert\mathbf{II}\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{n}^{\ell+1}X\partial_{n}^{m-1}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot(\partial_{n}^{\ell+1}X)\partial_{n}^{m-1}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert D^{\ell+1}X\otimes D^{m-1}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\\
+\lVert D^{\ell+2}X\otimes D^{m-1}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

Combining the estimates for
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
completes the proof of estimate (
4.1.14
).

For the remaining estimate, we first take
1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m
and use interpolation and Young’s inequality to bound

(4.1.24)

\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}^{\frac{m}{m-(1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor)}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Hence, for any
m
we see that (
4.1.15
) follows.
∎

As an application of the previous result, we develop the following estimate for the map
B_{m}
. As was the case for Lemma
4.1
, the point of this estimate is that the right hand side depends on fewer derivatives that one might expect from a naïve inspection of the left hand side.

Lemma 4.3

(Another bilinear estimate)
.

Suppose that
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(X\cdot e_{n})=0
and decomposes as
X=X_{0}+X_{1}
, where
DX_{0}\in H^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
DX_{1}\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Assume that
\varphi\in H^{3m}(\Omega)
satisfies the Neumann conditions
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Then

(4.1.25)

|B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)|\lesssim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}\rangle^{6}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Here the implicit constant depends on the domain, the dimension, and
m
.

Proof.

We begin by integrating by parts. Thanks to Lemma
C.6
, we have the identity

(4.1.26)

B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)=\int_{\Omega}L_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi)L_{m}\varphi+\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\Big(\int_{\Sigma}-\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)(-1)^{\ell}\partial_{n}^{m+\ell}(\nabla_{X}\varphi)\partial_{n}^{m-1-\ell}L_{m}\varphi.

Hence, by trace theory, we have the estimate

(4.1.27)

|B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)|\lesssim\Big|\int_{\Omega}L_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi)L_{m}\varphi\Big|+\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\lVert\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m+\ell}(\nabla_{X}\varphi))\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m-\ell}}=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

We will handle
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
separately.

For
\mathbf{I}
we first use
\nabla_{X}\varphi=\nabla\cdot(\varphi X)-\varphi\nabla\cdot X
to expand

(4.1.28)

\int_{\Omega}L_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi)L_{m}\varphi=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot L_{m}(\varphi X)L_{m}\varphi-L_{m}(\varphi(\nabla\cdot X))L_{m}\varphi\\
=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot(XL_{m}\varphi)L_{m}\varphi+\nabla\cdot([L_{m},X]\varphi)L_{m}\varphi-L_{m}(\varphi(\nabla\cdot X))L_{m}\varphi,

and then employ Proposition
B.9
on the first term on the right. This yields the bound

(4.1.29)

\mathbf{I}\leqslant\Big|\int_{\Omega}\frac{\nabla\cdot X}{2}(L_{m}\varphi)^{2}\Big|+\Big|\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot([L_{m},X]\varphi)L_{m}\varphi\Big|+\Big|\int_{\Omega}L_{m}(\varphi(\nabla\cdot X))L_{m}\varphi\Big|\\
\lesssim\Big(\lVert DX\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\lVert[\partial_{j}^{2m},X]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\Big)\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}.

We next use Corollary
D.7
, followed by interpolation and Young’s inequality, to estimate

(4.1.30)

\lVert(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }2m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<2m,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

On the other hand, for
j\in\{{1,\dots,n}\}
, we use Corollary
D.8
and another interpolation and Young inequality argument to estimate

(4.1.31)

\lVert[\partial_{j}^{2m},X]\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }2m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<2m,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Upon synthesizing (
4.1.29
),(
4.1.30
), and (
4.1.31
), we learn that

(4.1.32)

\mathbf{I}\lesssim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{2}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Now we turn our attention to
\mathbf{II}
. First we input the estimate (
4.1.15
) from Lemma
4.2
:

(4.1.33)

\mathbf{II}\leqslant\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell,\infty}}\rangle\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m-\ell}}\\
+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m-\ell}}.

Next we utilize the interpolation inequalities

(4.1.34)

\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m-\ell}}\lesssim\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}^{\frac{\ell}{m-1}}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{\frac{m-1-\ell}{m-1}},

(4.1.35)

\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\ell,\infty}}\\
\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}^{\frac{m-1-\ell}{m-1}}\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}^{\frac{\ell}{m-1}},

and

(4.1.36)

\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m+\ell}\times W^{1+m+\ell,\infty}}\lesssim\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m}\times W^{1+m,\infty}}^{\frac{m-1-\ell}{m-1}}\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}^{\frac{\ell}{m-1}},

together with Young’s inequality to bound

(4.1.37)

\mathbf{II}\leqslant\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\big(\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\rangle\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}\rangle\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\big)\\
+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\big(\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m}\times W^{1+m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{1}}\big).

Upon combining (
4.1.32
) and (
4.1.37
), we see that

(4.1.38)

\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}\lesssim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\rangle(\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{2}+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}})\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m+1}}.

We then interpolate again:
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}
, and if
m\geqslant 2
then we use Young’s inequality to bound

(4.1.39)

\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}}\rangle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m+1}}\\
\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}}\rangle^{\frac{3m}{m-1}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

By combining these with (
4.1.38
), we prove (
4.1.25
).
∎

4.2.
Some results on steady transport equations and elliptic regularizations

With the lemmas from Section
4.1
in hand, we may now begin to derive the required precise estimates for steady transport equations and their regularizations. The former is much simpler, and our first result gives all of the necessary a priori estimates.

Proposition 4.4

(A priori estimates for steady transport)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
\varphi\in H^{m}(\Omega)
,
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
4.0.2
) with
r=1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
, and that
\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)\in H^{m}(\Omega)
. Suppose additionally that
\Lambda\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega)
is such that
\Lambda>0
,
1/\Lambda\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
, and

(4.2.1)

\Lambda\varphi+\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)=\psi\text{ in }\Omega.

There exists a
\rho^{(m)}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
m
,
\Lambda
, and the dimension, such that if
0<\rho\leqslant\rho^{(m)}
, then

(4.2.2)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

Here the implicit constant depends only on
m
,
\rho^{(m)}
,
\Lambda
, and the dimension.

Proof.

We begin by multiplying (
4.2.1
) by
\varphi
and applying Proposition
B.9
to obtain the identity

(4.2.3)

\int_{\Omega}\Big(\Lambda+\frac{\nabla\cdot X}{2}\Big)\varphi^{2}=\int_{\Omega}\varphi\psi.

Selecting
\rho^{(0)}
sufficiently small, depending on
\lVert 1/\Lambda\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
, and using Cauchy-Schwarz on the right hand side, we obtain the a priori estimate

(4.2.4)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Now suppose that
m\geqslant 1
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho^{(0)}
. Plugging (
4.2.1
) into the bilinear form
B_{m}
from (
4.1.1
), we have the identity
B_{m}(\Lambda\varphi+\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)=B_{m}(\psi,\varphi)
, and hence

(4.2.5)

B_{m}(\Lambda\varphi,\varphi)\leqslant\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|.

By applying Gårding’s inequality, Lemma
C.5
, we thus obtain the bound

(4.2.6)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|.

Now we invoke Cauchy’s inequality for the
\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}
term, estimate (
4.2.4
) for the
\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}
term, and estimate (
4.1.3
) from Lemma
4.1
for the final term above; these then yield the bound

(4.2.7)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H^{m}}+\rho\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{m}}\\
+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m.\end{cases}

We define
\rho^{(m)}\in(0,\rho^{(0)}]
to be sufficiently small so that when taking
\rho\leqslant\rho^{(m)}
we can absorb the right hand side’s
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2}
-contribution with the left. Once this is done, estimate (
4.2.2
) follows from one last application of Cauchy’s inequality to the final term in (
4.2.7
).
∎

The remainder of this subsection develops corresponding a priori estimates for regularized steady transport equations, with the right uniformities with respect to the approximation parameters. This is more complicated than Proposition
4.4
since the theory now has to account for a rather unhappy marriage of elliptic and hyperbolic structures. Our first result in this direction handles the case of low regularity, namely
L^{2}
, data.

Proposition 4.5

(A priori estimate for regularized steady transport with data in
L^{2}
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
L_{m}
be the operator given in (
3.5.6
). Suppose that
\psi\in L^{2}(\Omega)
,
\varphi\in H^{2m}(\Omega)
,
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
4.0.2
) with
r=1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
,
\Lambda\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega)
satisfies
\Lambda>0
and
1/\Lambda\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
, and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Assume additionally that the equations

(4.2.8)

\begin{cases}N^{-1}\Lambda L_{m}\varphi+\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)=\psi&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega\end{cases}

are satisfied. If

(4.2.9)

N\gtrsim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor},

then we have the a priori estimate

(4.2.10)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}},

where the implied constants depend on
m
, the dimension,
\Lambda
, and
\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
.

Proof.

We multiply the first equation in (
4.2.8
) by
N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi
and integrate over
\Omega
. By applying Lemma
C.6
on the
\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)
-term, we acquire the identity

(4.2.11)

\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda(L_{m}\varphi)^{2}+\frac{1}{N}B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)=\int_{\Omega}\psi\frac{1}{N}L_{m}\varphi.

Now we use the hypotheses on
\Lambda
paired with Cauchy’s inequality and absorption:

(4.2.12)

\frac{1}{N^{2}}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{N}|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|.

We now handle the
B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)
term by using estimate (
4.1.2
) from Lemma
4.1
(with
\rho=\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
) and then interpolating:

(4.2.13)

\frac{1}{N}|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{N^{1/2}}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\frac{\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{1/2}}{N^{1/2}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{3/2}.

Therefore, if (
4.2.9
) is satisfied, then

(4.2.14)

\frac{1}{N}|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\frac{\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{1/2}}{N^{1/2}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{3/2}.

Upon returning to (
4.2.12
), we see that

(4.2.15)

\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim N\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}+N^{1/2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{1/2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{1/2}+N^{3/4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{1/4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{3/4}.

As the boundary conditions in (
4.2.8
) are satisfied, we may then invoke the a priori estimate for
L_{m}
from Lemma
C.7
to acquire the bound

(4.2.16)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi,L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}\lesssim N\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}+N^{1/2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{1/2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{1/2}+N^{3/4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}^{1/4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{3/4}.

The proof is complete upon applying Young’s inequality in order to absorb the
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}
terms from the right onto the left.
∎

Our next two results consider what happens when the data for a regularized steady transport equation is of higher regularity than
L^{2}
. The first of these analyzes the bonus regularity of the solution gained from the vanishing elliptic term.

Proposition 4.6

(A priori estimate for regularized steady transport with high regularity data, 1)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Suppose that
\psi\in H^{m}(\Omega)
,
\varphi\in H^{3m}(\Omega)
,
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
4.0.2
) with
r=2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
,
\Lambda\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega)
satisfies
\Lambda>0
and
1/\Lambda\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
, and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Assume additionally that (
4.2.8
) is satisfied. If

(4.2.17)

N\gtrsim\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor},

then we have the a priori estimate

(4.2.18)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lesssim N\lVert\psi,\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{m}}+N\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Here the implied constants depend only on
m
, the dimension,
\Lambda
, and
\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
.

Proof.

We rearrange the first equation in (
4.2.8
) as

(4.2.19)

N^{-1}\Lambda L_{m}\varphi=\psi-(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi-\nabla_{X}\varphi

and then take the
B_{m}
-product of the above equation with
N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi
. After applying Gårding’s inequality, Lemma
C.5
, followed by the
L^{2}
-a priori estimate of Proposition
4.5
, we find that

(4.2.20)

N^{-2}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{2}+N^{-1}\lVert\psi,(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{m}}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+N^{-1}|B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)|.

Hence, by Cauchy’s inequality we get the bound

(4.2.21)

N^{-2}\lVert L_{m}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\psi,(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{m}}^{2}+N^{-1}|B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)|.

Now we apply the a priori estimates for
L_{m}
of Lemma
C.7
to learn that

(4.2.22)

N^{-2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\psi,(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{m}}^{2}+N^{-1}|B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)|.

By Corollary
D.7
and a familiar argument using interpolation and Young’s inequality, we deduce that

(4.2.23)

\lVert(\nabla\cdot X)\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }m\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<m,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

On the other hand, Lemma
4.3
shows that

(4.2.24)

|B_{m}(\nabla_{X}\varphi,L_{m}\varphi)|\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}}\rangle^{6}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Inserting (
4.2.23
) and (
4.2.24
) into (
4.2.22
) and using Cauchy’s inequality then shows that

(4.2.25)

N^{-2}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{3m}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{m}}^{2}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
+N^{-1}\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}\rangle^{6}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}^{2},

and upon combining this with hypothesis (
4.2.17
) we deduce the bound

(4.2.26)

N^{-2}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{3m}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{m}}^{2}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This is the stated estimate.
∎

Next, we aim to estimate the norm of the solution to a regularized steady transport equation in the same space as the regular data, but independently of the approximation parameter.

Proposition 4.7

(A priori estimate for regularized steady transport with high regularity data, 2)
.

Let
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Suppose that
\psi\in H^{m}(\Omega)
,
\varphi\in H^{3m}(\Omega)
,
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
4.0.2
) with
r=1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
. Further suppose that, for
i\in\{{0,1}\}
,
\Lambda_{i}\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega)
satisfies
\Lambda_{i}>0
and
1/\Lambda_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
, and that the equations

(4.2.27)

\begin{cases}\Lambda_{0}\varphi+N^{-1}\Lambda_{1}L_{m}\varphi+\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)=\psi&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

are satisfied. There exists a
\widetilde{\rho}^{(m)}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on the dimension,
m
,
\Lambda_{0}
, and
\Lambda_{1}
such that if
0<\rho\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}^{(m)}
, then we have the a priori estimate

(4.2.28)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}},

where the implicit constant depends only on
m
,
\widetilde{\rho}^{(m)}
,
\Lambda_{0}
,
\Lambda_{1}
, and the dimension.

Proof.

We test the first equation in (
4.2.27
) with
L_{m}\varphi
in the
L^{2}(\Omega)
inner product and integrate by parts via Lemma
C.6
to obtain the identity

(4.2.29)

B_{m}(\Lambda_{0}\varphi,\varphi)+N^{-1}\lVert\sqrt{\Lambda_{1}}L_{m}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=B_{m}(\psi,\varphi)+B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi).

Hence,
B_{m}(\Lambda_{0}\varphi,\varphi)\leqslant\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+|B_{m}(\nabla\cdot(X\varphi),\varphi)|
. This is exactly inequality (
4.2.5
) from Proposition
4.4
. We can therefore argue in exactly the same way here to reach the desired conclusion.
∎

The final result of this section is the culmination of our steady transport analysis. Essentially, we interpolate between the low regularity estimate of Proposition
4.5
and the high regularity estimates of Propositions
4.6
and
4.7
.
In doing so, we aim to preserve a key structure of the previous estimates: all norms involving the vector field are multiplied by the lowest regularity norms of the data or solution. This requires some fine interpolation results, which are proved in Appendix
A.3
.

Theorem 4.8

(Estimates for regularized steady transport)
.

Let
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
j\in\{{1,\dots,m}\}
. Suppose that
\psi\in H^{j}(\Omega)
,
\varphi\in H^{j+2m}(\Omega)
, and
X\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
4.0.2
) with
r=2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{max}}
. Further suppose
\Lambda_{i}\in W^{\infty,\infty}(\Omega)
satisfies
\Lambda_{i}>0
and
1/\Lambda_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
for
i\in\{{0,1}\}
, and that the equations

(4.2.30)

\begin{cases}\Lambda_{0}\varphi+N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi+\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X\varphi)=\psi&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega\end{cases}

are satisfied. There exists a
\overline{\rho}^{(m)}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
m
,
\Lambda_{0}
, and
\Lambda_{1}
, such that if

(4.2.31)

\max\{{\rho,\lVert X_{0}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}},\lVert X_{1}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}}\}\leqslant\overline{\rho}^{(m)}

and

(4.2.32)

N\gtrsim\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m,\infty}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor},

then we have the a priori estimate

(4.2.33)

\lVert\varphi,\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X\varphi),N^{-1}\varphi\rVert_{H^{j}\times H^{j}\times H^{j+2m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{j}}+\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+2m}\times W^{1+2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Here the implied constants depend only on
\Lambda_{0}
,
\Lambda_{1}
,
m
, the dimension, and
\overline{\rho}^{(m)}
.

Proof.

We let
\overline{\rho}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be such that

(4.2.34)

\max\{{\rho,\lVert X_{0}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}},\lVert X_{1}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}}\}\leqslant\overline{\rho}.

Throughout the proof we will take
\overline{\rho}
to be ever smaller to meet various criteria. Ultimately, we then define
\overline{\rho}^{(m)}
to be the value for
\overline{\rho}
we have at the end of the proof.

For
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, we will also make use of the Banach space

(4.2.35)

Z(m,\ell)=\{{\phi\in H^{\ell+2m}(\Omega)\;:\;\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{m}\phi)=\cdots=\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\phi)=0}\}

and the bounded linear map
L:Z(m,\ell)\to H^{\ell}(\Omega)
defined via
L\varphi=\Lambda_{0}\varphi+N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi+\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X\varphi)
. We divide the remainder of the proof into several steps.

Step 1
: Establishing invertibility. We claim that
L
is a Banach isomorphism for every
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. This follows from standard elliptic theory arguments once we establish the fact that the bilinear form
\mathfrak{B}
defined by

(4.2.36)

H^{m}(\Omega)\times H^{m}(\Omega)\ni(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{0})\overset{\mathfrak{B}}{\mapsto}\int_{\Omega}\Lambda_{0}\varphi_{0}\varphi_{1}+\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X\varphi_{0})\varphi_{1}+N^{-1}B_{m}(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1})\in\mathbb{R}

is coercive when
\overline{\rho}
is sufficiently small. To prove coercivity, we first use Proposition
B.9
to compute

(4.2.37)

\mathfrak{B}(\varphi,\varphi)=\int_{\Omega}\big(\Lambda_{0}+2^{-1}\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X)\big)\varphi^{2}+N^{-1}B_{m}(\varphi,\varphi).

In light of (
4.0.2
), (
4.2.34
), and the Sobolev embeddings, we may bound

(4.2.38)

\left\lVert\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leqslant\left\lVert\Lambda_{1}\nabla\cdot X\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert\nabla\Lambda_{1}\cdot X\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim\overline{\rho},

where the implicit constant depends on
\Lambda_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1}
. Therefore, if we take
0<\overline{\rho}\leqslant\overline{\rho}^{(0)}
sufficiently small, we get that

(4.2.39)

\mathfrak{B}(\varphi,\varphi)\gtrsim\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}+N^{-1}B_{m}(\varphi,\varphi),

and hence Lemma
C.5
shows that
\mathfrak{B}
is coercive.

Step 2
: Low-norm estimates on the inverse. Assume that
\psi\in L^{2}(\Omega)
,
\varphi\in Z(m,0)
, and that
L\varphi=\psi
. According to (
4.2.39
), we have the estimate
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{B}(\varphi,\varphi)=\int_{\Omega}\psi\varphi
, and hence

(4.2.40)

\lVert L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}=\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Now we may invoke Proposition
4.5
(the hypotheses of which are satisfied thanks to (
4.2.32
)) followed by (
4.2.40
) to see that
\lVert L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{H^{2m}}=\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert\psi-\Lambda_{0}\varphi,\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}\lesssim N\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}
. Thus, we have shown that
L^{-1}
maps
L^{2}(\Omega)
into
Z(m,0)\subset H^{2m}(\Omega)
with the operator bounds

(4.2.41)

\lVert L^{-1}\psi,N^{-1}L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times H^{2m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Step 3
: High-norm estimates on the inverse. Now assume that
\psi\in H^{m}(\Omega)
and
\varphi\in Z(m,m)\subset H^{3m}(\Omega)
satisfy
L\varphi=\psi
. Note that this equation is equivalent to

(4.2.42)

\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}\varphi+\nabla\cdot(X\varphi)+N^{-1}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}L_{m}\varphi=\widetilde{\psi},

where
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}=\Lambda_{0}/\Lambda_{1}
,
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}=1/\Lambda_{1}
, and
\widetilde{\psi}=(\psi-\varphi X\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1})/\Lambda_{1}
. We take
\overline{\rho}\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}^{(m)}
, where the latter is given by Proposition
4.7
, and then apply the proposition to gain the bound

(4.2.43)

\lVert L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{\psi}\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\widetilde{\psi}\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Thanks to Corollary
D.7
, interpolation, and Young’s inequality, we have that

(4.2.44)

\lVert\widetilde{\psi}\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\overline{\rho}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert X_{0}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1},X_{1}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{H^{m}\times W^{m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

On the other hand, (
4.2.41
) provides the bound

(4.2.45)

\lVert\widetilde{\psi}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

We combine (
4.2.40
), (
4.2.43
), (
4.2.44
), and (
4.2.45
) and then take
\overline{\rho}\leqslant\overline{\rho}^{(m)}\leqslant\overline{\rho}^{(0)}
to be sufficiently small so that the right hand side’s
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}
-contribution can be absorbed by the left; this results in the bound

(4.2.46)

\lVert L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+m}\times W^{1+m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

We next apply Proposition
4.6
followed by estimates (
4.2.46
) and (
4.2.41
):

(4.2.47)

N^{-1}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi-\Lambda_{0}\varphi,\varphi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert D(\Lambda_{1}X_{0}),D(\Lambda_{1}X_{1})\rVert_{H^{2m}\times W^{2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+2m}\times W^{1+2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

The culmination of this analysis is that we have shown that
L^{-1}
maps
H^{m}(\Omega)
into
Z(m,m)\subset H^{3m}(\Omega)
with the operator bounds

(4.2.48)

\lVert L^{-1}\psi,N^{-1}L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{H^{m}\times H^{3m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+2m}\times W^{1+2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Step 4
: Interpolation and conclusion. Let
\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}
denote a Stein extension operator for
\Omega
(see Definition
A.2
) and let
\mathfrak{R}_{\Omega}
denote the operator given by restriction to
\Omega
of functions defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
(see Example
2.16
). We define a map
T\in\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap\mathcal{L}(H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{3m}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
via the formula
T=\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}L^{-1}\mathfrak{R}_{\Omega}
. Thanks to the previous steps, we know that for
A=\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+2m}\times W^{1+2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
,
T
satisfies the operator bounds

(4.2.49)

\begin{cases}\lVert T\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}&\text{for all }\psi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\\
\lVert T\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+A\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}&\text{for all }\psi\in H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\end{cases}

and

(4.2.50)

\begin{cases}\lVert T\psi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}&\text{for all }\psi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\\
\lVert T\psi\rVert_{H^{3m}}\lesssim N\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{m}}+NA\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}&\text{for all }\psi\in H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).\end{cases}

We are therefore in a position to apply Proposition
A.8
to deduce that
T\in\mathcal{L}(H^{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{j+2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
for
j\in\{{0,1,\dots,m}\}
, with the estimates

(4.2.51)

\lVert T\psi,N^{-1}T\psi\rVert_{H^{j}\times H^{j+2m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{j}}+A\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{j-m}}\text{ for all }\psi\in H^{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

By utilizing that
L^{-1}=\mathfrak{R}_{\Omega}T\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}
, we can port (
4.2.51
) to an estimate on
L^{-1}
, namely:

(4.2.52)

\lVert L^{-1}\psi,N^{-1}L^{-1}\psi\rVert_{H^{j}\times H^{j+2m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{j}}+\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+2m}\times W^{1+2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}

for all
\psi\in H^{j}(\Omega)
.

It remains to obtain an estimate on
\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}XL^{-1}\psi)
. For this we rearrange the equation as
\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X\varphi)=\psi-\Lambda_{0}\varphi-N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi
, take the norm in
H^{j}(\Omega)
of both sides, and apply the established estimates of (
4.2.52
). This yields the bound

(4.2.53)

\lVert\nabla\cdot(\Lambda_{1}X\varphi)\rVert_{H^{j}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{j}}+\langle\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+2m}\times W^{1+2m,\infty}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

The proof is complete upon combining (
4.2.52
) and (
4.2.53
).
∎

5.
Analysis of weak solutions to the principal part linear equations

In this section we study weak solutions to the PDE

(5.0.1)

\begin{cases}\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))=g&\text{in }\Omega,\\
-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}u+\varrho\nabla(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)-\gamma_{0}\nabla\cdot\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u=f&\text{in }\Omega,\\
-(\varrho q-\gamma_{0}\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u)e_{n}-\varsigma\Delta_{\|}\eta e_{n}=k&\text{on }\Sigma,\\
u\cdot e_{n}+\partial_{1}\eta=0&\text{on }\Sigma,\\
u=0&\text{on }\Sigma_{0}.\end{cases}

Here the given data are
g:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
,
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
k:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, as well as
\gamma_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and a vector field
v_{w_{0}}:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
defined via a fixed triple
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
as in (
3.4.11
) (see also Lemma
3.19
). The unknowns are
q:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
,
u:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\eta:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
. In other words, we are interested in the weak formulation principal part linear operator
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}
, which we recall is defined in (
3.5.14
).

The above system is not elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis, and Nirenberg
[
3
]
. Because of this and various other linear effects of the derivative loss, we are led to consider the following regularized version of (
5.0.1
) with parameters
\tau\in[0,1]
,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, and
m\geqslant 2
:

(5.0.2)

\begin{cases}\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)+\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))+N^{-1}L_{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)=g&\text{in }\Omega,\\
-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}u+\varrho\nabla(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)-\gamma_{0}\nabla\cdot\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u=f&\text{in }\Omega,\\
-(\varrho q-\gamma_{0}\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u)e_{n}-\varsigma\Delta_{\|}\eta e_{n}=k&\text{on }\Sigma,\\
u\cdot e_{n}+\partial_{1}\eta=N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\eta&\text{on }\Sigma,\\
u=0&\text{on }\Sigma_{0},\\
\partial_{n}^{m}q=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}q=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where the linear elliptic operator
L_{m}
is defined in (
3.5.6
). In other words, we are also considering in this section the weak formulation regularized principal part linear operators
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{\tau}}
, which are defined in (
3.5.16
).

The strategy is as follows. We begin in Section
5.1
by proving a priori estimates for weak solutions to the systems (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
) that are appropriately uniform with respect to the background solution,
N
, and
\tau
. In Section
5.2
we develop the existence theory for (
5.0.2
) from our a priori estimates and the method of continuity, which is the reason for including the homotopy parameter
\tau
. As it turns out, the problem with
\tau=0
can be solved by taking a two parameter limit of solutions to similar equations, which we solve with the help of the Lax-Milgram lemma.

5.1.
Estimates

In this subsection we prove a priori estimates for weak solutions to (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
). We first require the following technical lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
, and let
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
. Suppose that
\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Sigma)
has Fourier support in the punctured ball
\overline{B_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}(0,1)}\setminus\{{0}\}
. Then we have the estimate

(5.1.1)

\Big|\int_{\Omega}(\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}})\eta^{2}\Big|\lesssim\rho\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}},

where the implied constant depends only on the dimension, the various physical parameters, and
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
.

Proof.

First note that the support hypotheses on
\eta
imply that
\eta\in H^{\infty}(\Sigma)
(see, for instance, Proposition
B.1
). Second, we compute
\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}=\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}e_{1})
, and use this, integration by parts, Fubini-Tonelli, the fundamental theorem of calculus, and the fact that
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(v_{w_{0}}\cdot e_{n})=0
to rewrite

(5.1.2)

\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}e_{1})\eta^{2}=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot((v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}e_{1})\eta)\eta=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\Big((\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}(v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}e_{1})\eta\Big)\eta.

In turn, this readily implies that

(5.1.3)

\Big|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}(\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}})\eta^{2}\Big|\leqslant\Big[{(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}(v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}e_{1})\eta}\Big]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}[{\eta}]_{\dot{H}^{1}}.

By Proposition
B.4
, specifically (
B.1.25
),
[{\eta}]_{\dot{H}^{1}}\lesssim\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
, so it remains to estimate the
\dot{H}^{-1}
term on the right. For this we use the decomposition (
3.4.17
) of Lemma
3.19
, which allows us to rewrite

(5.1.4)

(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}(v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}/\mathfrak{g}e_{1})\eta=(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\eta+\partial_{1}\Big(\eta\int_{0}^{b}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}(\cdot,y)\cdot e_{1}\;\mathrm{d}y\Big)=\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2}.

We bound
\mathbf{I}_{1}
by using the fact that the integrand belongs to
L^{2}(\Omega)
(and tacitly using Proposition
B.1
, Remark
B.3
, and the first item of Lemma
3.19
):

(5.1.5)

[{\mathbf{I}_{1}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert v_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}^{(1)}\eta\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert\eta\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

We bound
\mathbf{I}_{2}
using the algebra properties of the specialized Sobolev spaces (see Proposition
B.2
) and the second item of Lemma
3.19
:

(5.1.6)

[{\mathbf{I}_{2}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\leqslant\Big\lVert\eta\Big(\int_{0}^{b}v_{\eta_{0}}^{(2)}(\cdot,y)\;\mathrm{d}y\Big)\Big\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim\Big\lVert\int_{0}^{b}v_{\eta_{0}}^{(2)}(\cdot,y)\;\mathrm{d}y\Big\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

Combining these bounds yields (
5.1.1
).
∎

With the lemma in hand, we are ready to study estimates of weak solutions to the principal part equations (
5.0.1
). Recall that the spaces
\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}
and
\mathbb{Y}^{-1}
are defined in equations (
3.1.3
) and (
3.1.6
), while the weak formulation operator
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}
is defined in (
3.5.14
). Also recall the surface tension
\varsigma
and viscosity
\upmu,\uplambda
hypotheses set forth in (
1.1.7
).

Proposition 5.2

(A priori estimates for weak solutions)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
, let
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
, and let
\gamma_{0}\in I
, where
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
is some interval. Suppose that
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}
and
(g,F)\in\mathbb{Y}^{-1}
satisfy the equation

(5.1.7)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,F).

There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{weak}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that if
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{weak}}
, then we have the a priori estimate

(5.1.8)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}}\lesssim\lVert g,F\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}.

The implicit constants and
\rho_{\mathrm{weak}}
depend on the physical parameters, the dimension,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1
: Reduction to
g=0
. We claim first that it suffices to prove the result under the specialized assumption that
g=0
. Indeed, suppose this special case has been proved and let
(q,u,\eta)
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
,
\gamma_{0}
, and
(g,F)
be related as in (
5.1.7
). With the help of the operator
\mathcal{B}_{0}
from Proposition
C.2
, we define
w\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
via
w=u-\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{B}_{0}g
. Since
\mathcal{B}_{0}g\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we have that
\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(w\cdot e_{n})=\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})=-\partial_{1}\eta
, and hence
(q,w,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}
. Recalling that
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}
is defined in (
3.5.12
), we calculate that

(5.1.9)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,w,\eta)=F-\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(0,\mathcal{B}_{0}g/\varrho,0)\text{ and }\nabla\cdot(\varrho w)+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))=0.

Thus, we can apply the special case to obtain the estimate

(5.1.10)

\lVert q,w,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}}\lesssim\lVert F-\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(0,\mathcal{B}_{0}g/\varrho,0)\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim\lVert g,F\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}.

To switch from
w
to
u
in this bound we use the estimate provided by Proposition
C.2
, namely
\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert w\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert g\rVert_{\hat{H}^{0}}
. By chaining this together with the previous estimate we prove the result in general, which completes the proof of the claim. In the remaining steps we will prove the result in the special case that
g=0
.

Step 2
: A priori bound on
u
. We claim that the bound

(5.1.11)

\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert^{2}_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}}^{2})

holds. We make the following notational simplification for the Fourier space decompositions of
\eta
from (
B.1.4
):

(5.1.12)

\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}=\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta\text{ and }\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}=\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta.

According to Proposition
B.1
, we have that
\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\in L^{2}(\Sigma)
and
\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
. We test the equation

(5.1.13)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})=F-\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(0,0,\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})

with
u
; the left hand side of the resulting identity reads

(5.1.14)

\langle\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}),u\rangle=\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}\Big(\frac{\upmu(\varrho)}{2}|\mathbb{D}^{0}u|^{2}+\uplambda(\varrho)(\nabla\cdot u)^{2}\Big)-(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)\\
+\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}},

and we next aim to estimate the latter two terms on the right side this expression. To this end, we recall that

(5.1.15)

\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})=-\partial_{1}\eta\text{ and }-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)=\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)).

These allow us to compute

(5.1.16)

\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}=-\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}},\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}=0

and (employing the integration by parts trick of Proposition
B.9
)

(5.1.17)

\int_{\Omega}-(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}}{2}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})^{2}+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})=\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2}.

We handle
\mathbf{I}_{1}
first by expanding

(5.1.18)

\mathbf{I}_{1}=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}}{2}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})^{2}+\mathfrak{g}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})(\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}-\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})\\
+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mathfrak{g}^{2}\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}}{2}(\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}-\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})^{2}=\mathbf{I}_{1,1}+\mathbf{I}_{1,2}+\mathbf{I}_{1,3}.

According to the third item of Lemma
3.19
and Proposition
B.1
, we may estimate

(5.1.19)

|\mathbf{I}_{1,1}|\leqslant\lVert\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}})

and

(5.1.20)

|\mathbf{I}_{1,2}|\lesssim\lVert\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}-\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{2}).

For
\mathbf{I}_{1,3}
, we instead use Lemma
5.1
:

(5.1.21)

|\mathbf{I}_{1,3}|\lesssim\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{2}.

Upon piecing together the previous three estimates, we deduce that

(5.1.22)

|\mathbf{I}_{1}|\lesssim\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

Now we turn our attention to the term
\mathbf{I}_{2}
. Again, we first decompose

(5.1.23)

\mathbf{I}_{2}=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})+\mathfrak{g}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})(\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}-\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})=\mathbf{I}_{2,1}+\mathbf{I}_{2,2}.

\mathbf{I}_{2,1}
is handled via fourth item of Lemma
3.19
:

(5.1.24)

|\mathbf{I}_{2,1}|\leqslant\lVert\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

For
\mathbf{I}_{2,2}
we instead integrate by parts and use Fubini-Tonelli:

(5.1.25)

|\mathbf{I}_{2,2}|=\mathfrak{g}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\Big((\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\Big)(\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}-\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})\Big|\lesssim\Big[{(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}}\Big]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}[{\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}-\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}}]_{\dot{H^{1}}}.

By decomposing
v_{w_{0}}=\varrho^{\prime}\mathfrak{g}^{-1}e_{1}+v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}+v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}
(see (
3.4.17
)) and arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, we acquire the bound

(5.1.26)

\Big[{(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}}\Big]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

Hence,

(5.1.27)

|\mathbf{I}_{2}|\lesssim\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}),

and upon combining this with the
\mathbf{I}_{1}
estimate we deduce that

(5.1.28)

\Big|\int_{\Omega}-(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)\Big|\lesssim\rho(\lVert q\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}})+\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

With (
5.1.28
) and (
5.1.16
) in hand, we return to (
5.1.14
) to obtain the inequality

(5.1.29)

\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}\Big(\frac{\upmu(\varrho)}{2}|\mathbb{D}^{0}u|^{2}+\uplambda(\varrho)(\nabla\cdot u)^{2}\Big)\lesssim\lVert F-\mathscr{I}(0,0,\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\rho(\lVert q\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}})\\
+\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

This holds for all
\kappa\in(0,1)
and the implicit constant is independent of
\kappa
. Thus we may send
\kappa\to 0
and use the fact that, as a consequence of the dominated convergence theorem and the definition of the norm on the anisotropic Sobolev spaces (
B.1.2
),
\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\to 0
to arrive at the bound

(5.1.30)

\gamma_{0}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\upmu(\varrho)}{2}|\mathbb{D}^{0}u|^{2}+\uplambda(\varrho)(\nabla\cdot u)^{2}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\rho(\lVert q\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

Recall that the assumptions on
\upmu,\uplambda\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
are that
\upmu>0
and
\uplambda>0
if
n=2
, while
\upmu>0
and
\uplambda\geqslant 0
if
n\geqslant 3
. Thus, the bound (
5.1.11
) follows from (
5.1.30
), the inclusion
\gamma_{0}\in I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and either Proposition
A.4
in the case
n\geqslant 3
or else Proposition
A.3
in the case
n=2
.

Step 3
: A priori bound on
\nabla_{\|}\eta
. Next we claim that we have the a priori bound

(5.1.31)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

We first consider the case that surface tension is positive:
\varsigma>0
.
To prove this we will utilize the operator
\mathcal{B}_{2}
from Corollary
C.4
. Recall from the previous step that for
\kappa\in(0,1)
we have the decomposition
\eta=\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}+\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}
defined in (
5.1.12
). We test identity (
5.1.13
) with
w_{\kappa}\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, defined via

(5.1.32)

w_{\kappa}=-\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{B}_{2}(\varrho(b)\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}),

noting that
\nabla\cdot(\varrho w_{\kappa})=0
,
\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(w_{\kappa}\cdot e_{n})=-\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}
, and

(5.1.33)

\lVert w_{\kappa}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{\dot{H}^{-1}\cap H^{1/2}}\asymp\lVert\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{1/2}|\nabla_{\|}|\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{1/2}}.

The result is the identity

(5.1.34)

\langle\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}),w_{\kappa}\rangle=\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u):\nabla w_{\kappa}+\mathfrak{g}\varrho\nabla\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\cdot w_{\kappa}\\
+\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}},-\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}.

As

(5.1.35)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{1/2}}^{2}\lesssim\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}},-\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}},

we obtain the estimate

(5.1.36)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rVert^{2}_{H^{1/2}}\lesssim(\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}})\lVert w_{\kappa}\rVert_{H^{1}}.

Then (
5.1.31
) follows from this and (
5.1.33
) upon sending
\kappa\to 0
, which is valid since the implicit constants are independent of
\kappa
. This proves the claim in the case of positive surface tension.

Now we consider the case of vanishing surface tension,
\varsigma=0
, in dimension
n=2
. For this we simply look to the boundary condition satisfied by
u
, namely
\partial_{1}\eta=-\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})
. Since
n=2
and
\eta
is defined on
\Sigma\simeq\mathbb{R}
, we have
\partial_{1}\eta=\nabla_{\|}\eta
. Therefore we have
\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}=\lVert\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}
, so (
5.1.31
) holds.

Step 4
: A priori bound on
q
. We claim that we have the a priori bound

(5.1.37)

\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}+\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

To see this, we first let
w=\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{B}q\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, where
\mathcal{B}
is constructed in Corollary
C.3
. By construction, we have that
\nabla\cdot(\varrho w)=q
and

(5.1.38)

\lVert w\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Then we we test the identity
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta)=F
with
w
to see that

(5.1.39)

\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u):\nabla w+\mathfrak{g}\varrho\nabla\eta\cdot w-q^{2}-\varsigma\langle\Delta_{\|}\eta,\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(w\cdot e_{n})\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}=\langle F,w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}.

Estimate (
5.1.37
) readily follows from this, (
5.1.38
), and the estimates established in the previous steps. This proves the claim.

Step 5
: A priori bound on
\partial_{1}\eta
. Next we claim that

(5.1.40)

[{\partial_{1}\eta}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

First, we note that by using the decomposition of
v_{w_{0}}
from Lemma
3.19
, the continuity equation is equivalently written as

(5.1.41)

0=\nabla\cdot(\varrho u+v_{w_{0}}q)+\mathfrak{g}\nabla\cdot(v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\eta)+\mathfrak{g}\partial_{1}(v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}\cdot e_{1}\eta)+\varrho^{\prime}\partial_{1}\eta.

We integrate this in the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
coordinate over
(0,b)
; after recalling the identities
\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})+\partial_{1}\eta=0
and (
B.2.11
) from Proposition
B.10
, this results in the equality

(5.1.42)

\varrho(0)\partial_{1}\eta=(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}(\varrho u+v_{w_{0}}q+\mathfrak{g}v_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}^{(1)}\eta)+\mathfrak{g}\partial_{1}\Big(\eta\Big(\int_{0}^{b}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}(\cdot,y)\cdot e_{1}\;\mathrm{d}y\Big)\Big).

Hence, we may use the estimates from Lemma
3.19
, the fact that
\int_{0}^{b}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}(\cdot,y)\;\mathrm{d}y
has
r_{n-1}
as a band limit, the algebra properties of the anisotropic Sobolev spaces in Proposition
B.2
, and estimate (
B.2.12
) from Proposition
B.10
to bound

(5.1.43)

\varrho(0)[{\partial_{1}\eta}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert\varrho+v_{w_{0}}q+\mathfrak{g}v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\eta\rVert_{L^{2}}+\mathfrak{g}\Big\lVert\Big(\int_{0}^{b}v_{\eta_{0}}^{(2)}(\cdot,y)\;\mathrm{d}y\Big)\eta\Big\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

This proves the claim since
\varrho(0)>0
.

Step 6
: Conclusion. We now synthesize the claims of the previous steps to conclude. First, we take the bound from (
5.1.11
) and plug it into the right hand side of (
5.1.31
); this yields the inequality

(5.1.44)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\sqrt{\rho}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}}).

Second, we take (
5.1.44
) and (
5.1.11
) and insert them into the right hand side of (
5.1.37
) to get

(5.1.45)

\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\sqrt{\rho}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}}).

Now, while heeding to (
B.1.25
), we sum the estimates (
5.1.11
), (
5.1.40
), (
5.1.44
), and (
5.1.45
), and then use (
5.1.11
) and (
5.1.45
) on the right hand side; the resulting estimate is

(5.1.46)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\sqrt{\rho}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}.

We choose
\rho_{\mathrm{weak}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
sufficiently small so that when taking
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{weak}}
we can absorb the
\mathbb{X}_{-1}
contribution onto the left side and obtain the clean a priori bound

(5.1.47)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

It remains to only estimate the
L^{2}(\Omega)
-norm of
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)
in terms of the data. This is now a simple matter since we can isolate it in the continuity equation via
-\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)=\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)+\mathfrak{g}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta)
and then note that the right hand side is controlled by
\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}
(see in particular the fourth item of Lemma
3.19
).
∎

Before our next a priori estimates result, we need a lemma from the theory of regularized steady transport equations.

Lemma 5.3

(Regularized steady transport lemma)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
\tau\in[0,1]
,
\varphi\in H^{2m}(\Omega)
, and
\psi\in L^{2}(\Omega)
. Suppose that

(5.1.48)

\begin{cases}N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi+\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)=\psi&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where
L_{m}
is defined in (
3.5.6
). If
N\gtrsim\langle\tau\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{m}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
, then we have the a priori estimate

(5.1.49)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert\varphi,\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}},

where the implicit constant depends only on the physical parameters, the dimension,
m
, and
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
.

Proof.

Most of the work in verifying this result has already been executed in Section
4
. We invoke Proposition
4.5
with
\Lambda=1
and the decomposed vector field
X=X_{0}+X_{1}
, where
X=\tau v_{w_{0}}
,
X_{0}=\tau v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}
, and
X_{1}=\tau(v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}+\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\varrho^{\prime}e_{1})
,
with
v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}
and
v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}
as in Lemma
3.19
. The estimate (
5.1.49
) then follows from properties of the vector field
v_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}
stated in Lemma
3.19
, namely: for
s\in\{{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,m}\}
we have
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{s}\times W^{s,\infty}}\leqslant\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times W^{1+s,\infty}}\lesssim\tau\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\rangle
.
∎

Our next result studies estimates on weak solutions to the regularized equations (
5.0.2
). Recall that the spaces
\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}
, the norms
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}
, and the mappings
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{\tau}_{m,N}}
are defined in (
3.5.7
), (
3.5.9
), and (
3.5.16
), respectively.

Proposition 5.4

(A priori estimates for regularized weak solutions)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is from Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
, and
\gamma_{0}\in I
, where
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
is some interval. Suppose that
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
\tau\in[0,1]
,
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}
, and that
(g,F)\in\mathbb{Y}^{-1}
satisfy the equation
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{\tau}_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,F)
. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{weak,reg}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that if

(5.1.50)

0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{weak,reg}}\text{ and }N\gtrsim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{m}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor},

then we have the a priori estimate

(5.1.51)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\lVert g,F\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}.

The implicit constants and
\rho_{\mathrm{weak,reg}}
depend on the dimension, physical parameters,
m
,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We proceed in much the same way as in the proof of Proposition
5.2
, breaking to steps that mirror the structure of the argument used there.

Step 1
: Reduction to the case
g=0
. We claim that it suffices to prove the result in the special case that
g=0
. Indeed, the exact same argument used in the first step of Proposition
5.2
proves the claim here. In the remaining steps we will prove the result in the special case that
g=0
.

Step 2
: A priori bound on
u
. We claim that we have the a priori bound

(5.1.52)

\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}^{2}+\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}}).

To prove the claim we again use the Fourier space decomposition of (
5.1.12
), which again yields (
5.1.13
). We then test (
5.1.13
) with
u
to arrive at (
5.1.14
) as in the proof of Proposition
5.2
. Note, though, that in the present context we have the identities

(5.1.53)

\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})=-\partial_{1}\eta+N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\eta

and

(5.1.54)

-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)=\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))+N^{-1}L_{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta),

which are somewhat different from (
5.1.15
). We insert (
5.1.53
) and (
5.1.54
) into (
5.1.14
) and integrate by parts to see that

(5.1.55)

\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}\Big(\frac{\upmu(\varrho)}{2}|\mathbb{D}^{0}u|^{2}+\uplambda(\varrho)(\nabla\cdot u)^{2}\Big)+\frac{\tau\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}}{2}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\partial_{j}^{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}))^{2}\\
+\mathfrak{g}\int_{\Omega}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})\Big(\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})+\frac{1}{N}L_{m,\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\lVert(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})^{1/2}(\Delta_{\|})^{m/2-1/8}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}\\
=\langle F-\mathscr{I}(0,0,\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}),u\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}.

By combining (
5.1.55
), the Korn inequalities from Propositions
A.4
and
A.3
as well as the end of the second step of Proposition
5.2
, and the fact that
\tau\in[0,1]
, we obtain the estimate

(5.1.56)

\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\Big|\int_{\Omega}\frac{\nabla\cdot v_{w_{0}}}{2}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})^{2}\Big|+\mathfrak{g}\Big|\int_{\Omega}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})\Big(\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})+\frac{1}{N}L_{m,\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\Big)\Big|\\
+(\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}})\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}=\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2}+\mathbf{I}_{3}.

The term
\mathbf{I}_{1}
is identical to the term
\mathbf{I}_{1}
that appeared in (
5.1.17
), so we may use the same argument used in the second step of the proof of Proposition
5.2
to arrive at the estimate

(5.1.57)

\mathbf{I}_{1}\lesssim\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

For the term
\mathbf{I}_{2}
we bound

(5.1.58)

\mathbf{I}_{2}\leqslant\mathfrak{g}\Big|\int_{\Omega}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}})\Big|+\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{N}\Big|\int_{\Omega}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}})L_{m,\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\Big|=\mathbf{I}_{2,1}+\mathbf{I}_{2,2}.

Arguing as in (
5.1.23
)–(
5.1.27
), we find that

(5.1.59)

\mathbf{I}_{2,1}\lesssim\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

For the remaining piece we exploit the fact that
\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}
and
\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{H}}
have disjoint Fourier supports, together with (
B.1.25
) to see that

(5.1.60)

\mathbf{I}_{2,2}=\frac{g}{N}\Big|\int_{\Omega}qL_{m,\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert L_{m,\|}\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{L^{2}}.\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

Finally, we plug (
5.1.57
), (
5.1.59
), and (
5.1.60
) into (
5.1.56
) and then use Cauchy’s inequality on
\mathbf{I}_{3}
to deduce that

(5.1.61)

\lVert u\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert^{2}_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\rho(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{2})+\lVert\eta_{\kappa,\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

The implicit constant is independent of
\kappa
, so we may send
\kappa\to 0
to obtain (
5.1.52
) from this. The claim is proved.

Step 3
: A priori bound on
\nabla_{\|}\eta
. We claim that we have the a priori bound

(5.1.62)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Indeed, the claim is proved in exactly the same way as the claim from third step of the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Step 4
: A priori bound on
q
. We claim that we have the a priori bound

(5.1.63)

\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}+\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Again, this is proved in exactly the same way as the fourth step in the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Step 5
: High norm a priori bounds on
\eta
and
q
. We next claim that we have the bound

(5.1.64)

\lVert\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{2m}}+\lVert q\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N(\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}).

To see this, we begin by rewriting the equation

(5.1.65)

\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)+\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))+N^{-1}L_{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)=0

with the help of the splitting (
5.1.12
) with
\kappa=1
:

(5.1.66)

N^{-1}L_{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})+\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})=-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)-\mathfrak{g}\tau\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta_{1,\mathrm{L}})-\mathfrak{g}N^{-1}L_{m,\|}\eta_{1,\mathrm{L}}.

As
\partial_{n}^{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}})=0
, we are in a position to apply Lemma
5.3
, provided that
N\gtrsim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{m}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
, which we are free to assume; this yields the bound

(5.1.67)

\lVert q+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N(\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta\rVert_{H^{1/2}}).

Next we obtain a bound on
\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}
in
H^{2m}(\Sigma)
through the normal trace boundary condition. Indeed, we test equation (
5.1.53
) with
N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m+1/4}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}
to see that

(5.1.68)

N^{-2}\lVert(-\Delta_{\|})^{m}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}\leqslant N^{-1}\lVert\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lVert(-\Delta_{\|})^{m+1/4}\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{-1/2}},

and hence

(5.1.69)

\lVert\eta_{1,\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert u\rVert_{H^{1}}.

We then obtain (
5.1.64
) by combining (
5.1.67
) and (
5.1.69
). The claim is proved.

Step 6
: A priori bounds on
\partial_{1}\eta
. We claim that we have the a priori bound

(5.1.70)

[{\partial_{1}\eta}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+N^{-1}\lVert q\rVert_{H^{2m}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

As in the fifth step of the proof of Proposition
5.2
, we integrate equation (
5.1.54
) in the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
coordinate from
0
to
b
and isolate the non-small
\partial_{1}\eta
contributions:

(5.1.71)

((1-\tau)\varrho(b)+\tau\varrho(0))\partial_{1}\eta=(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}(\varrho u+\tau v_{w_{0}}q+\tau\mathfrak{g}v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\eta)+\tau\mathfrak{g}\partial_{1}\Big(\eta\int_{0}^{b}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}(\cdot,y)\cdot e_{1}\;\mathrm{d}y\Big)\\
+\frac{1}{N}L_{m,\|}\int_{0}^{b}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)+\frac{\varrho(b)}{N}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\eta=\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2}+\mathbf{I}_{3}+\mathbf{I}_{4}.

Fix
\kappa\in(0,1)
and set
\eta_{\kappa}=(\Uppi^{1/\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}-\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}})\eta
. We take the
L^{2}
-inner product of (
5.1.71
) with
|\nabla_{\|}|^{-2}\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}
. The terms involving
\mathbf{I}_{1}
and
\mathbf{I}_{2}
are estimated via Cauchy-Schwarz

(5.1.72)

\langle\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2},|\nabla_{\|}|^{-2}\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}\rangle_{L^{2},L^{2}}\lesssim[{\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}[{\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}},

but then the argument used in the fifth step of the proof of Proposition
