5.2
shows that

(5.1.73)

[{\mathbf{I}_{1}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}+[{\mathbf{I}_{2}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}},

so

(5.1.74)

\langle\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2},|\nabla_{\|}|^{-2}\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}\rangle_{L^{2},L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\right)[{\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}.

On the other hand, for the term involving
\mathbf{I}_{3}
we have

(5.1.75)

\langle\mathbf{I}_{3},|\nabla_{\|}|^{-2}\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}\rangle_{L^{2},L^{2}}=\frac{1}{N}\Big\langle|\nabla_{\|}|^{-1}L_{m,\|}\int_{0}^{b}q,|\nabla_{\|}|\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}\Big\rangle_{L^{2},L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\lVert q\rVert_{H^{2m-1}}[{\eta_{\kappa}}]_{\dot{H^{-1}}}.

while for the
\mathbf{I}_{4}
term we compute
\langle\mathbf{I}_{4},|\nabla_{\|}|^{-2}\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}\rangle_{L^{2},L^{2}}=0
. All together, these combine to show that

(5.1.76)

[{\partial_{1}\eta_{\kappa}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+N^{-1}\lVert q\rVert_{H^{2m}}+\rho\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}},

and since the implicit constant is independent of
\kappa
, we can send
\kappa\to 0
to arrive at (
5.1.70
). The claim is proved.

Step 7
: Conclusion. Finally, we derive the desired estimate (
5.1.51
) by combining the bounds from the previous steps and taking
\rho
to be sufficiently small to absorb, exactly as in the proof of the sixth step of Proposition
5.2
.
∎

5.2.
Existence of solutions to the regularization

In this subsection we prove the existence of weak solutions to the regularized problem (
5.0.2
) and then quickly deduce qualitative regularity. First we have our main existence result for weak solutions.

Theorem 5.5

(Existence of regularized weak solutions)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is from Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
m\geqslant 2
,
\tau\in[0,1]
,
(g,F)\in\mathbb{Y}^{-1}
, and
\gamma_{0}\in I
for some interval
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
. If (
5.1.50
) is satisfied, then there exists a unique
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}
satisfying

(5.2.1)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{\tau}_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,F).

Proof.

In functional analytic terms, we aim to prove that for every
\tau\in[0,1]
the operator
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{\tau}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{-1};\mathbb{Y}^{-1})
, which is well-defined thanks to Lemma
3.23
, is an isomorphism. We divide the proof of this into several steps.

Step 1
: Reduction to proving existence with
g=0
and
\tau=0
. We will achieve the reduction to
\tau=0
through the method of continuity (see, for instance, Theorem 5.2 in Gilbarg and Trudinger
[
35
]
). Indeed, the convex homotopy of operators
[0,1]\ni\tau\mapsto\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{\tau}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{-1};\mathbb{Y}^{-1})
satisfies
\tau
-uniform a priori estimates thanks to Proposition
5.4
. Thus, the method of continuity guarantees that
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{\tau}}
is an isomorphism for every
\tau\in[0,1]
if and only if
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{0}}
is an isomorphism, so we reduce to proving that
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{0}}
is an isomorphism.

Next, we note that the a priori estimate of Proposition
5.4
guarantees that
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}_{m,N}^{0}}
is injective, so we further reduce, by way of the bounded inverse theorem, to proving that this map is surjective. In turn, we reduce to proving the existence of solutions to (
5.2.1
) with
g=0
and
\tau=0
with the help of the operator
\mathcal{B}_{0}
, exactly as in the first step in the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

We have now shown that it suffices to establish the existence of solutions to (
5.2.1
) with
g=0
and
\tau=0
. Thus, in the remainder of the proof we set
g=0
and
\tau=0
.

Step 2
: Existence of a two parameter family of approximate solutions. We introduce the approximation parameters
M,K\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and fix data
F\in({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
. We then claim that there exists a collection

(5.2.2)

\{{(q_{M,K},u_{M,K},\eta_{M,K})}\}_{M,K\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\subset H^{2m}(\Omega)\times{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{2m}(\Sigma)

satisfying

(5.2.3)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q_{M,K},u_{M,K},\eta_{M,K})=F,

(5.2.4)

\begin{cases}N^{-1}\big(K^{-1}+L_{m}\big)(q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K})=-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u_{M,K})&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}(q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K})=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}(q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K})=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

and

(5.2.5)

\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u_{M,K}\cdot e_{n})+\partial_{1}\eta_{M,K}=N^{-1}(M^{-1}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4})\eta_{M,K}.

The high-level idea for producing these approximate solutions is as follows. First, we note that equation (
5.2.5
) determines
\eta_{M,K}
as a function of
u_{M,K}
. Then equation (
5.2.4
) determines
q_{M,K}
as a function of
u_{M,K}
and
\eta_{M,K}
. These allow us to rewrite (
5.2.3
) as an equation relating
F
and
u_{M,K}
alone, and it turns out that this can be solved by utilizing the Lax-Milgram lemma.

To prove the claim we begin by recalling that the space
{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
is defined by (
1.7.3
) and defining the bounded linear maps
\mathrm{p}_{K}:{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to H^{2m}(\Omega)
and

\upeta_{M}:{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to H^{2m}(\Sigma)
via

(5.2.6)

\mathrm{p}_{K}(u)=-N(K^{-1}+L_{m})^{-1}(\nabla\cdot(\varrho u))\text{ and }\upeta_{M}(u)=N(M^{-1}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}-N\partial_{1})^{-1}\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n}).

The map
\upeta_{M}
is well-defined and bounded in light of the symbol inversion result in Lemma
C.9
, while
p_{\mathrm{K}}
is well-defined and bounded by virtue of Lemma
C.8
. These maps allow us to define the bilinear form
B_{M,K}:{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to\mathbb{R}
via

(5.2.7)

B_{M,K}(u,w)=\langle\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(\mathrm{p}_{K}(u)-\mathfrak{g}\upeta_{M}(u),u,\upeta_{M}(u)),w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}.

Thanks to integration by parts and the definitions of
\mathrm{p}_{K}
and
\upeta_{M}
, we have the equivalent formulation

(5.2.8)

B_{M,K}(u,w)=\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u):\nabla w-\mathrm{p}_{K}(u)\nabla\cdot(\varrho w)\\
+\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\upeta_{M}(u),\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(w\cdot e_{n})\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}\\
=\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u):\nabla w+\frac{1}{N}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{K}\mathrm{p}_{K}(u)\mathrm{p}_{K}(w)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\partial_{j}^{m}\mathrm{p}_{K}(u)\partial_{j}^{m}\mathrm{p}_{K}(w)\\
+\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\upeta_{M}(u),-\partial_{1}\upeta_{M}(w)+N^{-1}(M^{-1}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4})\upeta_{M}(w)\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}.

The boundedness of
B_{M,K}
is straightforward to check, but it is also coercive since by anti-symmetry we have

(5.2.9)

\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho u\otimes e_{1}:\nabla u=0,

by orthogonality we have

(5.2.10)

\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u:\nabla u=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\upmu(\varrho)}{2}|\mathbb{D}^{0}u|^{2}+\uplambda(\varrho)(\nabla\cdot u)^{2},

by squaring we have

(5.2.11)

\frac{1}{N}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{K}\mathrm{p}_{K}(u)^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\partial_{j}^{m}\mathrm{p}_{K}(u))^{2}\geqslant 0,

and by anti-symmetry and symmetry considerations again we have

(5.2.12)

\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\upeta_{M}(u),-\gamma\partial_{1}\upeta_{M}(w)+N^{-1}(M^{-1}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4})\upeta_{M}(w)\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}\\
=\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\upeta_{M}(u),N^{-1}(M^{-1}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4})\upeta_{M}(u)\rangle\geqslant 0,

which together with the Korn inequalities of Propositions
A.4
and
A.3
imply the coercivity estimate

(5.2.13)

B_{M,K}(u,u)\geqslant\gamma_{0}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\upmu(\varrho)}{2}|\mathbb{D}^{0}u|^{2}+\uplambda(\varrho)(\nabla\cdot u)^{2}\gtrsim\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Recall that our conditions on
\upmu,\uplambda\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
are that
\upmu,\uplambda>0
if
n=2
, and
\upmu>0
,
\uplambda\geqslant 0
if
n\geqslant 3
.
The hypotheses of the Lax-Milgram lemma (see, for instance Theorem 6 in Chapter 6 of Lax
[
58
]
) are satisfied, so we are therefore granted
u_{M,K}\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
with the property that

(5.2.14)

B_{M,K}(u_{M,K},w)=\langle F,w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}\text{ for all }w\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}).

We then set
\eta_{M,K}=\upeta_{K}(u_{M,K})\in H^{2m}(\Sigma)
and
q_{M,K}=\mathrm{p}_{K}(u_{M,K})-\mathfrak{g}\upeta_{M}(u_{M,K})\in H^{2m}(\Omega)
. By construction, the collection
\{{(q_{M,K},u_{M,K},\eta_{M,K})}\}_{M,K\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
satisfies the claimed inclusions and satisfies (
5.2.3
), (
5.2.4
), and (
5.2.5
).

Step 3
: Estimates on the two parameter family of approximate solutions. We claim that the approximate solutions (
5.2.2
) obey the
K
-independent bounds

(5.2.15)

\lVert q_{M,K},u_{M,K},\eta_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times H^{1}\times H^{2m}}\lesssim_{m,M,N}\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

To prove the claim, we first take
w=u_{M,K}
in (
5.2.14
) and then use the coercive inequality (
5.2.13
) to get the control

(5.2.16)

\sup_{M,K\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\lVert u_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}},

where the implied constant only depends on the physical parameters. Next, we employ the continuity of the map
\upeta_{M}
to see that

(5.2.17)

\lVert\eta_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim_{M}N\lVert u_{M,N}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{M}N\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Obtaining a
K
-independent bound on the
q_{M,K}
is slightly more involved. We test the weak formulation (
5.2.14
) with
w=\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{B}(q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K})\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, where
\mathcal{B}
is the right inverse to the divergence constructed in Corollary
C.3
. Since
\nabla\cdot(\varrho w)=q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K}=\mathrm{p}_{K}(u_{M,K})
, the identity
B_{M,K}(u_{M,K},w)=\langle F,w\rangle
and the bounds (
5.2.16
) and (
5.2.17
) imply that

(5.2.18)

\lVert q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert u_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert\eta_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{3/2}}+\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim_{N,M}\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

To get a higher-regularity estimate, we rewrite the first equation in (
5.2.4
) as
(1+L_{m})(q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K})=\frac{K-1}{K}(q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K})-N\nabla\cdot(\varrho u_{M,K})
and then apply Lemma
C.8
with
\kappa=1
(and the already established
K
-independent bounds) to arrive at the estimate

(5.2.19)

\lVert q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim_{m}(1-K^{-1})\lVert q_{M,K}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M,K}\rVert_{L^{2}}+N\lVert u_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{m,M,N}\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Then (
5.2.17
) and (
5.2.19
) imply that
\lVert q_{M,K}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim_{m,M,N}\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}},
and we then combine this with (
5.2.16
) and (
5.2.17
) to complete the verification of (
5.2.15
), and hence the proof of the claim.

Step 4
: Existence of a one parameter family of approximate solutions. With the fixed data
F\in({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
as in the previous steps, we claim that there exists a sequence

(5.2.20)

\{{(q_{M},u_{M},\eta_{M})}\}_{M\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\subset H^{2m}(\Omega)\times{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{2m}(\Sigma)

such that

(5.2.21)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q_{M},u_{M},\eta_{M})=F,

(5.2.22)

\begin{cases}N^{-1}L_{m}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M})=-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u_{M})&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M})=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M})=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

and

(5.2.23)

\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u_{M}\cdot e_{n})+\partial_{1}\eta_{M}=N^{-1}(M^{-1}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4})\eta_{M}.

The existence of this sequence follows by taking a weak subsequential limit in the
K
parameter in our previously constructed collection. More precisely, for each fixed
M\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
we have established in the previous step that the
K
-independent bounds (
5.2.15
) hold. Thus, by weak compactness, there exist
(q_{M},u_{M},\eta_{M})\in H^{2m}(\Omega)\times{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{2m}(\Sigma)
that are a weak subsequential limit of the sequence
\{{(q_{M,K},u_{M,K},\eta_{M,K})}\}_{K\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
. Routine weak convergence arguments applied to the identities (
5.2.3
), (
5.2.4
), and (
5.2.5
) then show that (
5.2.21
), (
5.2.22
), and (
5.2.23
) hold. This completes the construction and the proof of the claim.

Step 5
: Estimates on the one parameter family of approximate solutions. We claim that the one parameter family of approximate solutions from (
5.2.20
) obeys the
M
-independent bounds

(5.2.24)

\lVert q_{M},u_{M},\eta_{M}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times H^{1}\times\mathcal{H}^{2m}}\lesssim_{m,N}\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

To see this, we first employ the weak sequential lower semicontinuity of the norm and (
5.2.16
) to obtain the estimate

(5.2.25)

\sup_{M\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Next, as in the third step of the proof of Proposition
5.2
, in the case that
\varsigma>0
, we test (
5.2.21
) against the function
w_{M}=-\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{B}_{2}(\varrho(b)\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{M})\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}),
where
\mathcal{B}_{2}
is defined in Corollary
C.4
. This yields the identity

(5.2.26)

\langle F,w_{M}\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u_{M}\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u_{M}):\nabla w_{M}+\langle(\mathfrak{g}\varrho-\varsigma\Delta_{\|})\eta_{M},-\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle^{-1}\Delta_{\|}\eta_{M}\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}},

which in turn yields the estimate

(5.2.27)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{M}\rVert_{H^{1/2}}^{2}\lesssim(\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}})\lVert w_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}\lVert w_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}.

The continuity of
\mathcal{B}_{2}
shows that
\lVert w_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{M}\rVert_{H^{1/2}}
, and hence

(5.2.28)

\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{M}\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

On the other hand, if
\varsigma=0
and
n=2
, estimate (
5.2.28
) follows by testing (
5.2.23
) with
\partial_{1}\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle\eta_{M}=\nabla_{\|}\langle\nabla_{\|}\rangle\eta_{M}\in H^{-1/2}(\Sigma)
and using orthogonality, Cauchy-Schwarz, boundedness of traces, and (
5.2.25
).

Now, as in the fourth step of Proposition
5.2
, we employ
w_{M}=\varrho^{-1}\mathcal{B}q\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, where
\mathcal{B}
is constructed in Corollary
C.3
, as a test function in (
5.2.21
) in order to see that

(5.2.29)

\langle F,w_{M}\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u_{M}\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u_{M}):\nabla w_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\varrho\nabla\eta_{M}\cdot w_{M}-\int_{\Omega}q_{M}^{2}\\
-\varsigma\langle\Delta_{\|}\eta_{M},\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(w_{M}\cdot e_{n})\rangle.

From this we readily deduce the bound
\lVert q_{M}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim(\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}+\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{M}\rVert_{H^{1/2}})\lVert w_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}
, but this combines with our already established bounds and the continuity estimate
\lVert w_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\lVert q_{M}\rVert_{L^{2}}
to show the low regularity bound
\lVert q_{M}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

We now have low regularity estimates on
q_{M}
and
\eta_{M}
. To promote these to high regularity bounds, we proceed as in the fifth step of the proof of Proposition
5.4
. The first identity in (
5.2.22
) is equivalent to
N^{-1}L_{m}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}})=-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u_{M})-\mathfrak{g}N^{-1}L_{m,\|}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{L}}
, where we have decomposed
\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{L}}=\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta_{M}
and
\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}=\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta_{M}
. Since
\partial_{n}^{m}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}})=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}})=0
, we can apply Lemma
5.3
with
\tau=0
and exploit the Fourier supports of
\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{L}}
and
\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}
to obtain the estimate

(5.2.30)

\lVert q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N(\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert L_{m,\|}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{L}}\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{L^{2}})\\
\lesssim N(\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}+\lVert\nabla_{\|}\eta_{M}\rVert_{H^{1/2}}+\lVert q_{M}\rVert_{L^{2}}).

Now we test the identity (
5.2.23
) against
N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m+1/4}\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}\in H^{-1/2}(\Sigma)
and employ the Fourier support of
\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}
as well as the bound (
5.2.24
) to arrive at the estimate

(5.2.31)

\lVert\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lesssim N\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Together, (
5.2.30
) and (
5.2.31
) imply that

(5.2.32)

\lVert q_{M},\eta_{M}^{\mathrm{H}}\rVert_{H^{2m}\times H^{2m}}\lesssim N\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

In light of Proposition
B.1
and equation (
B.1.25
), it remains only to obtain
M
-uniform bounds on
[{\partial_{1}\eta_{M}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}
. First, we integrate (
5.2.22
) over
(0,b)
in the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
-coordinate and recall (
5.2.23
) to acquire the equality

(5.2.33)

\varrho(b)\partial_{1}\eta_{M}=\frac{1}{N}L_{m,\|}\int_{0}^{b}(q_{M}+\mathfrak{g}\eta_{M})+\frac{\varrho(b)}{N}\Big(\frac{1}{M}+(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\Big)\eta_{M}+(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}\varrho u_{M}.

For
\kappa\in(0,1)
we write
\eta_{M}^{\kappa}=(\Uppi^{1/\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}-\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}})\eta_{M}\in H^{\infty}(\Sigma)
and then take the
L^{2}
inner product of (
5.2.33
) with
|\nabla_{\|}|^{-2}\partial_{1}\eta_{M}^{\kappa}\in H^{\infty}(\Sigma)
. The right hand side
\eta_{M}
terms all vanish due to the
\partial_{1}
operator, and we arrive at the equality

(5.2.34)

\varrho(b)[{\partial_{1}\eta_{M}^{\kappa}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}^{2}=\Big\langle\frac{|\nabla_{\|}|^{-1}}{N}L_{m,\|}\int_{0}^{b}q_{M}+|\nabla_{\|}|^{-1}(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\int_{0}^{b}\varrho u_{M},|\nabla_{\|}|^{-1}\partial_{1}\eta_{M}^{\kappa}\Big\rangle_{L^{2},L^{2}},

from which we readily deduce that

(5.2.35)

[{\partial_{1}\eta_{M}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}=\lim_{\kappa\to 0}[{\partial_{1}\eta_{M}^{\kappa}}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim_{m}N^{-1}\lVert q_{M}\rVert_{H^{2m-1}}+\lVert u_{M}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m}\lVert F\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}.

Then (
5.2.24
) follows by combining (
5.2.25
), (
5.2.32
), and (
5.2.35
) and recalling the equivalent norm on
\mathcal{H}^{2m}(\Sigma)
given in (
B.1.25
). The claim is proved.

Step 6
: Conclusion. The
M
-uniform bounds (
5.2.24
) guarantee the existence of a weak subsequential limit for the sequence (
5.2.20
), say
(q,u,\eta)\in H^{2m}(\Omega)\times{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathcal{H}^{2m}(\Sigma)
. Routine weak convergence arguments applied to the identities (
5.2.21
), (
5.2.22
), and (
5.2.23
) then reveal that
(q,u,\eta)
satisfy
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta)=F
,

(5.2.36)

\begin{cases}N^{-1}L_{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)=-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

and
\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n})+\partial_{1}\eta=N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\eta
. Therefore
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{0}_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(0,F)
, and so the proof is complete in light of the first step.
∎

As a consequence of the existence of weak solutions to the regularization, we now show that the operators associated to the strong formulation of the regularization, namely
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}
defined in (
3.5.10
), are automatically isomorphisms. The catch is that at this point we cannot guarantee the inverses come with estimates independent of
N
, so will will have to work harder in subsequent sections to verify this.

Corollary 5.6

(Isomorphisms induced by the regularization)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is from Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
m\geqslant 2
, and
\gamma_{0}\in I
for an interval
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Suppose that (
5.1.50
) holds. Then for
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, the map

(5.2.37)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}:\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{\upnu}\to\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}

defined by (
3.5.11
) is a Banach isomorphism.

Proof.

That the map (
5.2.37
) is well-defined is a consequence of the third item of Lemma
3.23
. We begin by proving injectivity. Suppose that
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathrm{ker}\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}\subseteq\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{\upnu}
. Lemma
3.24
shows that strong solutions are weak solutions, and hence
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{1}_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(0,0)
. We then deduce that
(q,u,\eta)=0
by invoking the a priori estimates of Proposition
5.4
.

We now turn to the proof of surjectivity. Suppose that

(5.2.38)

(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu},

and, by utilizing Theorem
5.5
and the map from (
3.5.23
), define
(q,u,\eta)
via

(5.2.39)

(q,u,\eta)=\big(\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{1}_{m,N}}\big)^{-1}(g,\mathscr{K}(f,k))\in\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}.

We claim that we have the higher-regularity inclusion
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
. Once this is shown, another application of Lemma
3.24
reveals that
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, which establishes surjectivity.

To prove the claim we will employ a finite induction argument to promote the regularity of the triple
(q,u,\eta)
one step at a time. To this end, it is useful to unpack the definition of weak solution in a more helpful way. Equation (
5.2.39
) is equivalent to:
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}_{-1}
,

(5.2.40)

\begin{cases}N^{-1}L_{m}q=g-\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))-\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)-\mathfrak{g}N^{-1}L_{m,\|}\eta&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}q=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}q=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

(5.2.41)

N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta=-N^{-1}(-\Delta_{\|})^{m-1/4}\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta+\partial_{1}\eta+\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(u\cdot e_{n}),\quad\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma_{0}}(u)=0,

and for all
w\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
it holds that

(5.2.42)

\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\gamma_{0}\varrho u\otimes e_{1}+\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u):\nabla w=\langle\widetilde{F},w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}},

where
\widetilde{F}=\mathscr{K}(\widetilde{f},\widetilde{k})
for
\widetilde{f}=f-\varrho\nabla(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)
and
\widetilde{k}=k+(\varrho q+\varsigma\Delta_{\|}\eta)e_{n}
.

The identity (
5.2.42
) means, in other words, that
u
is a weak solution to the elliptic boundary value problem

(5.2.43)

\begin{cases}-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}u-\gamma_{0}\nabla\cdot\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u=\widetilde{f}&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\gamma_{0}\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}ue_{n}=\widetilde{k}&\text{on }\Sigma,\\
u=0&\text{on }\Sigma_{0}.\end{cases}

From the inclusions (
5.2.38
) and
(q,\eta)\in H^{2m}(\Omega)\times\mathcal{H}^{2m}(\Sigma)
and the norm (
B.1.25
) we deduce that

(5.2.44)

\widetilde{f}\in H^{\min\{{\upnu,2m-1}\}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\text{ and }\widetilde{k}\in H^{\min\{{1/2+\upnu,2m-2}\}}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R}^{n}),

and so the standard elliptic regularity gain for the problem (
5.2.43
) (see, for instance, Agmon, Douglis, and Nirenberg
[
3
]
) guarantees the inclusion

(5.2.45)

u\in H^{\min\{{2+\upnu,2m-1/2}\}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\hookrightarrow H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}),

where the embedding holds since
m\geqslant 2
. With the improved
u
regularity from (
5.2.45
) in hand, we return to (
5.2.41
) to see that
\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta\in H^{1+2m}(\Sigma)
, and hence, by Proposition
B.1
,
\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{1+2m}(\Sigma)
. In turn, we use use the improved
u
and
\eta
regularity together with the fourth item of Lemma
3.19
in (
5.2.40
), appealing to Lemma
C.7
to deduce the improvement
q\in H^{1+2m}(\Omega)
.

Proceeding by finite induction, we assume now that for some
0\leqslant\nu\leqslant\upnu-1
we have the inclusion

(5.2.46)

(q,u,\eta)\in H^{\nu+1+2m}(\Omega)\times H^{\nu+2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathcal{H}^{\nu+1+2m}(\Sigma).

This implies that
\widetilde{f}\in H^{\min\{{\upnu,\nu+2m}\}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\widetilde{k}\in H^{\min\{{1/2+\upnu,\nu-1+2m}\}}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and so elliptic regularity for (
5.2.43
) implies that
u\in H^{\min\{{2+\upnu,\nu+1/2+2m}\}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\hookrightarrow H^{\nu+3}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
. We then argue exactly as above to use the improved
u
regularity to promote to
\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{\nu+2+2m}(\Sigma)
and
q\in H^{\nu+2+2m}(\Omega)
. Thus, (
5.2.46
) holds with
\nu
replaced by
\nu+1
. By finite induction, (
5.2.46
) then also holds for
\nu=\upnu
, and so
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
, which completes the proof of the claim.
∎

6.
Analysis of strong solutions to the linearization

Previously, we have established estimates for weak solutions to (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
), and for the latter we proved existence and qualitative regularity in Corollary
5.6
. The majority of this section is devoted to building a series of tools to aid in the estimation of the higher regularity norms of these weak solutions when given sufficiently regular data. In other words, we require a specific quantitative understanding of the regularity for systems (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
); for the former we will develop tame estimates of the solution norms with respect to the background
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
and
\gamma_{0}
, while for the latter we will prove high regularity bounds that are independent of the approximation parameter.

We proceed as follows. Section
6.1
analyzes the equations satisfied by the tangential derivatives of the solutions to systems (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
). Section
6.2
reduces the regularity promotion of solutions to estimates on tangential derivatives via analysis of the so-called normal system. Section
6.3
combines results from the tangential derivative and normal system analysis to derive the sought-after precise a priori estimates for the principal part equations. We then conclude in Section
6.4
by deducing the existence of strong solutions to (
5.0.1
) and then to the full linearization.

6.1.
Analysis of tangential derivatives

In order to study the tangential derivatives of solutions to (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
), we must first understand the commutators of the operators
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}
,
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}
,
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}
, and
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{1}_{m,N}}
with the tangential derivatives
\partial_{j}
for
j\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
. These operators are nearly tangentially translation invariant, and so nearly commute with the tangential derivatives; the failure of each to commute is precisely due to the appearance of
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))
in their continuity equations. The following definition and subsequent lemma capture this almost tangential translation invariance. We recall that the
\hat{H}^{s}(\Omega)
spaces are defined in (
C.1.1
).

Definition 6.1

(The principal part commutator)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
, and let
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
. For
j\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
we define the map
\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}:H^{1+s}(\Omega)\times\mathcal{H}^{1+s}(\Sigma)\to\hat{H}^{s}(\Omega)
via
\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta)=\nabla\cdot(\partial_{j}v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))
.

We now check that the
\mathscr{C}
maps are well-defined and then explore their mapping properties.

Lemma 6.2

(Mapping properties of
\mathscr{C}
)
.

Under the hypotheses of Definition
6.1
, we have the following estimates for
s\in\mathbb{N}
:

(6.1.1)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta)\big\rVert_{\hat{H}^{s}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,\eta\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times\mathcal{H}^{1+s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,\eta\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

Here the implicit constant depends only on
s
, the physical parameters, the dimension, and
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
.

Proof.

We first use Lemma
3.19
(in particular the splitting (
3.4.17
)) to compute

(6.1.2)

\partial_{j}v_{w_{0}}=\partial_{j}v_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}^{(1)}+\partial_{j}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}.

Then, given
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
, we use the first item of Lemma
3.19
to estimate

(6.1.3)

\lVert\partial_{j}v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\rVert_{H^{s}}\leqslant\lVert v_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{1+s}}\lesssim\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}.

For the other piece, we may use the second item of Lemma
3.19
to deduce that
v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}=(v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}\cdot e_{1})e_{1}
and that
v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}
has
r_{n-1}
as a band-limit. This, in addition to the second item of the aforementioned lemma, (
B.1.25
), and Proposition
B.1
, allow us to estimate

(6.1.4)

\lVert\partial_{j}v_{\eta_{0}}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\sup_{0\leqslant p\leqslant s}\sup_{y\in[0,b]}\lVert\partial_{j}\partial_{n}^{p}v_{\eta_{0}}^{(2)}(\cdot,y)\rVert_{H^{s-p}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}\leqslant\sup_{0\leqslant p\leqslant s}\sup_{y\in[0,b]}\lVert\partial_{n}^{p}v_{\eta_{0}}(\cdot,y)\cdot e_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim\lVert\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

Together, (
6.1.2
), (
6.1.3
), and (
6.1.4
) imply the bound
\lVert\partial_{j}v_{w_{0}}\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}
for
s\geqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
. With this established, proving the stated estimates is a simple application of Corollary
D.7
. Indeed, it shows that for any
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
(q,\eta)\in H^{1+s}(\Omega)\times\mathcal{H}^{1+s}(\Sigma)
, we have

(6.1.5)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta)\big\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)\rVert_{H^{1+s}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta,\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times H^{1+s}\times W^{1+s,\infty}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s<\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert\partial_{j}v_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}\rVert_{H^{1+s}}\lVert q,\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta,\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor\leqslant s,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\rho\lVert q,\eta\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times\mathcal{H}^{1+s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s<\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,\eta\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor\leqslant s.\end{cases}

Additionally, since
\mathrm{Tr}_{\partial\Omega}(v_{w_{0}}\cdot e_{n})=0
and
\partial_{j}v_{w_{0}}\in(L^{\infty}\cap L^{2})(\Omega)
, we have the estimate

(6.1.6)

\Big[{\int_{0}^{b}\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta)(\cdot,y)\;\mathrm{d}y}\Big]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}\eta,\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}\eta\rVert_{L^{2}\times L^{2}\times L^{\infty}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,\eta\rVert_{L^{2}\times\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

Then (
6.1.1
) follows by combining (
6.1.5
) and (
6.1.6
).
∎

We now apply the mapping properties of
\mathscr{C}
to obtain low regularity estimates for first order tangential derivatives.

Proposition 6.3

(Low norm estimates on tangential derivatives)
.

Under the hypotheses of Definition
6.1
, the following hold for
\gamma_{0}\in I
with
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval,
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{0}
, and
j\in\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
.

(1)

If
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{0}}
satisfies
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then
(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}
and obeys the estimate

(6.1.7)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{0}}.

(2)

If
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
m\geqslant 2
and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{0}_{m,N}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then
(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}
and obeys the estimate

(6.1.8)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{1}_{m,N}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{0}}.

Here the implicit constants depend on the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We will prove only the first item; the proof of the second follows from a nearly identical argument. We begin with three observations.

First, recall the bounded linear map
\mathscr{K}:L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{1/2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
defined by (
3.5.23
). For each
j\in\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
we construct a related bounded linear map
\mathscr{K}_{j}:L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{1/2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
as follows. For
(f,k)\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{1/2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R}^{n})
we initially define the linear map
\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k):{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to\mathbb{R}
via
\langle\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k),w\rangle=\langle\mathscr{K}(f,k),-\partial_{j}w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=-\int_{\Omega}f\cdot\partial_{j}w-\int_{\Sigma}k\cdot\partial_{j}w
. For such
f
,
k
, and
w
, we may bound

(6.1.9)

\char 12\relax{\langle\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k),w\rangle}\leqslant\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert\partial_{j}w\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert k\rVert_{H^{1/2}}\lVert\partial_{j}\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}w\rVert_{H^{-1/2}}\lesssim\lVert f,k\rVert_{L^{2}\times H^{1/2}}\lVert w\rVert_{H^{1}},

and from this and the density of
{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
in
{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
we deduce that
\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k)
uniquely extends to an element
\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k)\in({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
such that

(6.1.10)

\langle\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k),w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=\langle\mathscr{K}(f,k),-\partial_{j}w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}

for all
w\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\lVert\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k)\rVert_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim\lVert f,k\rVert_{L^{2}\times H^{1/2}}
. In particular, the latter estimate shows that the induced map
\mathscr{K}_{j}
is bounded and linear with the domain and codomain stated above.

Second, we recall the map
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}:\mathbb{X}_{-1}\to({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
defined by (
3.5.12
). Suppose that
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}_{0}
. For
j\in\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
we have that
(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\in\mathbb{X}_{-1}
, which means that
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)
defines an element of
({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}
. We may compute the action of this functional on any
w\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
by integrating by parts:

(6.1.11)

\langle\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta),-\partial_{j}w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=-\int_{\Omega}-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}u\cdot\partial_{j}w-q\nabla\cdot(\varrho\partial_{j}w)+\mathfrak{g}\varrho\nabla\eta\cdot\partial_{j}w+\gamma_{0}\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}u:\nabla\partial_{j}w\\
+\varsigma\langle\Delta_{\|}\eta,\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(\partial_{j}w\cdot e_{n})\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}=\int_{\Omega}-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}\partial_{j}u\cdot w-\partial_{j}q\nabla\cdot(\varrho w)+\mathfrak{g}\varrho\nabla\partial_{j}\eta\cdot w\\
+\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}\mathbb{S}^{\varrho}\partial_{j}u:\nabla w-\varsigma\langle\Delta_{\|}\partial_{j}\eta,\mathrm{Tr}_{\Sigma}(w\cdot e_{n})\rangle_{H^{-1/2},H^{1/2}}=\langle\mathscr{I}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta),w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}.

For the third observation, suppose that
q\in H^{1}(\Omega)
satisfies
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\in H^{1}(\Omega)
. Testing against members of
C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega)
and appealing to Lemma
6.2
, we see that for each
j\in\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
we have the distributional identity

(6.1.12)

\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\partial_{j}q)=\partial_{j}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)-\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0)\in L^{2}(\Omega).

In particular, this identity establishes that
\partial_{j}q\in H^{0}_{v_{w_{0}}}(\Omega)
, where this space is defined in Appendix
B.2
.

Having established these three observations, we are now ready to prove the first item. Suppose that
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{0}}
and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{0}
satisfy the strong from equation
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, which (due to the assumed level of regularity) is equivalent to the weak form equation
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,\mathscr{K}(f,k))
(see Lemma
3.24
). In turn, the weak formulation unpacks into the pair of equations

(6.1.13)

\nabla\cdot(\varrho u)+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))=g\text{ in }L^{2}(\Omega)\text{ and }\mathscr{I}(q,u,\eta)=\mathscr{K}(f,k)\text{ in }({{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}))^{\ast}.

Let
j\in\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
. For the first equation in (
6.1.13
) we apply
\partial_{j}
and use (
6.1.12
) from the third observation to see that

(6.1.14)

\nabla\cdot(\varrho\partial_{j}u)+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\partial_{j}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta))=\partial_{j}g-\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta).

For the second equation in (
6.1.13
) we let
w\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, test against
-\partial_{j}w
, and use (
6.1.10
) and (
6.1.11
) from the first and second observations to see that

(6.1.15)

\langle\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta),w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=\langle\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(q,u,\eta),-\partial_{j}w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}\\
=\langle\mathscr{K}(f,k),-\partial_{j}w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}=\langle\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k),w\rangle_{({{}_{0}}H^{1})^{\ast},{{}_{0}}H^{1}}.

Since
H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
is dense in
{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we deduce that

(6.1.16)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{I}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)=\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k).

By combining (
6.1.14
) and (
6.1.16
), we deduce that

(6.1.17)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)=(\partial_{j}g-\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta),\mathscr{K}_{j}(f,k)).

Therefore, (
6.1.7
) follows by taking the norm in
\mathbb{Y}^{-1}
of (
6.1.17
) and applying both Lemma
6.2
and the boundedness of
\mathscr{K}_{j}
established in the first observation.
∎

The next result is a higher regularity version of the previous one.

Proposition 6.4

(High norm estimates on tangential derivatives)
.

Under the hypotheses of Definition
6.1
, the following hold for
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
j\in\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
for
I
an interval, and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{s}
.

(1)

If
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s}}
satisfies
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then
(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s-1}}
and obeys the estimate

(6.1.18)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

(2)

If
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
m\geqslant 2
and
(q,u,\eta)\in{\mathbb{X}^{s}_{m,N}}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then
(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{s-1}_{m,N}
and obeys the estimate

(6.1.19)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

Here the implicit constants depend on
s
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We will only prove the first item, as the second follows from a nearly identical argument. Employing the identity
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\partial_{j}q)=\partial_{j}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)-\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0)
and Lemma
6.2
, it is evident that the differentiated triple
(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)
belongs to the space
\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s-1}}
. Applying
\partial_{j}
to the equations in
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
and rearranging provides the identity
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta)=\big(\partial_{j}g-\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,\eta),\partial_{j}f,\partial_{j}k\big)
. Then (
6.1.18
) follows by taking the
\mathbb{Y}^{s-1}
norm of both sides of this identity and applying the estimates from Lemma
6.2
.
∎

To conclude this subsection, we iterate Proposition
6.4
and combine with Proposition
6.3
to obtain low norm estimates on high-order tangential derivatives.

Theorem 6.5

(Synthesis of tangential derivative analysis)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is as in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
for an interval
I
,
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{s}
, and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\setminus\{{0}\}
be a multiindex such that
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0
and
|\alpha|\leqslant 1+s
. The following hold.

(1)

If
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s}}
satisfies
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then
(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}q)\in\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}
and we have the estimate

(6.1.20)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

(2)

If
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
m\geqslant 2
and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{s}_{m,N}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then
(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}
and we have the estimate

(6.1.21)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}^{1}_{m,N}}(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

The implicit constants depend on
s
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
I
.

Proof.

Again we only prove the first item, as the second follows from a nearly identical argument. First we claim that if
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s}}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
satisfies
1\leqslant|\alpha|\leqslant s
and
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0
, then
(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s-|\alpha|}}
and

(6.1.22)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s-|\alpha|}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

We establish this via strong induction on
|\alpha|
.

The base case of the claim,
|\alpha|=1
, was already established in Proposition
6.4
. Suppose now that for a fixed
1\leqslant\upnu\leqslant s-1
the claim holds for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
such that
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0
and
1\leqslant|\alpha|\leqslant\upnu
. Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
with
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0
be such that
|\alpha|=\upnu+1
. Let
\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\setminus\{{0}\}
be such that
\alpha=\beta+\gamma
and
|\gamma|=1
. By repeated applications of the induction hypothesis, it follows that
(\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s-|\beta|}}
and, in turn, that
(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{s-|\alpha|}}
. Moreover, the induction hypothesis also provides the estimates

(6.1.23)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s-|\alpha|}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-|\beta|}}+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s-|\beta|}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s-|\beta|\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s-|\beta|}}\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s-|\beta|,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\rho\big(\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}+\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-|\beta|}}\big)+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s,\end{cases}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s-|\beta|\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s-|\beta|}}\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s-|\beta|.\end{cases}

To morph this estimate into the correct form, we note the following two facts. First, the
\partial^{\beta}
-continuity estimates:
\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-|\beta|}}\lesssim\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}
and
\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{|\beta|+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}
. Second, by the log-convexity of the norm in the
\mathbb{X}
-spaces (see Lemma
3.25
) and Young’s inequality, we have that if
\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s-|\beta|
, then

(6.1.24)

\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s-|\beta|}}\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s-|\beta|}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{|\beta|+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\\
\lesssim\big(\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\big)^{\frac{|\beta|}{s-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\big(\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\big)^{1-\frac{|\beta|}{s-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\\
\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}+\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}.

Upon combining these facts with (
6.1.23
), we verify that (
6.1.22
) holds, which completes the proof of the inductive step and hence the claim.

Now, if
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
is such that
\alpha\cdot e_{n}
and
1\leqslant|\alpha|\leqslant s
, then the sought-after estimate (
6.1.20
) is true thanks to the estimate (
6.1.22
) established in the above claim and the trivial bounds

(6.1.25)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}\lesssim\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{0}}\leqslant\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s-|\alpha|}}.

It then only remains to prove (
6.1.20
) in the case that
|\alpha|=s+1
. In this case we may write
\partial^{\alpha}=\partial_{j}\partial^{\beta}
for some
j\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
and
|\beta|=s
. Applying Proposition
6.3
, followed by (
6.1.22
), we arrive at the estimate

(6.1.26)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{J}}(\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}+\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial^{\beta}q,\partial^{\beta}u,\partial^{\beta}\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{0}}\\
\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}+\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s,\end{cases}

which completes the proof in the case
|\alpha|=s+1
.
∎

6.2.
Analysis of normal systems

A useful technique in the study of the dynamic compressible Navier-Stokes system, originally developed by Matsumura and Nishida
[
75
]
, is to take linear combinations of a normal, or vertical, derivative of the continuity equation with certain components of the momentum equation in order to reveal a subtle dissipative structure for the normal derivative of the density. Our goal now is to implement a version of this technique for our traveling wave problem. The result will essentially be a bound on various high norms of a solution in terms of the data and norms of tangential derivatives alone.

We begin with a computation that motivates the definition of the normal system. Suppose that
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A^{1}}(q,u,\eta)=g
and
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{A^{2}}(q,u,\eta)=f
, where the operators
A^{i}
are as defined in (
3.4.8
) and (
3.4.9
), and consider the linear combination

(6.2.1)

\partial_{n}\overset{w_{0}}{A^{1}}(q,u,\eta)+\frac{\varrho}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}\overset{\gamma_{0}}{A^{2}}(q,u,\eta)\cdot e_{n}=\partial_{n}g+\frac{\varrho}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}f\cdot e_{n}.

On the left hand side the
\partial_{n}^{2}(u\cdot e_{n})
-terms cancel each other out. We may then rearrange the above equation to create a (differentiated) steady transport equation in
q
, i.e. (
6.2.1
) is equivalent to

(6.2.2)

\partial_{n}\Big(\frac{\varrho^{2}}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\Big)=\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f),

where

(6.2.3)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)=\Big(\frac{\varrho^{2}}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}\Big)^{\prime}q+\partial_{n}g\\
-\partial_{n}(\varrho^{\prime}u\cdot e_{n})-\varrho^{\prime}\partial_{n}u\cdot e_{n}-(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\partial_{n}(\varrho u)-\mathfrak{g}\partial_{n}\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta)\\
+\frac{\varrho}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}\big(f\cdot e_{n}+\gamma_{0}^{2}\partial_{1}u\cdot e_{n}+\gamma_{0}\upmu(\varrho)\Delta_{\|}u\cdot e_{n}\\
+\gamma_{0}(\upmu(\varrho)(1-2/n)+\uplambda(\varrho))(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\partial_{n}u+\gamma_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(\upmu^{\prime}(\varrho)\mathbb{D}^{0}ue_{n}\cdot e_{n}+\gamma_{0}\uplambda^{\prime}(\varrho)\nabla\cdot u)\big).

The key point is that (
6.2.3
) depends only on tangential derivatives of
q,u,\partial_{n}u,\eta
along with
\partial_{n}g
and
f
.

If we perform the same manipulations under the regularized hypotheses
N^{-1}L_{m}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)+\overset{w_{0}}{A^{1}}(q,u,\eta)=g
and
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{A^{2}}(q,u,\eta)=f
, where we recall that
L_{m}
is defined in (
3.5.6
), then we obtain the equation

(6.2.4)

\partial_{n}\Big(\frac{\varrho^{2}}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)+\frac{1}{N}L_{m}q\Big)=\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f).

The identities (
6.2.2
) and (
6.2.4
) are only half of the normal system in that they only allow us to gain control of the normal derivative of
q
in terms of lower order and tangential derivatives. Next, we see how to obtain similar control of the normal derivatives
u
. For this we only need to examine the equation
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{A^{2}}(q,u,\eta)=f
. If
j\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
, then we take the
j^{\mathrm{th}}
-component of this equation and isolate the
\partial_{n}^{2}(u\cdot e_{j})
term:
\partial_{n}^{2}(u\cdot e_{j})=\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{j}}(q,u,\eta,f)
, where

(6.2.5)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{j}}(q,u,\eta,f)=\frac{1}{\gamma_{0}\upmu(\varrho)}\big(-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}u\cdot e_{j}+\varrho\partial_{j}(q+\mathfrak{g}\eta)-\gamma_{0}\upmu(\varrho)\Delta_{\|}u\cdot e_{j}\\
-\gamma_{0}(\upmu(\varrho)(1-2/n)+\uplambda(\varrho))\partial_{j}\nabla\cdot u-\gamma_{0}\mathbb{D}^{0}ue_{n}\cdot e_{j}(\upmu(\varrho))^{\prime}-f\cdot e_{j}\big).

On the other hand, if we take the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
-component of
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{A^{2}}(q,u,\eta)=f
and isolate the
\partial_{n}^{2}(u\cdot e_{n})
contribution, then we get the equation
\partial_{n}^{2}(u\cdot e_{n})=\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{n}}(q,u,\eta,f)
, where

(6.2.6)

\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{n}}(q,u,\eta,f)=\frac{1}{\gamma_{0}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))}\big(-\gamma_{0}^{2}\varrho\partial_{1}u\cdot e_{n}+\varrho\partial_{n}q-\gamma_{0}\upmu(\varrho)\Delta_{\|}u\cdot e_{n}\\
-\gamma_{0}(\upmu(\varrho)(1-2/n)+\uplambda(\varrho))(\nabla_{\|},0)\cdot\partial_{n}u-\gamma_{0}\varrho^{\prime}(\upmu^{\prime}(\varrho)\mathbb{D}^{0}ue_{n}\cdot e_{n}+\uplambda^{\prime}(\varrho)\nabla\cdot u)-f\cdot e_{n}\big).

We record the output of these calculations in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.6

(Existence of the normal systems)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}+
an interval, and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{0}
. The following hold upon setting
\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)=\gamma_{0}^{-1}\varrho^{2}(2(1-1/n)\upmu(\varrho)+\uplambda(\varrho))^{-1}
.

(1)

If
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{0}}
satisfies
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then

(6.2.7)

\partial_{n}(\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q))=\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,f)

and

(6.2.8)

\partial_{n}^{2}u=(\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{1}},\dots,\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{n}})(q,u,\eta,f).

(2)

If
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, with
m\geqslant 2
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{0}_{m,N}
satisfy the equations
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then

(6.2.9)

\partial_{n}(\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)+N^{-1}L_{m}q)=\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,f),

and (
6.2.8
) holds.

We next study the mapping properties of the
\mathscr{N}
mappings, starting with
\mathscr{N}^{0}
.

Lemma 6.7

(Boundedness of
\mathscr{N}^{0}
)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval, and
s\in\mathbb{N}
. The linear map
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}:\mathbb{X}_{s}\times H^{1+s}(\Omega)\times H^{s}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to H^{s}(\Omega)
from (
6.2.3
) is well-defined, bounded, and obeys the following estimate.

(6.2.10)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)\big\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-1}}\\
+\lVert g,f\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times H^{s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s<\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor\leqslant s.\end{cases}

Here the implicit constant depends on the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We decompose

(6.2.11)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)=\overset{0,\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)-\mathfrak{g}\partial_{n}\nabla\cdot((v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}e_{1}/\mathfrak{g})\eta).

By inspection, it is clear that for any
s\geqslant 0
we have the estimate

(6.2.12)

\big\lVert\overset{0,\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)\big\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-1}}+\lVert g,f\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times H^{s}}.

For the remaining piece we appeal to the fourth item of Lemma
3.19
to estimate

(6.2.13)

\lVert\partial_{n}\nabla\cdot((v_{w_{0}}-\varrho^{\prime}e_{1}/\mathfrak{g})\eta)\rVert_{H^{s}}\leqslant\lVert\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\eta)\rVert_{H^{1+s}}+\lVert\partial_{1}\eta\rVert_{H^{s}}\\
\lesssim\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2+s}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }s<\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor\leqslant s.\end{cases}

We then conclude by noting that

(6.2.14)

\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5/2+s}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2+s}}.

∎

Next, we study the
\mathscr{N}^{j}
maps for
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
, as defined by (
6.2.5
) and (
6.2.6
).

Lemma 6.8

(Boundedness of
\mathscr{N}^{j}
, for
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
)
.

Suppose that
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval,
s\in\mathbb{N}
, and
i\in\{{1,\dots,n}\}
. Then the linear map
\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{i}}:\mathbb{X}_{s}\times H^{s}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\to H^{s}(\Omega)
from (
6.2.5
) when
j<n
or (
6.2.6
) when
j=n
is well-defined, bounded, and satisfies the following estimates. If
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
, then

(6.2.15)

\lVert\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{i}}(q,u,\eta,f)\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-1}}+\lVert f\rVert_{H^{s}},

and if
j=n
, then

(6.2.16)

\lVert\overset{\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{n}}(q,u,\eta,f)\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s-1}}+\lVert\partial_{n}q\rVert_{H^{s}}+\lVert f\rVert_{H^{s}}.

Proof.

These estimates are clear by inspection.
∎

The next result is an application of some of the analysis from Section
4
to the specific steady transport structure appearing here in the normal system.

Lemma 6.9

(Steady transport estimate)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval, and
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
\varphi,\psi\in H^{\upnu}(\Omega)
satisfy
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)\in H^{\upnu}(\Omega)
and

(6.2.17)

\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)\varphi+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)=\psi\quad\text{in }\Omega,

where
\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)
is defined as in Lemma
6.6
. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{ST},\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on the physical parameters,
\upnu
, and the dimension, and
I
, such that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{ST},\upnu}
then we have the estimate

(6.2.18)

\lVert\varphi,\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)\rVert_{H^{\upnu}\times H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

The implicit constant depends on
\upnu
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{ST},\upnu}
, and
I
.

Proof.

Most of the work was already carried out in Section
4
in the sense that we endeavor to apply Proposition
4.4
with the decomposed vector field
X=\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\varrho^{\prime}}v_{w_{0}}=X_{0}+X_{1}
, where
X_{0}=\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\varrho^{\prime}}v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}
and
X_{1}=e_{1}+\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\varrho^{\prime}}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}
,
which is split according to the decomposition of
v_{w_{0}}
from Lemma
3.19
. Write
\Uplambda=\mathfrak{g}\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)/\gamma\varrho^{\prime}
. By hypothesis,
\widetilde{\varphi}=\varrho^{\prime}\varphi/\mathfrak{g}\in H^{\upnu}(\Omega)
satisfies

(6.2.19)

\Uplambda\widetilde{\varphi}+\nabla\cdot(X\widetilde{\varphi})=\psi\text{ in }\Omega\text{ and }\nabla\cdot(X\widetilde{\varphi})=\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)\in H^{\upnu}(\Omega).

Thus, we may employ Proposition
4.4
to see that there exists
\rho^{(\upnu)}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
(depending only
\upnu
, the physical parameters, and
I
) such that if
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}\leqslant\rho^{(\upnu)}
, then we have the estimate

(6.2.20)

\lVert\widetilde{\varphi}\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{\upnu}\times W^{\upnu,\infty}}\lVert\widetilde{\varphi}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

Next we note that, with implicit constants depending only on the physical parameters and
s\in\{{\upnu,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\}
, we have the estimates

(6.2.21)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{s}}\asymp\lVert\widetilde{\varphi}\rVert_{H^{s}}\text{ and }\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{s}\times W^{s,\infty}}\lesssim\lVert v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}},v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times W^{1+s,\infty}}.

By invoking the first and second items of Lemma
3.19
, we also see that

(6.2.22)

\lVert v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}},v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times W^{1+s,\infty}}\lesssim\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}.

The claimed bound on
\varphi
in the
H^{\upnu}(\Omega)
norm now follows by combining these bounds and taking
\rho_{\mathrm{ST},\upnu}
small enough.

It remains to establish a bound on
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)
in the same space. The equation (
6.2.17
) is equivalent to
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)=\psi-\Uplambda(\rho)\varphi
, so by taking the norm in
H^{\upnu}(\Omega)
and utilizing the established bounds on
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}
, we complete the proof of (
6.2.18
).
∎

We are now ready to identify a recursive estimate for the norm of a solution to the principal part equations. The previous normal system identification and boundedness results merge in this next proposition and allow us to control the solution’s norm in terms of the data and a lower norm of the tangentially differentiated solution.

Proposition 6.10

(Synthesis of normal system results, 1)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is from Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})
be as in Lemma
3.19
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval, and
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}
and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

, depending only on the physical parameters, the dimension,
\upnu
, and
I
, such that that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}
then we have the estimate

(6.2.23)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

The implicit constant depends on
\upnu
,
\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}
, the dimension, the physical parameters, and
I
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{ST},1+\upnu}
, where this parameter is from Lemma
6.9
. We begin by proving the stated bounds on
q
. Denote
\psi=\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)
, where
\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)
is from Lemma
6.6
. According to the steady transport estimate, Lemma
6.9
, we have that

(6.2.24)

\lVert q,\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{1+\upnu}}\lesssim\begin{cases}\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}^{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

To go from this to the desired bounds on
q
we will carefully estimate
\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{H^{1+\nu}}
by splitting into three pieces:

(6.2.25)

\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\lVert\partial_{n}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}.

For the
L^{2}
-norm in (
6.2.25
) we bound via the definition of the norm on
\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}
:

(6.2.26)

\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}}.

Next, we consider the tangential (
1\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
) derivative terms in (
6.2.25
) by employing the commutator operator from Definition
6.1
to write

(6.2.27)

\partial_{j}\psi=\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)\partial_{j}q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\partial_{j}q)+\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0).

The first and second terms are readily dealt with using the definition of the space
\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}
:

(6.2.28)

\lVert\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)\partial_{j}q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\partial_{j}q)\rVert_{H^{\nu}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}}.

We next use Lemma
6.2
on the
\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0)
-term in (
6.2.27
) and then combine with estimate (
6.2.28
) to see that

(6.2.29)

\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}}+\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu,\end{cases}

for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
.

In order to handle the normal (
j=n
) derivative in (
6.2.25
), we use the existence of the normal system, Lemma
6.6
, which shows that
\partial_{n}\psi=\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)
. Then we use the boundedness of the linear map
\mathscr{N}^{0}
, Proposition
6.7
, to bound

(6.2.30)

\lVert\partial_{n}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu-1}}\\
+\lVert g,f\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

We have now handled all of the terms on the right side of (
6.2.25
); by combining (
6.2.24
), (
6.2.25
), (
6.2.26
), (
6.2.28
), (
6.2.29
), and (
6.2.30
), we deduce the estimate

(6.2.31)

\lVert q,\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{1+\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}\\
+\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}-1}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

Next, we turn our attention to the estimate of
u
in (
6.2.23
). Note that

(6.2.32)

\lVert u\rVert_{H^{2+\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}u\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\lVert\partial_{n}u\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}.

The
L^{2}
-norm and the sum of tangential (
1\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
) derivatives are trivially controlled by the right hand side of (
6.2.23
).
For the normal
(j=n)
derivative, we split as before:

(6.2.33)

\lVert\partial_{n}u\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}\leqslant\lVert\partial_{n}u\rVert_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}\partial_{n}u\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\lVert\partial_{n}^{2}u\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}.

The first two terms in (
6.2.33
) are again trivially bounded by the right hand side of (
6.2.23
). According to Lemma
6.6
,
\partial_{n}^{2}u
satisfies (
6.2.8
), so we may employ the boundedness of the linear maps
\mathscr{N}^{1},\dotsc,\mathscr{N}^{n}
, proved in Lemma
6.8
, to estimate

(6.2.34)

\lVert\partial_{n}^{2}u\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu-1}}+\lVert\partial_{n}q\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\lVert f\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}.

For the
\lVert\partial_{n}q\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}
term on the right we insert the already established bounds for
\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}
from (
6.2.31
). We then synthesize these bounds to deduce the sought-after estimate of
u
in (
6.2.23
). In remains only to handle
\eta
. However, the estimate for
\eta
in (
6.2.23
) follows directly from (
6.2.14
).
∎

Remark 6.11
.

We are free to choose the function
\mathbb{N}\ni\upnu\mapsto\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
to be nonincreasing without altering the statement or conclusion of Proposition
6.10
.

Next, we consider the normal system corresponding to the regularized principal part operator
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}
. We require the following preliminary result, which is analogous to Lemma
6.9
from the unregularized case.

Lemma 6.12

(Regularized steady transport estimate)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{X}^{\infty}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, and
\upnu\in\{{1,\dots,m}\}
. Suppose that
\varphi\in H^{\upnu+2m}(\Omega)
and
\psi\in H^{\upnu}(\Omega)
satisfy

(6.2.35)

\begin{cases}\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)\varphi+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi)+N^{-1}L_{m}\varphi=\psi,&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{n}^{m}\varphi=\cdots=\partial_{n}^{2m-1}\varphi=0&\text{in }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where
\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)
and
L_{m}
are defined in Lemma
6.6
and (
3.5.6
), respectively. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{RST},m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on the physical parameters, the dimension,
m
, and
I
, such that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{RST},m}
and
N\gtrsim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
, then we have the estimate

(6.2.36)

\lVert\varphi,\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\varphi),N^{-1}\varphi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}\times H^{\upnu}\times H^{\upnu+2m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2m}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}},

where the implied constants depend on
m
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{RST},m}
, and
I
.

Proof.

Once more, the goal is to invoke the work from Section
4
by applying Theorem
4.8
. To this end, we set
\Lambda_{0}=\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)
and
\Lambda_{1}=\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\varrho^{\prime}
,
and we use Lemma
3.19
to define the decomposed vector field
X=X_{0}+X_{1}
, where
X_{0}=\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\varrho^{\prime}}v^{(1)}_{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}
and
X_{1}=e_{1}+\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\varrho^{\prime}}v^{(2)}_{\eta_{0}}
. Thanks to the first and second items of the lemma, we have that

(6.2.37)

\lVert X_{0},X_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+s}\times W^{1+s,\infty}}\lesssim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\rangle\text{ for }\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor.

By the additional fact that
\nabla\Lambda_{1}=\gamma\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\varrho^{\prime\prime}e_{n}
, we see that

(6.2.38)

\max\{{\lVert DX_{0},DX_{1}\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times W^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}},\lVert X_{0}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}},\lVert X_{1}\cdot\nabla\Lambda_{1}\rVert_{W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}}}\}\\
\lesssim\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\leqslant\rho.

Thus, we may take
0<\rho_{\mathrm{RST},m}\lesssim\overline{\rho}^{(m)}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
4.8
, in order to reach the asserted conclusion.
∎

The next result is the analog of Proposition
6.10
for the
m,N
-regularized principal part operator. There are a few key differences, the most glaring of which is the finite range, depending on
m
, in which the estimate holds. Another more minor distinction is that while the following estimates are no longer tame (due to the appearance of the
\langle\cdot\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
term), they still place all of the high norms of the background onto the low norms of the solution.

Proposition 6.13

(Synthesis of normal system results, 2)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{X}^{\infty}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval,
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
m\geqslant 2
, and
\upnu\in\{{0,\dots,m-1}\}
. Suppose that
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on the physical parameters, the dimension,
\upnu
,
m
, and
I
such that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}
and

(6.2.39)

N\gtrsim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}}\rangle^{4+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor},

then we have the estimate

(6.2.40)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\\
+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

The implicit constants depend on the dimension, the physical parameters,
m
,
\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}
, and
I
.

Proof.

The proof largely mirrors that of Proposition
6.10
, but since we have to appeal to estimates for solutions to the regularized steady transport equation, the estimates we get are somewhat different in a few key places. As such, we work through most of the argument in detail, appealing to the proof of Proposition
6.10
when possible.

Suppose first that
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{RST},m}
. We begin with the bounds on
q
by setting
\psi=\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)+N^{-1}L_{m}q
. According to the regularized steady transport estimate, Lemma
6.12
, we have the bound

(6.2.41)

\lVert q,\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q),N^{-1}q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{1+\upnu+2m}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2m}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}},

and to parlay this into our desired estimate we split

(6.2.42)

\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}\leqslant\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}+\lVert\partial_{n}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}.

For the
L^{2}
-norm we bound as in (
6.2.26
):

(6.2.43)

\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{-1}}}.

We study the tangential
(1\leqslant j\leqslant n-1)
derivatives in (
6.2.42
) via the identity

(6.2.44)

\partial_{j}\psi=\Lambda_{\gamma_{0}}(\varrho)\partial_{j}q+\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}\partial_{j}q)+N^{-1}L_{m}\partial_{j}q+\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0),

where
\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}
is as in Definition
6.1
, which shows that

(6.2.45)

\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0)\big\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}.

By the boundedness of
\mathscr{C}^{j}
established in Lemma
6.2
, we obtain the estimate

(6.2.46)

\lVert\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0)\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

In the case that
\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu
, we use interpolation (see Lemma
3.25
) and Young’s inequality to further estimate
