\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\upnu}}\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}
. Hence, for any
\upnu
we have the bound

(6.2.47)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0}}{\mathscr{C}^{j}}(q,0)\big\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\upnu}}\lVert q\rVert_{L^{2}}

Upon combining (
6.2.45
) and (
6.2.47
) and then summing over
1\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
, we acquire the tangential bound

(6.2.48)

\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}q,\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}+\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}.

It remains to estimate the
\lVert\partial_{n}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}
-term in (
6.2.42
). For this, we recall the regularized normal system, Lemma
6.6
, which says that
\partial_{n}\psi=\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{\mathscr{N}^{0}}(q,u,\eta,g,f)
. Therefore, we may apply the continuity properties of
\mathscr{N}^{0}
, Lemma
6.7
, to see that

(6.2.49)

\lVert\partial_{n}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu-1}}+\lVert g,f\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{\upnu}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

Again, in the latter case of
\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu
we use interpolation and Young’s inequality to bound

(6.2.50)

\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1/2}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2+\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1/2+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3/2}},

and hence in all cases

(6.2.51)

\lVert\partial_{n}\psi\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu-1}}+\lVert g,f\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1/2+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}.

Upon piecing together (
6.2.42
), (
6.2.43
), (
6.2.48
), and (
6.2.51
), we arrive at the bound

(6.2.52)

\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}+\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

Now we insert (
6.2.43
) and (
6.2.52
) into (
6.2.41
) and choose
\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{RST},m}
and sufficiently small to allow absorption of the
\lVert q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}
term on the right by the left hand side. This yields

(6.2.53)

\lVert q,\nabla\cdot({v_{w_{0}}}q),N^{-1}q\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{1+\upnu}\times H^{1+\upnu+2m}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}},

and this implies the asserted bound on
q
in (
6.2.40
).

Finally, the proof of the estimates for
u
and
\eta
is mostly a reprise of the latter part of the proof of Proposition
6.10
. We get equations (
6.2.32
), (
6.2.33
), and (
6.2.34
) exactly as before. Then we insert our new bound for
q
from (
6.2.53
) into the
\lVert\partial_{n}q\rVert_{H^{\upnu}}
-term. This yields the
u
bound of (
6.2.40
). The
\eta
bound is again trivial.
∎

Remark 6.14
.

For fixed
m
, we are free to choose the function
\{{0,\dots,m-1}\}\ni\upnu\mapsto\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal,\upnu,m}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
to be nonincreasing without altering the statement or conclusion of Proposition
6.13
.

The final result of this subsection iterates the conclusions of Propositions
6.10
and
6.13
to alter the form of the estimate’s right hand side to one in which higher order tangential derivatives appear in a low norm.

Theorem 6.15

(Synthesis of normal system results, 3)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
r\in\{{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\}
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{r}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{X}^{\infty}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval,
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
, and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}
. The following hold.

(1)

If
r=1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
,
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then we have the estimate

(6.2.54)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant 1+\upnu\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

The implied constant depends on
\upnu
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}
, and
I
.

(2)

If
r=2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
,
\mathbb{N}\ni m\geqslant\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies (
6.2.39
),
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
satisfy
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, then there exists
\chi_{\upnu,m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
\upnu
,
m
, and the dimension, and
C_{1/\rho}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
1/\rho
,
\upnu
,
m
, such that we have the estimate

(6.2.55)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant 1+\upnu\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}_{m,N}^{-1}}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+C_{1/\rho}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi_{\upnu,m}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

Here the implied constant depends on
m
, the dimension, the various physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We begin by proving the first item via an induction argument. The proposition to be proved inductively is as follows: if
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
satisfies
s\leqslant\upnu+1
for some
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}
, then we have the estimate

(6.2.56)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant s\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-s}}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

The base case,
s=1
, was established in Proposition
6.10
. Suppose now that
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 2
is such that the proposition holds at the level
s-1
. We will now verify it at level
s
. Suppose that
s\leqslant\upnu+1
for some
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}
. We first invoke Proposition
6.10
to obtain the estimate

(6.2.57)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

Fix
\sigma\in\{{0,1}\}
and
j\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
. To handle the
\lVert\partial^{\sigma}_{j}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial^{\sigma}_{j}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}}
term appearing in (
6.2.57
), we invoke the induction hypothesis at level
s-1
(while heeding to Remark
6.11
) in order to learn that

(6.2.58)

\lVert\partial^{\sigma}_{j}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial^{\sigma}_{j}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu-1}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant s-1\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\sigma}_{j}u,\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-s}}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu-1}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

We estimate the first term on the right hand side trivially via
\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu-1}}\lesssim\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}
. For the term involving the operator
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}
, we invoke the first item of Proposition
6.4
(noting that
\upnu>0
) to bound

(6.2.59)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s.\end{cases}

Upon combining inequalities (
6.2.57
), (
6.2.58
), and (
6.2.59
) with the fact that

(6.2.60)

\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant 1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<s,\end{cases}

we establish (
6.2.56
) for the level
s
, and thus prove the proposition at the
s
level. The proposition then holds for all
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
by induction. By taking
s=1+\upnu
in (
6.2.56
) we obtain (
6.2.54
), which completes the proof of the first item.

Next, we turn our attention to the second item. The strategy here is the same as in the first item, but due to the dissimilarities between (
6.2.54
) and (
6.2.55
), we cannot apply precisely the same argument. Our new proposition to be proved inductively is as follows: if
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, then for all
\mathbb{N}\ni\upnu\geqslant s-1
and
\mathbb{N}\ni m\geqslant\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\}
, there exists
\chi\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending on
s
,
m
, and
\upnu
, and
C_{1/\rho}
, depending on
s
,
1/\rho
,
\upnu
, and
m
, such that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying (
6.2.39
),
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
we have the estimate

(6.2.61)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant s\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-s}_{m,N}}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+C_{1/\rho}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

As before, the base case,
s=1
, was established already, this time in Proposition
6.13
.

Suppose now that
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 2
is such that the induction proposition holds at level
s-1
. We will now verify it at level
s
. Suppose that
\mathbb{N}\ni\upnu\geqslant s-1
,
\mathbb{N}\ni m\geqslant\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies (
6.2.39
),
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}
, and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
. As before, we first invoke Proposition
6.13
and obtain the estimate

(6.2.62)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\sum_{\sigma=0}^{1}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\\
+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

For
\sigma\in\{{0,1}\}
and
j\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
, we invoke the
(s-1)
-induction hypotheses while again heeding Remark
6.14
to bound

(6.2.63)

\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant s-1\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-s}_{m,N}}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu-1}}+C_{1/\rho}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

Now we make three estimates. First, we again have the trivial estimate

(6.2.64)

\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu-1}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}.

Second, by invoking the second item of Proposition
6.4
, the log-convexity of the
\mathbb{X}
-norms from Lemma
3.25
, and Young’s inequality, we get the bounds

(6.2.65)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu-1}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\rho^{-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{-1}}.

Third, by the log-convexity of the
\mathbb{X}
-norms again and Young’s inequality, we get the bound

(6.2.66)

C_{1/\rho}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi}\lVert\partial_{j}^{\sigma}q,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}u,\partial_{j}^{\sigma}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\\
+\rho^{-1/\upnu}C_{1/\rho}^{(1+\upnu)/\upnu}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+m+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\}}}\rangle^{\chi(1+\upnu)/\upnu}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}.

Now we combine estimates (
6.2.62
) and (
6.2.63
) with the trio (
6.2.64
), (
6.2.65
), and (
6.2.66
); this shows that the induction proposition holds at the level
s
, and hence for all
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
by induction. Estimate (
6.2.55
) from the second item now follows by taking
s=1+\upnu
in (
6.2.61
).
∎

6.3.
Estimates and existence for the principal part

In this subsection we first prove a priori estimates for systems (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
). After this, we derive the existence theory for the former.

Theorem 6.16

(A priori estimates for the principal part and the regularization)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
,
r\in\{{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\}
,
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{r}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{X}^{\infty}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval,
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
, and
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}
. The following hold.

(1)

Let
r=1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
\upnu
, the dimension, the various physical parameters, and
I
, such that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu}
, then for
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}
satisfying
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
we have the a priori estimate

(6.3.1)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

The implicit constants depend on
\upnu
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu}
, and
I
.

(2)

Let
r=2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
and
\mathbb{N}\ni m\geqslant\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\}
. There exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{reg},\upnu,m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
\upnu
,
m
, the various physical parameters, and
I
such that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg},\upnu,m}
, then for
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}
satisfying
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{m,N}}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
we have the a priori estimate

(6.3.2)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi_{\upnu,m}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}},

where
\chi_{\upnu,m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
is from the second item of Theorem
6.15
. Here the implicit constant depends on
\upnu
,
m
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\mathrm{reg},\upnu,m}
, and
I
.

Proof.

We begin by proving the first item. Assume that
\rho\leqslant\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{weak}},\rho_{\mathrm{normal},\upnu}}\}
, where these smallness parameters are from Propositions
5.2
and
6.10
, respectively. By combining the a priori estimates for weak solutions from Proposition
5.2
with the tangential derivative estimates from the first item of Theorem
6.5
, we learn that

(6.3.3)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant 1+\upnu\\
\alpha\cdot e_{n}=0\end{subarray}}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}q,\partial^{\alpha}u,\partial^{\alpha}\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\\
+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

We insert the bound (
6.3.3
) into conclusion (
6.2.54
) of the first item of Theorem
6.15
to acquire the estimate

(6.3.4)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

Hence, we may define
\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
to be sufficiently small so that when
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu}
, we may absorb the right hand side’s
\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}
-contribution by the left and obtain (
6.3.1
). This completes the proof of the first item.

Next we consider the second item. The argument is basically the same, but with an extra step. Assume
0<\rho\leqslant\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{weak,reg}},\rho_{\mathrm{reg,normal},\upnu,m}}\}
, where these smallness parameters are from Propositions
5.4
and
6.13
, respectively. As in the proof of the first item, we combine the conclusions of Proposition
5.4
, the second item of Theorem
6.5
, and the second item of Theorem
6.15
; however, the resulting estimate is slightly different:

(6.3.5)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\\
+C_{1/\rho}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi_{\upnu,m}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}}\\
+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu.\end{cases}

To reach the estimate (
6.3.2
), we note that the log-convexity of the
\mathbb{X}
-norms from Lemma
3.25
, paired with Young’s inequality grants the bound

(6.3.6)

\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\upnu\leqslant\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }\lfloor n/2\rfloor<\upnu,\end{cases}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}}}\\
+\rho^{-1-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{-1}_{m,N}}},

and thus we may combine estimates (
6.3.5
) and (
6.3.6
), and then take
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg},\upnu,m}
sufficiently small to obtain (
6.3.2
).
∎

We now are in a position to give an existence result for the principal part of the linearization, system (
5.0.1
).

Theorem 6.17

(Existence for the principal part)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, where the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
, and let
w_{0}=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0})\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{X}^{\infty}
,
\gamma_{0}\in I
for
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
an interval. For each
\upnu\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a
\rho_{\mathrm{exi},\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending on
\upnu
, the physical parameters, and
I
, such that if
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{exi},\upnu}
then the map

(6.3.7)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}:\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\to\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu},

the action of which is given via (
3.4.7
), is a Banach isomorphism.

Proof.

Take
\rho_{\mathrm{exi},\upnu}=\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu},\rho_{\mathrm{reg},\upnu,\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\}}}\}
, where these smallness parameters are from the first and second items of Theorem
6.16
. That the map (
6.3.7
) is well-defined is a consequence of the first item of Proposition
3.21
. This map is injective as a consequence of a priori estimate (
6.3.1
) from the first item of Theorem
6.16
. It remains only to verify surjectivity.

The proof of the second item of Theorem
6.16
shows that
\rho_{\mathrm{exi},\upnu}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{reg,weak}}
, and hence we are in a position to apply Corollary
5.6
, which tells us that the maps
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\},N}}:\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\},N}\to\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}
are Banach isomorphisms for
N\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, say
N\geqslant\overline{N}
. Thus, given
(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}
we can define the sequence
\{{(q_{N},u_{N},\eta_{N})}\}_{N=\overline{N}}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}
via
(q_{N},u_{N},\eta_{N})=\big(\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A_{\max\{{2,1+\upnu}\},N}}\big)^{-1}(g,f,k)
. In this we are tacitly using that
\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}_{m,N}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}
for any
m,N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
; in fact, this embedding is non-expansive. Therefore, by applying the a priori estimate (
6.3.2
) from the second item of Theorem
6.16
, followed by the regularized weak solution a priori estimate from Proposition
5.4
(and by also invoking Lemma
3.24
), we obtain the uniform bounds

(6.3.8)

\sup_{N\geqslant\overline{N}}\big(\lVert q_{N},u_{N},\eta_{N}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\lVert\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q_{N})\rVert_{H^{1+\upnu}}\big)\lesssim\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\max\{{2m,1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+m}\}}}\rangle^{\chi_{\upnu,m}}\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}.

Therefore, we may extract a weak limit
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}
with
\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\in H^{1+\upnu}(\Omega)
such that along some unlabeled subsequence we have that

(6.3.9)

(q_{N},u_{N},\eta_{N})\rightharpoonup(q,u,\eta)\text{ in }\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}\text{ and }\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q_{N})\rightharpoonup\nabla\cdot(v_{w_{0}}q)\text{ in }H^{1+\upnu}(\Omega).

By routine weak convergence arguments, we readily deduce that
(q,u,\eta)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}
and
\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)
, which completes the proof of surjectivity.
∎

6.4.
Synthesis of linear analysis

In this final subsection of linear analysis, we turn to the study of the full derivative of the nonlinear map
\overline{\Psi}
from (
3.3.1
), which is associated to the PDE (
1.4.9
). In other words, we consider the question of existence and tame estimates for the system

(6.4.1)

D\overline{\Psi}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=(g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma).

As usual, the unknowns are
q:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
,
u:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\eta:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
, while the given data are
g:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
,
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
k:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\mathcal{T}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, as well as the background tuple
(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})
. We now state our main linear analysis result.

Theorem 6.18

(Analysis of the linearization)
.

Let
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
(recall that the latter is defined in Theorem
3.17
),
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
be an interval, and

(6.4.2)

(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})=(q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0},\mathcal{T}_{0},\mathcal{G}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\gamma_{0})\in(B_{\mathbb{X}^{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}(0,\rho)\times B_{\mathbb{W}_{4+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}(0,\rho)\times I)\cap(\mathbb{X}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{W}_{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}).

For every
\mathbb{N}\ni\upnu\geqslant 3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
there exists a
\rho_{\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending on
\upnu
, the physical parameters, and
I
, such that if
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\upnu}
, then the following hold.

(1)

The map

(6.4.3)

D\overline{\Psi}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0}):\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}

is well-defined and a Banach isomorphism.

(2)

Assume that
\Xi=(g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\Theta=(D\overline{\Psi}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0}))^{-1}\Xi\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}
. We have the tame estimate

(6.4.4)

\lVert\Theta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}}\lesssim\lVert\Xi\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}}+\langle\lVert\theta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}^{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\Xi\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}},

where the implicit constant depends on
\upnu
, the dimension, the physical parameters,
\rho_{\upnu}
, and
I
.

Proof.

Suppose initially that
\mathbb{N}\ni\upnu\geqslant 2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
. The idea of the proof is to prove a priori estimates, uniform with respect to a parameter, and utilize the method of continuity. To this end, for
\tau\in[0,1]
we define the convex homotopy of operators

(6.4.5)

L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0}):\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}

via

(6.4.6)

L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=\begin{pmatrix}\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)+\tau\big(\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{P}+\overset{\theta_{0}}{Q}\big)(q,u,\eta,\gamma)+\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{R}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\\
\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma\end{pmatrix}.

Note that
L^{1}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})=D\overline{\Psi}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})
from (
3.4.4
) and that the mapping properties of
A
,
P
,
Q
, and
R
established in Propositions
3.21
and
3.22
ensure well-definedness and continuity of the maps
L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})
.

We now aim to prove
\tau
-uniform a priori estimates for
\{{L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})}\}_{\tau\in[0,1]}
. Assume that

(6.4.7)

(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}\text{ and }(g,f,k)\in\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}

are related via

(6.4.8)

L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=(g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma).

The first three components of the above equation are equivalent to

(6.4.9)

\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}(q,u,\eta)=(g,f,k)-\tau\big(\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{P}+\overset{\theta_{0}}{Q}\big)(q,u,\eta,\gamma)-\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{R}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})=(\widetilde{g},\widetilde{f},\widetilde{k}).

Assume that
0<\rho\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu}
; then we may invoke the principal part estimates from the first item of Theorem
6.16
to see that

(6.4.10)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{P}(q,u,\eta,\gamma)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\big\lVert\overset{\theta_{0}}{Q}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\\
+\big\lVert\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{R}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}.

According to the second item of Proposition
3.21
and the first and second items of Proposition
3.22
, we have the bounds

(6.4.11)

\big\lVert\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{P}(q,u,\eta,\gamma)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta,\gamma\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}},

(6.4.12)

\big\lVert\overset{\theta_{0}}{Q}(q,u,\eta)\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\lVert\theta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}},

and

(6.4.13)

\big\lVert\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{R}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}}\lesssim\lVert\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}+\langle\lVert q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}\rangle\lVert\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}.

Inserting (
6.4.11
), (
6.4.12
), and (
6.4.13
) into the right hand side of (
6.4.10
), we obtain the estimate

(6.4.14)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{\upnu}}+\lVert g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}\\
+\langle\lVert\theta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}}.

Now we take
0<\rho\leqslant\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{est},\upnu},\rho_{\mathrm{exi},\upnu}}\}
, where the latter smallness parameters are from Theorems
6.16
and
6.17
, to be sufficiently small so that we can absorb as usual in (
6.4.14
) to see that

(6.4.15)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}}\lesssim\lVert g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}+\langle\lVert\theta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}}.

We need to handle the norm of
(q,u,\eta)
in
\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
on the right hand side of (
6.4.15
). According to the first item of Theorem
6.16
and (
6.4.2
), we know that

(6.4.16)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\leqslant\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}}\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{g},\widetilde{f},\widetilde{k}\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}},

where the latter is defined in (
6.4.9
). By arguing as in (
6.4.11
), (
6.4.12
), and (
6.4.13
) again but using (
6.4.2
), we learn that

(6.4.17)

\lVert\widetilde{g},\widetilde{f},\widetilde{k}\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\rho\lVert q,u,\eta,\gamma\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}}+\lVert g,f,k\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}+\lVert\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}.

By combining (
6.4.16
) and (
6.4.17
) and taking
\rho
smaller, if necessary, we gain the estimate

(6.4.18)

\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\lesssim\lVert g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}}.

Finally, we insert (
6.4.18
) into (
6.4.15
) to acquire the estimate

(6.4.19)

\lVert q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma\rVert_{\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{\mathbb{X}^{\upnu}}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}}\lesssim\lVert L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{R}}\\
+\langle\lVert\theta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+\upnu}\times\mathbb{W}_{1+\upnu}}\rangle\lVert L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\times\mathbb{R}}.

The bound (
6.4.19
) establishes the desired
\tau
-uniform a prior bounds for
\{{L^{\tau}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})}\}_{\tau\in[0,1]}
, as long as
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\upnu}
for some
\rho_{\upnu}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Therefore, by the method of continuity (see, for instance, Theorem 5.2 in Gilbarg and Trudinger
[
35
]
), the invertibility of
L^{1}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})=D\overline{\Psi}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})
is established as soon as we know that
L^{0}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0})
is invertible. The latter holds thanks to Theorem
6.17
(and the fact that
\rho_{\upnu}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{exi},\upnu}
) since

(6.4.20)

(L^{0}(\theta_{0},\gamma_{0}))^{-1}(g,f,k,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=\big(\big(\overset{w_{0},\gamma_{0}}{A}\big)^{-1}\big((g,f,k)-\overset{q_{0},u_{0},\eta_{0}}{R}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\big),\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma\big).

This completes the proof of the first item. The second item follows from the first and estimate (
6.4.19
) at
\tau=1
.
∎

7.
Conclusion

In this section we prove our main result, Theorem
1
. We begin with Theorem
7.1
, which is an abstract construction in the Nash-Moser inverse function theorem framework from Section
2
. We then employ the abstract construction for the PDE-style result in Theorem
7.3
.

7.1.
Abstract construction

Recall the scales of Banach spaces from (
3.1
), the nonlinear operator
\overline{\Psi}
from (
3.3.1
), and the parameter
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
from Theorem
3.17
. To state the next theorem we introduce the following notation. First, we set
\beta=9+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor
. Second, for
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta
and
\rho\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
we define the open sets

(7.1.1)

E_{s}(\rho)=B_{\mathbb{E}^{\beta}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{E}^{s}\text{ and }F_{s}(\rho)=B_{\mathbb{F}^{\beta}}(0,\rho)\cap\mathbb{F}^{s},

where
\mathbb{E}^{s}
and
\mathbb{F}^{s}
are defined by (
3.1.10
). Finally, it is convenient to introduce a translation of
\overline{\Psi}
; namely, for
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
we set

(7.1.2)

\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=\overline{\Psi}(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\Gamma+\gamma)-(0,0,0,0,0,0,\Gamma),

defined for
(q,u,\eta)\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{\beta}}(0,\rho_{\mathrm{WD}})
,
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\in\mathbb{W}_{1+\beta}
, and
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\gamma>-\Gamma
. The utility of this definition is that
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}(0)=0
.

Theorem 7.1

(Traveling waves for free boundary compressible Navier-Stokes, 1)
.

Assume that the parameters
\upmu
,
\uplambda
, and
\varsigma
satisfy (
1.1.7
) and that
P
satisfies
P^{\prime}>0
and (
1.1.9
). Let
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and let
\beta,

E_{s}(\rho)
,
F_{s}(\rho)
, and
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
be as defined above. There exists a nonincreasing sequence
\{{\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma)}\}_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\subset(0,\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\Gamma/2}\})
and
\kappa(\Gamma)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that the following hold.

(1)

Existence and uniqueness: Given
\mathbf{f}\in F_{\beta}(\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma))
, there exists a unique
\mathbf{e}\in E_{\beta}(\kappa(\Gamma)\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma))
such that
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{e})=\mathbf{f}
. This induces the local inverse map

(7.1.3)

\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}:F_{\beta}(\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma))\to\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(F_{\beta}(\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma)))\subseteq E_{\beta}(\kappa(\Gamma)\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma)).

(2)

Higher regularity, given low norm smallness: If
\nu\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{f}\in F_{\beta+\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma))
, then
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\in\mathbb{E}^{\beta+\nu}
, and we have the tame estimate

(7.1.4)

\lVert\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\rVert_{\mathbb{E}^{\beta+\nu}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}^{\beta+\nu}},

for an implicit constant depending only on the dimension, the physical parameters,
\nu
,
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
, and
\Gamma
.

(3)

Continuous dependence: For every
\nu\in\mathbb{N}
, the restricted map

(7.1.5)

\overline{\Psi}^{-1}_{\Gamma}:F_{\beta+\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma))\to\mathbb{E}^{\beta+\nu}

is continuous with respect to the norms on
\mathbb{F}^{\beta+\nu}
and
\mathbb{E}^{\beta+\nu}
.

(4)

Continuous differentiability: For every
\nu\in\mathbb{N}
, the restricted map

(7.1.6)

\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}:F_{\beta+\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu+2}(\Gamma))\to\mathbb{E}^{\beta+\nu-1}

is differentiable. Moreover, if we view
D\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}
as a map

(7.1.7)

D\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}:F_{\beta+\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu+2}(\Gamma))\times\mathbb{F}^{\beta+\nu-1}\to\mathbb{E}^{\beta+\nu-1},

then
D\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}
is continuous.

Proof.

Our aim is to show that the hypotheses of Theorem
2.21
are satisfied by
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
with the Banach scales
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{E}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{F}}
defined in (
3.1
). Thanks to Lemma
3.2
, we have that condition
II
from the hypotheses of Theorem
2.21
is satisfied by
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{E}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{F}}
. In verifying the rest of the hypotheses we consider the triple
(\boldsymbol{\mathbb{E}},\boldsymbol{\mathbb{F}},\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma})
with parameters
(\mu,r,R)=(1,3+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,17+3\lfloor n/2\rfloor)
, which obey the requisite inequality
2(r+\mu)+1=\beta<(r+R)/2
with
\beta=9+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor
.

We next set
\delta_{r}=\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\Gamma/2}\}
. Then Theorem
3.17
and Remark
3.18
prove that the second item (
C^{2}
and
\mu
-tameness) of Definition
2.20
is satisfied. Invoking Theorem
6.18
with
\upnu=R
and interval
I=[\Gamma/2,2\Gamma]
then shows that the third item (derivative inversion) of Definition
2.20
holds with
\delta_{R}=\min\{{\rho_{\upnu},\delta_{r}}\}
(where
\rho_{\upnu}
is given by Theorem
6.18
) and that the remainder of the LRI mapping hypotheses from the definition are also satisfied.

Hence, the hypotheses of Theorem
2.21
are satisfied. We thus obtain an
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma)
such that the first item holds, and we have the estimate

(7.1.8)

\lVert\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\rVert_{\mathbb{E}^{\beta}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}^{\beta}}

for all
\mathbf{f}\in F_{\beta}(\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma))
. In fact, by the second item of Theorem
2.21
, we have that for all
s\in[\beta,R+r-\beta]\cap\mathbb{N}=[9+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,11+3\lfloor n/2\rfloor]\cap\mathbb{N}
it holds that if
\mathbf{f}\in F_{\beta}(\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma))\cap\mathbb{F}^{s}
, then
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\in\mathbb{E}^{s}
and estimate (
7.1.8
) holds with
\beta
replaced by
s
. Therefore, we may set
\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma)=\varepsilon_{0}
for
\nu\in\{{0,1,\dots,2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor=R+r-2\beta}\}
.

Now, given
\mathbb{N}\ni\nu>2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor
, we define
\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma)\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
to be the smallness parameter
\varepsilon
provided by Theorem
2.21
with parameter triple
(\mu,r,\widetilde{R})
, where
\widetilde{R}=R+\nu-(2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor)
. Note that the LRI mapping hypotheses are satisfied in this case if we take
\delta_{\widetilde{R}}=\min\{{\rho_{R+\nu-(2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor)},\delta_{r}}\}
, where the former parameter is from Theorem
6.18
(with
\upnu=R+\nu-(2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor)
and
I=[\Gamma/2,2\Gamma]
). The second item now follows from this definition of
\{{\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma)}\}_{\nu=0}^{\infty}
, the second item of Theorem
2.21
, and uniqueness. Finally, the third and fourth items now follow directly from Theorem
2.24
.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

As a consequence of the third conclusion of Theorem
2.24
, the local inverse map
\overline{\Psi}_{\Gamma}^{-1}
produced in Theorem
7.1
satisfies certain higher-order differentiability assertions beyond the basic continuous differentiability result in the third item of the previous theorem. However, the precise statements become cumbersome to enumerate due to a compounding of the derivative loss in the formulas for higher-order derivatives of the inverse map. As such, we have chosen not to state these precisely.

7.2.
PDE construction

We utilize Theorem
7.1
to prove our main result about the free boundary compressible Navier-Stokes equations, system (
1.4.9
).

Theorem 7.3

(Traveling wave for free boundary compressible Navier-Stokes, 2)
.

Assume that the parameters
\upmu
,
\uplambda
, and
\varsigma
satisfy (
1.1.7
) and that
P
satisfies
P^{\prime}>0
and (
1.1.9
).
Let
\beta=9+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor
. There exist a family
\{{\mathcal{V}(\gamma)}\}_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}
of open sets of
\mathbb{X}^{\beta}
and a nonincreasing sequence
\{{\mathcal{U}_{s}}\}_{s=\beta}^{\infty}
of open sets of
\mathbb{W}_{1+\beta}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that the following hold.

(1)

Nondegeneracy: We have that
\{{0}\}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\subseteq\bigcap_{s=\beta}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}_{s}
and
0\in\bigcap_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\mathcal{V}(\gamma)
.

(2)

Existence and uniqueness: For all
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathcal{U}_{\beta}
there exists a unique
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma)
such that the traveling wave formulation for the free boundary compressible Navier-Stokes equations, system (
1.4.9
), is classically satisfied with wave speed
\gamma
, data
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})
, and solution
(q,u,\eta)
.

(3)

Higher regularity, given low norm smallness: If
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta
and
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R})
, then the corresponding solution satisfies
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma)\cap\mathbb{X}^{1+s}
.

(4)

Continuous dependence: For any
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta
, the solution map

(7.2.1)

\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R})\ni(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\mapsto(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{1+s}

is continuous with respect to the
\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{X}^{1+s}
norms.

(5)

No vacuum formation: There exists positive constants
c,C\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for all
(q,u,\eta)\in\bigcup_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\mathcal{V}(\gamma)
we have that
c\leqslant\sigma_{q,\eta}\leqslant C
, where
\sigma_{q,\eta}
is defined in (
1.4.11
).

(6)

Flattening map diffeomorphism: For any
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta
and
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{s}\cap\bigcup_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\mathcal{V}(\gamma)
we have that the flattening map
\mathfrak{F}_{\eta}
from (
1.4.4
) is a smooth diffeomorphism from
\Omega
to
\Omega[\eta]
that extends to a
C^{s+2-\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
diffeomorphism from
\overline{\Omega}
to
\overline{\Omega[\eta]}
.

Proof.

For each
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we many invoke Theorem
7.1
and acquire a nonincreasing sequence
\{{\varepsilon_{\nu}(\Gamma)}\}_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\subset(0,\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\Gamma/2}\})
and a
\kappa(\Gamma)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that the various conclusions of the theorem hold. We then define the open sets

(7.2.2)

\mathcal{U}_{s}=\bigcup_{\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}B_{\mathbb{W}_{1+\beta}\times\mathbb{R}}((0,\Gamma),\varepsilon_{s-\beta+1}(\Gamma)/C)\text{ for }\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta

for some constant
C\geqslant 1
(independent of
s
) to be determined, and

(7.2.3)

\mathcal{V}(\Gamma)=B_{\mathbb{X}^{\beta}}(0,\kappa(\Gamma)\varepsilon_{0}(\Gamma))\text{ for }\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

The first item is now clear by inspection.

For the second item, we apply the first conclusion of Theorem
7.1
. Indeed, for
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathcal{U}_{\beta}
we have existence by setting
(q,u,\eta)
to be the first three components of the tuple
\overline{\Psi}_{\gamma}^{-1}(0,0,0,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},0)
. Uniqueness in
\mathcal{V}(\gamma)
follows from the fact that if
\overline{\Psi}(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=\overline{\Psi}(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{u},\widetilde{\eta},\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)
, then
\overline{\Psi}_{\gamma}(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},0)=\overline{\Psi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{u},\widetilde{\eta},\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},0)
and hence
(q,u,\eta)=(\widetilde{q},\widetilde{u},\widetilde{\eta})
by the uniqueness assertions of Theorem
7.1
.

We now prove the third item. An immediate consequence of the second conclusion of Theorem
7.1
is that if
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta
and
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R})
, then the corresponding solution satisfies
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma)\cap\mathbb{X}^{s}
. In fact, the solution is one degree more regular. To see this, we recall the map
\Phi
from (
3.3.5
) and note that the equation
\overline{\Psi}(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)=(0,0,0,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)
is equivalent to
\overline{\Psi}(q,u,\eta,0,0,0,\gamma)=(-\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}),0,0,0,\gamma)
, which in turn is equivalent to

(7.2.4)

\overline{\Psi}_{\gamma}(q,u,\eta,0,0,0,0)=(-\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}),0,0,0,0).

Note that
\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})
is linear in
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})
; consequently, Propositions
3.14
and
3.15
(with
m=0
) show that

(7.2.5)

\lVert\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{\beta}}\leqslant c\lVert\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}_{\beta}}\text{ and }\lVert\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}^{1+s}}\lesssim_{\lVert q,u,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}^{s}}}\lVert\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}_{1+s}}

for a constant
c
depending only on the dimension, the physical parameters, and
\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}
. Assume the constant
C\geqslant 1
from (
7.2.2
) satisfies
C\gtrsim c
. We then use the inclusion
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R})
and (
7.2.5
) to see that

(7.2.6)

(-\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}),0,0,0,\gamma)\in\mathbb{F}^{1+s}\cap\bigcup_{\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}B_{\mathbb{F}^{\beta}\times\mathbb{R}}((0,\Gamma),\varepsilon_{s-\beta+1}(\Gamma)).

Hence, we can apply the first and second conclusions of Theorem
7.1
to deduce from (
7.2.4
) and (
7.2.6
) that the third item holds.

It remains to justify the fourth item. Identity (
7.1.2
) and the third conclusion of Theorem
7.1
show that for
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\beta
, the solution map

(7.2.7)

\mathcal{U}_{s-1}\cap(\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R})\ni(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\mapsto(q,u,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}^{s}

is continuous. In fact, we can do one derivative better for the target space topology by arguing as in the proof of the third item above. Indeed, by Propositions
3.14
and
3.15
again, along with (
7.2.7
), we find that the map

(7.2.8)

\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{W}_{1+s}\times\mathbb{R})\ni(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\mapsto(-\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}),0,0,0,\gamma)\in\mathbb{F}^{1+s}

is continuous. In light of the identity

(7.2.9)

\overline{\Psi}(q,u,\eta,0,0,0,\gamma)=(-\Phi(q,u,\eta,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}),0,0,0,\gamma),

the third conclusion of Theorem
7.1
(which implies that
\overline{\Psi}^{-1}
is continuous), the inclusion (
7.2.6
) (which implies that the right side of (
7.2.9
) is in the domain of
\overline{\Psi}^{-1}
), the continuity of the map (
7.2.8
), and the continuity of compositions, we then complete the verification of the fourth item.

The fifth and sixth items are immediate consequences of the first conclusions of Proposition
3.10
and Theorem
3.17
. This completes the proof.
∎

We conclude this section by recording some simple consequences of Theorem
7.3
.

Corollary 7.4

(Open set of data for fixed wave speed)
.

For each
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
there exists a nonempty open set
(0,0,0)\in\mathcal{W}(\gamma)\subset\mathbb{W}_{1+\beta}
with the property that for all stress-force data tuples
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\in\mathcal{W}(\gamma)
there exists a unique
(q,u,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma)
such that system (
1.4.9
) is satisfied with solution
(q,u,\eta)
, wave speed
\gamma
, and data
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

Given
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
we take
\mathcal{W}(\gamma)=\{{(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\;:\;(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F},\gamma)\in\mathcal{U}_{\beta}}\}
.
∎

Corollary 7.5

(On the Eulerian formulation)
.

Each solution to the flattened, perturbative enthalpy formulation of the traveling wave problem for free boundary compressible Navier-Stokes, i.e system (
1.4.9
), produced by Theorem
7.3
, gives rise to a classical solution to the traveling Eulerian formulation of the problem given by system (
1.2.1
).

Proof.

The sixth item of Theorem
7.3
verifies that the flattening map
\mathfrak{F}_{\eta}:\Omega\to\Omega[\eta]
from (
1.4.4
) is a smooth diffeomorphism that is sufficiently smooth up to the boundary as to preserve the notion of classical solutions upon undoing the flattening. This same theorem also gives us classical solutions to (
1.4.9
) as an easy consequence of various supercritical Sobolev embeddings. By combining these facts and undoing the nonlinear changes of unknowns that took us from (
1.2.1
) to (
1.4.9
), we obtain the stated result.
∎

Appendix A
Standard Sobolev space tools

A.1.
Extension operators

Recall the Poisson extension operator
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and the variant
\mathcal{E}
introduced in (
1.4.2
) and (
1.4.3
), respectively. The following lemma records some simple mapping properties.

Lemma A.1

(Mapping properties of the Poisson extension operator variants)
.

The following hold.

(1)

\mathcal{E}_{0}:H^{s+1/2}(\Sigma)\to{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega)\cap H^{1+s}(\Omega)
is a bounded linear map for each for
s\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

\mathcal{E}\circ\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}=\mathcal{E}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{E}\circ\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}:\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Sigma)\to W^{\infty,\infty}((0,b);\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(1)}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}))
is a continuous linear map, where
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(1)}
is defined by (
B.1.3
).

Proof.

The first item is clear by standard elliptic theory for the Dirichlet problem. The second item is immediate.
∎

Throughout the paper is is also frequently useful to consider extension operators mapping functions defined on domains to functions defined on the entire Euclidean space that are regularity preserving for the
entire
Sobolev scale. For these we use the celebrated extension operators of Stein; the following definition sets our notation for these.

Definition A.2

(Stein-Extension operators and domains)
.

The notion of a Stein extension operator is given in Section 3.1 in Chapter VI of Stein
[
96
]
. A Stein extension domain is an open set
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
for which there exists a Stein extension operator, which we will denote by
\mathfrak{E}_{U}
. We will also employ this notation more generally for open subsets of finite dimensional real vector spaces of dimension
d
via the standard identification with
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

A.2.
Korn’s inequalities

Our first result here, which is classical, involves the (not normalized) symmetric gradient, which we recall is defined for differentiable vector fields
f
by
\mathbb{D}f=\nabla f+\nabla f^{\mathrm{t}}
.

Proposition A.3

(Korn’s inequality for the symmetric gradient,
n\geqslant 2
)
.

Let
n\geqslant 2
be the ambient dimension. Then there exists a constant
c\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only
\Omega
and
n
, such that for all
f\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
we have the inequality
\lVert f\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant c\left\lVert\mathbb{D}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}}
.

Proof.

The proof can be found in Lemma 2.7 in Beale
[
8
]
, and is based on Theorem 12.II of Fichera
[
34
]
, which gives a Korn inequality in cubes of general dimension. We note that the result in
[
8
]
is only stated for
n=3
, but the same argument works in general for
n\geqslant 2
.
∎

We are also interested in inequalities involving the trace-free part of the symmetric gradient, also known as the deviatoric gradient. We denote this for differentiable vector fields
f
by
\mathbb{D}^{0}f=\nabla f+\nabla f^{\mathrm{t}}-\frac{2}{n}(\nabla\cdot f)I
. In dimensions
n\geqslant 3
, the analog of Proposition
A.3
for
\mathbb{D}^{0}
holds.

Proposition A.4

(Deviatoric Korn’s inequality,
n\geqslant 3
)
.

Let
n\geqslant 3
be the ambient dimension. There exists a constant
c\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
\Omega
and
n
, such that for all
f\in{{}_{0}}H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
we have the inequality
\lVert f\rVert_{H^{1}}\leqslant c\lVert\mathbb{D}^{0}f\rVert_{L^{2}}
.

Proof.

See, for instance, Theorem 1.1 in Dain
[
22
]
for a proof of the bound

(A.2.1)

\lVert f\rVert_{H^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\lVert\mathbb{D}^{0}f\rVert_{L^{2}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})}+\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n})}\text{ for all }f\in H^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n})

whenever
Q
is a cube. By a standard compactness argument, we can then show that
\lVert f\rVert_{H^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\lVert\mathbb{D}^{0}f\rVert_{L^{2}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})}
for all
f\in H^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{n})
vanishing on one side of the cube, where the implicit constant depends on
Q
. We then utilize this inequality and the symmetries of
\Omega
, as was done in Proposition
A.3
, to obtain the desired result.
∎

Remark A.5

(Failure of deviatoric Korn in dimension
n=2
)
.

For dimension
n=2
, however, the analog of Proposition
A.4
is false. See, for instance, Section 6.6 of Bauer, Neff, Pauly, and Starke
[
6
]
, where it is proved that there exists a Lipschitz domain
\varnothing\neq U\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and a proper segment
\varnothing\neq S\subset\partial U
with the property that
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{H^{1}}\not\lesssim\lVert\mathbb{D}^{0}(\cdot)\rVert_{L^{2}}
on the subspace of
H^{1}(U;\mathbb{R}^{2})
consisting of vector fields vanishing on
S
.

A.3.
Refined interpolation of Sobolev spaces

In this subsection we derive refined interpolation inequalities for linear mappings between Sobolev spaces; in particular, this discussion considers improvements for the
K
-method of real interpolation. For more information on the basic
K
-method of interpolation, we refer to Chapter 3 in Bergh and Löfström
[
9
]
. First, we recall the definition of the
K
-functional. Given a pair of Banach spaces
X_{0}
and
X_{1}
that embed into a Hausdorff topological vector space
Z
, i.e.
X_{0},X_{1}\hookrightarrow Z
, we define the map
K(\cdot,\cdot;X_{0},X_{1}):\mathbb{R}^{+}\times(X_{0}+X_{1})\to\mathbb{R}
, via

(A.3.1)

K(t,x;X_{0},X_{1})=\inf\{{\lVert x_{0}\rVert_{X_{0}}+t\lVert x_{1}\rVert_{X_{1}}\;:\;x=x_{0}+x_{1},\;(x_{0},x_{1})\in X_{0}\times X_{1}}\}

for
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times(X_{0}+X_{1})
. Our first lemma realizes the
K
-functional on Sobolev spaces.

Lemma A.6

(Sobolev space
K
-functional)
.

Let
s_{0},s_{1},s\in\mathbb{R}
, be such that
s>0
and
s_{0}+s=s_{1}
. For
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
define the bounded linear map
P_{t}^{s}:H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\to H^{s_{0}+2s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
via
P_{t}^{s}=(1+t^{2}\langle\nabla\rangle^{2s})^{-1}
. For all
f\in H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
we have that

(A.3.2)

2^{-1}K(t,f;H^{s_{0}};H^{s_{1}})^{2}\leqslant\lVert(I-P^{s}_{t})f\rVert_{H^{s_{0}}}^{2}+t^{2}\lVert P^{s}_{t}f\rVert_{H^{s_{1}}}^{2}\leqslant K(t,f;H^{s_{0}};H^{s_{1}})^{2}.

Proof.

Define
\hat{K}(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})=\inf_{g\in H^{s_{1}}}\big(\lVert f-g\rVert^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}+t^{2}\lVert g\rVert^{2}_{H^{s_{1}}}\big)
and note that elementary estimates show that
2^{-1}K(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})^{2}\leqslant\hat{K}(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})\leqslant K(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})^{2}
. The benefit of switching to
\hat{K}
is that we may readily employ the direct method in the calculus of variations to see that the infimum in the definition of
\hat{K}(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})
is actually a minimum, achieved by
f_{\star}\in H^{s_{1}}
satisfying (by virtue of the Euler-Lagrange equations)

(A.3.3)

-\langle\langle\nabla\rangle^{s_{0}}(f-f_{\star}),\langle\nabla\rangle^{s_{0}}g\rangle_{L^{2}}+t^{2}\langle\langle\nabla\rangle^{s_{0}+s}f_{\star},\langle\nabla\rangle^{s_{0}+s}g\rangle_{L^{2}}=0\text{ for every }g\in H^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

This is equivalent to saying that
f_{\star}
is a weak solution to the elliptic pseudo-differential equation
(I+t^{2}\langle\nabla\rangle^{2s})\langle\nabla\rangle^{s_{0}}f_{\ast}=\langle\nabla\rangle^{s_{0}}f
, or alternatively,
f_{\star}=(I+t^{2}\langle\nabla\rangle^{2s})^{-1}f=P_{t}^{s}f
. Thus,
\hat{K}(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})=\lVert(I-P^{s}_{t})f\rVert_{H^{s_{0}}}^{2}+t^{2}\lVert P^{s}_{t}f\rVert_{H^{s_{1}}}^{2}
.
∎

Our next lemma expresses the norm in Sobolev spaces in terms of the
K
-functional. The point of the following computation is to divide the
K
-functional norm by a suitable constant to remove the degeneracies near the end-points.

Lemma A.7

(A norm computation)
.

Let
s_{0},s_{1},s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be such that
s>0
and
s_{0}+s=s_{1}
, and let
\sigma\in(0,1)
. Define
\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{1-2\sigma}}{1+\tau^{2}}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. We have the equivalence of norms

(A.3.4)

\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}}\leqslant\Big(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-2\sigma-1}K(t,f;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{1/2}\leqslant\sqrt{2}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}}.

Proof.

Given
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we may equate

(A.3.5)

\lVert(I-P^{s}_{t})f\rVert^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}+t^{2}\lVert P^{s}_{t}f\rVert^{2}_{H^{s_{1}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s_{0}}\Big(\frac{t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}{1+t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}\Big)^{2}|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2(s_{0}+s)}\Big(\frac{t}{1+t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}\Big)^{2}|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s_{0}}\frac{t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}{1+t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi.

Hence, by Tonelli’s theorem and a change of variables, we have that

(A.3.6)

\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-2\sigma-1}(\lVert(I-P^{s}_{t})f\rVert^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}+t^{2}\lVert P_{t}^{s}f\rVert_{H^{s_{1}}}^{2})\;\mathrm{d}t\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}t^{-2\sigma-1}\frac{t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}{1+t^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2s}}\;\mathrm{d}t\;|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi\\
=\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2(s_{0}+\sigma s)}|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi=\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}}^{2}.

The result then follows by combining this with Lemma
A.6
.
∎

We now come to the main result of this subsection of the appendix.

Proposition A.8

(Refined interpolation of Sobolev spaces)
.

Let
s_{0},s_{1},s,r_{0},r_{1},r\in\mathbb{R}
with
s,r>0
,
s_{0}+s=s_{1}
, and
r_{0}+r=r_{1}
. Assume that

(A.3.7)

T\in\mathcal{L}(H^{r_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap\mathcal{L}(H^{r_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))

is such that for some constants
C_{0},C_{1},A\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
we have the bounds

(A.3.8)

\lVert Tf\rVert_{s_{0}}\leqslant C_{0}\lVert f\rVert_{r_{0}}\text{ and }\lVert Tf\rVert_{s_{1}}\leqslant C_{1}\lVert f\rVert_{r_{1}}+A\lVert f\rVert_{r_{0}},

for all appropriate
f
. Set
r_{-1}=r_{0}-r
. Then for all
\sigma\in[0,1]
we have the inclusion

(A.3.9)

T\in\mathcal{L}(H^{(1-\sigma)r_{0}+\sigma r_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))

and the bound

(A.3.10)

\frac{\lVert Tf\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}}}{4C_{0}^{1-\sigma}C_{1}^{\sigma}}\leqslant\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)r_{0}+\sigma r_{1}}}+\sigma^{1/2}\frac{A}{C_{1}}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)r_{-1}+\sigma r_{0}}}

for all appropriate
f
.

Proof.

Given
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
f\in H^{r_{0}}
, we decompose
f=(I-P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t})f+P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t}f
, with
(I-P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t})f\in H^{r_{0}}
and
P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t}f\in H^{r_{1}}
. By the definition of
K
and the boundedness hypotheses, we see may then estimate

(A.3.11)

K(t,Tf;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})\leqslant\lVert T(I-P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t})f\rVert_{H^{s_{0}}}+t\lVert TP^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t}f\rVert_{H^{s_{1}}}\\
\leqslant C_{0}\big(\lVert(I-P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t})f\rVert_{H^{r_{0}}}+C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t\lVert P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t}f\rVert_{H^{r_{1}}}\big)+At\lVert P_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t}^{r}f\rVert_{H^{r_{0}}}.

We apply Lemma
A.6
to see that

(A.3.12)

K(t,Tf;H^{s_{0}},H^{s_{1}})\leqslant\sqrt{2}C_{0}K(C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t,f;H^{r_{0}},H^{r_{1}})+At\lVert P^{r}_{C_{0}^{-1}C_{1}t}f\rVert_{H^{r_{0}}}.

Upon squaring, multiplying by
t^{-2\sigma-1}
, integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and employing Lemma
A.7
, we acquire the bound

(A.3.13)

4^{-1}\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)\lVert Tf\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}}^{2}\leqslant\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)C_{0}^{2(1-\sigma)}C_{1}^{2\sigma}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)r_{0}+\sigma r_{1}}}^{2}\\
+A^{2}(C_{0}^{-1}C_{1})^{2(\sigma-1)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\tau^{-2\sigma+1}\lVert P^{r}_{\tau}f\rVert^{2}_{H^{r_{0}}}\;\mathrm{d}\tau.

The final integral above can then be computed explicitly by using Tonelli’s theorem:

(A.3.14)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\tau^{-2\sigma+1}\lVert P^{r}_{\tau}f\rVert^{2}_{H^{r_{0}}}\;\mathrm{d}\tau=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2r_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{-2\sigma+1}}{(1+\tau^{2}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2r})^{2}}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\;|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi\\
=\mathfrak{d}(\sigma)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle 2\pi\xi\rangle^{2(r_{0}+(\sigma-1)r)}|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi=\mathfrak{d}(\sigma)\lVert x\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)r_{-1}+\sigma r_{0}}}^{2},

where we define
\mathfrak{d}(\sigma)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{-2\sigma+1}}{(1+\tau^{2})^{2}}\;\mathrm{d}\tau
. Together, (
A.3.13
) and (
A.3.14
) imply that

(A.3.15)

\frac{\lVert Tf\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)s_{0}+\sigma s_{1}}}}{2C_{0}^{1-\sigma}C_{1}^{\sigma}}\leqslant\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)r_{0}+\sigma r_{1}}}+\frac{A}{C_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{d}(\sigma)}{\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)}}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{(1-\sigma)r_{-1}+\sigma r_{0}}}.

The proof is complete upon noting the bounds
\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)\geqslant\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\tau^{1-2\sigma}}{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{1-2\sigma}}{2\tau^{2}}\;\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{1}{4}(\frac{1}{1-\sigma}+\frac{1}{\sigma})
and
\mathfrak{d}(\sigma)\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\tau^{-2\sigma+1}\;\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{1}^{\infty}\tau^{-2\sigma-3}\;\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{1-\sigma}+\frac{1}{1+\sigma})
,
which imply that
\frac{\mathfrak{d}(\sigma)}{\mathfrak{c}(\sigma)}\leqslant\frac{4\sigma}{1+\sigma}\leqslant 4\sigma
.
∎

Appendix B
Some nonstandard function spaces

B.1.
The anisotropic Sobolev spaces

For
\mathbb{R}\ni s\geqslant 0
and
d\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
we define the anisotropic Sobolev space

(B.1.1)

\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\{{f\in\mathscr{S}^{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})\;:\;\mathscr{F}[f]\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{C}),\;\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}<\infty}\},

equipped with the norm

(B.1.2)

\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(|\xi|^{-2}(\xi_{1}^{2}+|\xi|^{4})\mathds{1}_{B(0,1)}(\xi)+\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B(0,1)}(\xi)\big)|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi\Big)^{1/2}.

These spaces were introduced in Leoni and Tice
[
60
]
, where it was shown, in Proposition 5.2 and Theorem 5.6, that
\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a Hilbert space and
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with equality in the first embedding if and only if
d=1
. The ‘anisotropic’ descriptor is justified by the low frequency multiplier in (
B.1.1
) as well as Theorem 5.2 in
[
60
]
, which shows that
\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is not closed under composition with rotations when
d\geqslant 2
.

We make the following notation for band-limited subspaces. Given
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we define the space

(B.1.3)

\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subseteq\bigcap_{s\geqslant 0}\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ via }\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\{{f\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\;:\;\mathrm{supp}\mathscr{F}[f]\subseteq\overline{B(0,\kappa)}}\}.

We will also sometimes write
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
0\leqslant s\in\mathbb{R}
.

Now let us enumerate the properties of theses spaces pertinent to this work. First, we discuss a high-low decomposition for which the following notational convention is set. For
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we define the linear operators
\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}
and
\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}
on the subspace of
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{C})
such that
\mathscr{F}[f]
is locally integrable via

(B.1.4)

\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}f=\mathscr{F}^{-1}[\mathds{1}_{B(0,\kappa)}\mathscr{F}[f]]\text{ and }\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}f=(I-\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}})f.

Proposition B.1

(Frequency splitting for anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

The following hold for
0\leqslant s\in\mathbb{R}
,
f\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

(1)

We have the equivalence
\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\asymp_{s,\kappa}\sqrt{\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{H}}^{\kappa}f\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}}}
.

(2)

We have that
\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with the estimates
\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}}\lesssim_{k,\kappa}\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

This is Theorem 5.5 in Leoni and Tice
[
60
]
.
∎

The following algebra properties of the anisotropic Sobolev spaces are extremely important in our nonlinear analysis.

Proposition B.2

(Algebra properties of anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

Suppose that
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. The following hold.

(1)

The pointwise product
f_{1}f_{2}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{(2\kappa)}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies the estimate
\lVert f_{1}f_{2}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{\kappa}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
.

(2)

Set

(B.1.5)

r_{d}=\begin{cases}1+\big\lfloor\frac{d+1}{d-1}\big\rfloor,&\text{if }d>1,\\
1&\text{if }d=1.\end{cases}

Assume additionally that
f_{3},\dots,f_{r_{d}}\in\mathcal{H}_{(\kappa)}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then the pointwise product
\prod_{j=1}^{r_{d}}f_{j}
belongs to
(L^{2}\cap\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(r_{d}\cdot\kappa)})(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

(B.1.6)

\Big\lVert\prod_{j=1}^{r_{d}}f_{j}\Big\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\kappa,d}\prod_{j=1}^{r_{d}}\lVert f_{j}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

(3)

If
g\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then the pointwise product
f_{1}g
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

(B.1.7)

\lVert f_{1}g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\lesssim_{\kappa,s}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}.

Proof.

The first item is proved in Section 2.2 in Koganemaru and Tice
[
57
]
.

We now prove the second item in the case that
\kappa=1
and
d\geqslant 2
. The general case for
d\geqslant 2
can be handled similarly, and the case
d=1
is trivial. We define the function
\mu:B(0,1)\to\mathbb{R}
via
\mu(\xi)=|\xi|^{-2}(\xi_{1}^{2}+|\xi|^{4})
and note that
\mu
is the multiplier that encodes the low frequency control in
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We will calculate for which
q\in[1,\infty)
we have
1/\mu\in L^{q}(B(0,1))
. Consider the decomposition
B(0,1)=E_{0}\sqcup E_{1}

(B.1.8)

E_{0}=\{{\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi^{\ast})\in B(0,1)\;:\;|\xi_{1}\pm 1/2|^{2}+|\xi^{\ast}|^{2}<1/4}\},\quad E_{1}=B(0,1)\setminus E_{0}.

An elementary calculation shows that for
\xi\in B(0,1)
we have that

(B.1.9)

\xi\in E_{0}\;\Leftrightarrow\;|\xi|^{4}<|\xi_{1}|^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\xi\in E_{1}\;\Leftrightarrow\;|\xi_{1}|^{2}\leqslant|\xi|^{4}.

In turn, these show that

(B.1.10)

\begin{cases}\xi_{1}^{2}/\left|\xi\right|^{2}\leqslant\mu(\xi)\leqslant 2\xi_{1}^{2}/\left|\xi\right|^{2}&\text{for }\xi\in E_{0}\\
\left|\xi\right|^{2}\leqslant\mu(\xi)\leqslant 2\left|\xi\right|^{2}&\text{for }\xi\in E_{1}.\end{cases}

For
1\leqslant q<\infty
, we then have the equivalence

(B.1.11)

\int_{B(0,1)}\mu^{-q}\asymp\int_{E_{0}}\xi_{1}^{-2q}\left|\xi\right|^{2q}\;\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{E_{1}}\left|\xi\right|^{-2q}\;\mathrm{d}\xi.

We may use spherical coordinates in the
\xi^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
variable to see that

(B.1.12)

\int_{E_{1}}\left|\xi\right|^{-2q}\;\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty

if and only if

(B.1.13)

\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2}\int_{0}^{1/2-\sqrt{1/4-r^{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}x_{1}}{(x_{1}^{2}+r^{2})^{q}}\;\mathrm{d}r<\infty,

but for the latter we can bound

(B.1.14)

\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2}\int_{0}^{1/2-\sqrt{1/4-r^{2}}}\frac{dx_{1}}{(x_{1}^{2}+r^{2})^{q}}\;\mathrm{d}r\leqslant\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2-2q}\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-r^{2}}\Big)\;\mathrm{d}r,

and since the term in parentheses behaves like
r^{2}
for
r\sim 0
this will be finite if and only if
d-2-2q+2>-1\;\Leftrightarrow\;q<(d+1)/2
. By putting this together, we see that if
q<(d+1)/2
then the integral (
B.1.12
) is indeed finite. Next we consider the
E_{0}
integral, again using spherical coordinates in
\xi^{\ast}
. We have that

(B.1.15)

\int_{E_{0}}\xi_{1}^{-2q}\left|\xi\right|^{2q}\;\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty

if and only if

(B.1.16)

\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2}\int_{1/2-\sqrt{1/4-r^{2}}}^{1/2}\Big(1+\frac{r^{2}}{x_{1}^{2}}\Big)^{q}\;\mathrm{d}x_{1}\;\mathrm{d}r<\infty,

and we know that this integral is finite if we know that

(B.1.17)

\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2}\int_{1/2-\sqrt{1/4-r^{2}}}^{1/2}\Big(\frac{r^{2}}{x_{1}^{2}}\Big)^{q}\;\mathrm{d}x_{1}\;\mathrm{d}r<\infty.

For this latter integral we compute

(B.1.18)

\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2}\int_{1/2-\sqrt{1/4-r^{2}}}^{1/2}\Big(\frac{r^{2}}{x_{1}^{2}}\Big)^{q}\;\mathrm{d}x_{1}\;\mathrm{d}r\leqslant\frac{1}{2q-1}\int_{0}^{1/2}r^{d-2+2q}\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-r^{2}}\Big)^{1-2q}\;\mathrm{d}r,

and this is finite if and only if
d-2+2q+2(1-2q)>-1\Leftrightarrow q<\frac{d+1}{2}
. So once more we guarantee the integral (
B.1.15
) is finite if
q<(d+1)/2
. Hence (
B.1.11
) is finite for the same range of
q
.

Now suppose that
f\in\mathcal{H}_{(1)}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Since
\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\asymp\lVert\sqrt{\mu}(\nabla/2\pi\mathrm{i})f\rVert_{L^{2}}
, it follows from Hölder’s inequality that for
1\leqslant p<2
we have

(B.1.19)

\lVert\mathscr{F}[f]\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\Big(\int_{B(0,1)}(1/\mu)^{p/(2-p)}\Big)^{1/p-1/2}.

The above analysis shows that the coefficient given by the integral on the right hand side is finite if and only if
p/(2-p)<(d+1)/2
. This is equivalent to
p<\frac{2(d+1)}{d+3}
, and thus
\mathscr{F}[f]\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
1\leqslant p<\frac{2(d+1)}{d+3}
. In turn, thanks to the Hausdorff-Young inequality, we have that

(B.1.20)

\lVert f\rVert_{L^{q}}\lesssim_{q}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\text{ for }\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}<q\leqslant\infty.

Hence, by Hölder’s inequality, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\leqslant\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}
we have that

(B.1.21)

\lVert f^{k}\rVert_{L^{q}}\lesssim_{q,k}\lVert f\rVert^{k}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\text{ for }\frac{2(d+1)}{k(d-1)}<q\leqslant\infty.

Define
r_{d}=\min\big\{{k\in\mathbb{N}\;:\;\frac{2(d+1)}{k(d-1)}<2}\big\}
. Note that the minimum exists since
2<\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}\leqslant 6
. In fact, it is easy to see that

(B.1.22)

r_{d}=1+\Big\lfloor\frac{d+1}{d-1}\Big\rfloor=\begin{cases}4&\text{if }d=2,\\
3&\text{if }d=3,\\
2&\text{if }d>3,\end{cases}

and that
\lVert f^{r_{d}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{r_{d}}
. The second item then follows by one more application of Hölder’s inequality.

Finally, we prove the third item. Proposition
B.1
and the first item allow us to estimate

(B.1.23)

\lVert f_{1}g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\leqslant\lVert f_{1}\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}+\lVert f_{1}\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\lesssim\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\lVert f_{1}\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}g\rVert_{H^{s}}\\
\lesssim\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}+\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}\lVert\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}g\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}},

which completes the proof of the third item.
∎

Remark B.3
.

From the proof of Proposition
B.2
, specifically (
B.1.20
), and Proposition
B.1
we deduce that for
\mathbb{R}\ni s\geqslant 0
,
2(d+1)/(d-1)<q\leqslant\infty
and
f\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the low mode part
\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}f
belongs to
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and the high mode part
\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}f
belongs to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. In fact, the induced embedding
\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow(L^{q}+L^{2})(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is continuous.

We now develop a spatial characterization of the specialized Sobolev spaces that will be useful when employing these spaces in a priori estimates. Recall that the homogeneous spaces
\dot{H}^{-1}
are defined in (
1.7.4
).

Proposition B.4

(Characterizations of the anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

The following hold for
\mathbb{R}\ni s\geqslant 0
.

(1)

If
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
satisfies
\nabla f\in H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\partial_{1}f\in\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then there exists a constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that
f-c\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(2)

Assume
d\geqslant 2
and write
p_{d}=\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}>2
. Then we have the equality

(B.1.24)
