. Now suppose that
m=1
and
f\in C^{1+k}_{b}(V)
. For
(g,h)\in U
and
(g_{1},h_{1})\in W^{k,\infty}(O;\mathbb{R}^{d})\times H^{k}(O;\mathbb{R}^{d})
small enough that
(g+tg_{1},h+th_{1})\in U
for all
t\in[0,1]
, we have the identity

(D.1.31)

\Theta(f;g+g_{1},h+h_{1})-\Theta(f;g,h)-\sum_{b=1}^{n}\Theta(\partial_{b}f;g,h)(g_{1}+h_{1})_{b}\\
=\sum_{b=1}^{n}(g_{1}+h_{1})_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\left[\Theta(\partial_{b}f;g+tg_{1},h+th_{1})-\Theta(\partial_{b}f,g,h)\right]dt.

Using this and the results from Step 1, we may argue as in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem
D.2
to deduce from this that
\Theta(f;\cdot)
is differentiable on
U
and satisfies
D\Theta(f;g,h)(g_{1},h_{1})=\sum_{b=1}^{n}\Theta(\partial_{b}f;g,h)(g_{1}+h_{1})_{b}
, which may be rewritten as (
D.1.22
) when
m=1
. In turn, this formula and the results from the previous step show that
\Theta(f;\cdot)
is
C^{1}
.

Step 3
:
C^{m}
when
f\in C^{m+k}_{b}(V)
. With the
m=1
case established and formula (
D.1.22
) in hand, we may then employ a simple induction argument, which we omit for the sake of brevity, to conclude that if
f\in C^{m+k}_{b}(V)
for
m\geqslant 2
, then
\Theta(f;\cdot)
is
C^{m}
on
U
with the formula (
D.1.22
) holding for all
m\geqslant 1
.
∎

D.2.
Tools for tame estimates

In this subsection we record some useful tame estimates that form the basis for Section
3.2
and numerous other areas of our analysis. We begin by recalling two versions of the log-convexity result known as Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation. The first is for functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Theorem D.4

(Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation in full space)
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional real vector space,
1\leqslant t,p,q\leqslant\infty
and
s,r\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
be such that
1\leqslant r<s
and

(D.2.1)

\frac{r}{s}\frac{1}{p}+\Big(1-\frac{r}{s}\Big)\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{t}.

For all
\varphi\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V)\cap\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V)
we have the estimate
\lVert D^{r}\varphi\rVert_{L^{t}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{q}}^{1-r/s}\lVert D^{s}\varphi\rVert^{s/r}_{L^{p}}
, where the implicit constant depends only the dimension,
r
,
s
,
p
, and
q
.

Proof.

See, for instance, Theorem 12.85 in Leoni
[
59
]
for the proof when
V=\mathbb{R}
. The case for general
V
follows by choosing a basis and working component-wise.
∎

The second version of Gagliardo-Nirenberg is for functions in certain domains.

Corollary D.5

(Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation in domains)
.

Suppose that
V
is a finite dimensional real vector space and
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a Stein extension domain (see Definition
A.2
). Let
1\leqslant p,q,t\leqslant\infty
and
s,r\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
be such that
1\leqslant r<s
and (
D.2.1
) holds. For all
\varphi\in(L^{q}\cap W^{s,p})(U;V)
we have the estimate
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{W^{r,t}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert^{1-r/s}_{L^{q}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{W^{s,p}}^{s/r}
, where the implicit constant depends on the dimension,
r
,
s
,
p
,
q
, and
U
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{E}_{U}
denote a Stein-extension operator for the domain
U
. Given
\varphi
as in the hypotheses, we use the Theorem
D.4
followed by the log-convexity of the norm on the Lebesgue spaces to obtain the desired bound:

(D.2.2)

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{W^{r,t}(U;V)}\leqslant\lVert\mathfrak{E}_{U}\varphi\rVert_{W^{r,t}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V)}\lesssim\lVert\mathfrak{E}_{U}\varphi\rVert_{L^{t}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V)}+\lVert D^{r}\mathfrak{E}_{U}\varphi\rVert_{L^{t}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V)}\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{t}(U;V)}\\
+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{1-r/s}_{L^{q}(U;V)}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{s/r}_{W^{s,p}(U;V)}\leqslant\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{q}(U;V)}^{1-r/s}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{r/s}_{L^{p}(U;V)}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{1-r/s}_{L^{q}(U;V)}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{s/r}_{W^{s,p}(U;V)}\\
\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert^{1-r/s}_{L^{q}(U;V)}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{s/r}_{W^{s,p}(U;V)}.

∎

Armed with the interpolation theorems, we can now prove a series of useful tame estimates. We begin by studying products. The following principal result, which has a rather lengthy statement, is the source of numerous useful and simpler corollaries.

Theorem D.6

(Tame estimates on products)
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a Stein extension domain (see Definition
A.2
), and let
\ell,m,k,\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{\ell},\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfy
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}|\alpha_{i}|=\alpha
,
\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\beta_{j}|=\beta
, and
\alpha+\beta=k
. Suppose that
r,s,t,p_{1},\dots,p_{\ell},q_{1},\dots,q_{\ell},a_{1},\dots,a_{m},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\in[1,\infty]
satisfy
1/r=1/s+1/t
as well as

(D.2.3)

\frac{1}{p_{i}}-\frac{1}{q_{i}}=\frac{k}{\alpha}\Big(\frac{1}{s}-\sum_{\lambda=1}^{\ell}\frac{1}{q_{\lambda}}\Big)\text{ for }i\in\{{1,\dots,\ell}\}\text{ and }\frac{1}{a_{j}}-\frac{1}{b_{j}}=\frac{k}{\beta}\Big(\frac{1}{t}-\sum_{\mu=1}^{m}\frac{1}{b_{\mu}}\Big)\text{ for }j\in\{{1,\dots,m}\}.

Suppose additionally that

(D.2.4)

u_{i}\in(W^{k,p_{i}}\cap L^{q_{i}})(U)\text{ for }i\in\{{1,\dots,\ell}\}\text{ and }w_{j}\in(W^{k,a_{j}}\cap L^{b_{j}})(U)\text{ for }j\in\{{1,\dots,m}\}.

Then we have the product estimate

(D.2.5)

\Big\lVert\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\partial^{\alpha_{i}}u_{i}\Big)\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\partial^{\beta_{j}}w_{j}\Big)\Big\rVert_{L^{r}}\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\lVert v_{n}\rVert_{W^{k,c_{n}}}\prod_{\nu\neq n}\lVert v_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{d_{n}}},

where

(D.2.6)

v_{n}=\begin{cases}u_{n}&\text{if }n\leqslant\ell,\\
w_{n-\ell}&\text{if }\ell<n,\end{cases}\quad c_{n}=\begin{cases}p_{n}&\text{if }n\leqslant\ell,\\
a_{n-\ell}&\text{if }\ell<n,\end{cases}\text{ and }d_{n}=\begin{cases}q_{n}&\text{if }n\leqslant\ell,\\
b_{n-\ell}&\text{if }\ell<n.\end{cases}

Proof.

For
i\in\{{1,\dots,\ell}\}
and
j\in\{{1,\dots,m}\}
, we define
s_{i},t_{j}\in[1,\infty]
via

(D.2.7)

\frac{1}{s_{i}}=\frac{|\alpha_{i}|/k}{p_{i}}+\frac{1-|\alpha_{i}|/k}{q_{i}}\text{ and }\frac{1}{t_{j}}=\frac{|\beta_{j}|/k}{a_{j}}+\frac{1-|\beta_{j}|/k}{b_{j}}.

We first claim that
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}1/s_{i}=1/s
and
\sum_{j=1}^{m}1/t_{j}=1/t
. Indeed, by invoking (
D.2.3
), we see that equation (
D.2.7
) is equivalent to

(D.2.8)

\frac{1}{s_{i}}-\frac{1}{q_{i}}=\frac{|\alpha_{i}|}{k}\cdot\frac{k}{\alpha}\Big(\frac{1}{s}-\sum_{\lambda=1}^{\ell}\frac{1}{q_{\lambda}}\Big)\text{ and }\frac{1}{t_{j}}-\frac{1}{b_{j}}=\frac{|\beta_{j}|}{k}\cdot\frac{k}{\beta}\Big(\frac{1}{t}-\sum_{\mu=1}^{m}\frac{1}{b_{\mu}}\Big)

for
i\in\{{1,\dots,\ell}\}
and
j\in\{{1,\dots,m}\}
. Now we sum over
i
and
j
in these ranges and use the definition of
\alpha
and
\beta
to see that

(D.2.9)

\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\Big(\frac{1}{s_{i}}-\frac{1}{q_{i}}\Big)=\frac{1}{s}-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\frac{1}{q_{i}}\text{ and }\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\frac{1}{t_{j}}-\frac{1}{b_{j}}\Big)=\frac{1}{t}-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{b_{j}},

from which the claim immediately follows.

We next note that by hypothesis
1/r=1/s+1/t
, and so the claim allows us to begin the product estimate by applying Hölder’s inequality:

(D.2.10)

\Big\lVert\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\partial^{\alpha_{i}}u_{i}\Big)\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\partial^{\beta_{j}}w_{j}\Big)\Big\rVert_{L^{r}}\leqslant\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\lVert u_{i}\rVert_{W^{|\alpha_{i}|,s_{i}}}\Big)\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\lVert w_{j}\rVert_{W^{|\beta_{j}|,t_{j}}}\Big).

We then use (
D.2.7
) and apply Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation, Corollary
D.5
, to bound

(D.2.11)

\lVert u_{i}\rVert_{W^{|\alpha_{i}|,s_{i}}}\lesssim\lVert u_{i}\rVert_{W^{k,p_{i}}}^{|\alpha_{i}|/k}\lVert u_{i}\rVert_{L^{q_{i}}}^{1-|\alpha_{i}|,k}\text{ and }\lVert w_{j}\rVert_{W^{|\alpha_{j}|,s_{j}}}\lesssim\lVert w_{j}\rVert_{W^{k,a_{j}}}^{|\beta_{j}|/k}\lVert w_{j}\rVert_{L^{b_{j}}}^{1-|\beta_{i}|/k}.

We set
\gamma_{n}=\alpha_{n}
if
n\leqslant\ell
and
\gamma_{n}=\beta_{n-\ell}
if
n>\ell
and note that
\sum_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\left|\gamma_{n}\right|/k=(\alpha+\beta)/k=1
. The latter identity implies that if
\{B_{n}\}_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\subseteq[0,\infty)
, then

(D.2.12)

\prod_{n=1}^{m+\ell}B_{n}^{1-\left|\gamma_{n}\right|/k}=\prod_{n=1}^{m+\ell}B_{n}^{\sum_{\nu\neq n}\left|\gamma_{\nu}\right|/k}=\prod_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\prod_{\nu\neq n}B_{n}^{\left|\gamma_{\nu}\right|/k}=\prod_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\prod_{\nu\neq n}B_{\nu}^{\left|\gamma_{n}\right|/k}.

We then combine estimates (
D.2.10
) and (
D.2.11
), while using the notation of (
D.2.6
) and the identity (
D.2.12
), to arrive at the estimate

(D.2.13)

\Big\lVert\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\partial^{\alpha_{i}}u_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\partial^{\beta_{j}}w_{j}\Big\rVert_{L^{r}}\lesssim\prod_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\lVert v_{n}\rVert^{|\gamma_{n}|/k}_{W^{k,c_{n}}}\lVert v_{n}\rVert^{1-|\gamma_{n}|/k}_{L^{d_{n}}}=\prod_{n=1}^{m+\ell}\Big(\lVert v_{n}\rVert_{W^{k,c_{n}}}\prod_{\nu\neq n}\lVert v_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{d_{n}}}\Big)^{|\gamma_{n}|/k}.

Then (
D.2.5
) follows from this via an application of Young’s inequality.
∎

We now can derive more familiar-looking high-low type product estimates.

Corollary D.7

(Tame estimates on simple multipliers)
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a Stein extension domain (see Definition
A.2
),
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\varphi\in H^{k}(U)
. The following hold.

(1)

Suppose that
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
are such that
|\alpha|+|\beta|=k
and
\psi\in H^{\max\{{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,k}\}}(U)
. Then the product
\partial^{\alpha}\psi\partial^{\beta}\varphi
belongs to
L^{2}(U)
and obeys the bound

(D.2.14)

\lVert\partial^{\alpha}\psi\partial^{\beta}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{k}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }k\leqslant\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor<k.\end{cases}

(2)

Suppose that
\psi\in H^{\max\{{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,k}\}}(U)
. Then the product
\psi\varphi
belongs to
H^{k}(U)
and obeys the bound

(D.2.15)

\lVert\psi\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{k}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }k\leqslant 1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert\psi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor<k.\end{cases}

(3)

Suppose that
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
are such that
|\alpha|+|\beta|=k
and
\psi\in W^{k,\infty}(U)
. Then the product
\partial^{\alpha}\psi\partial^{\beta}\varphi
belongs to
L^{2}(U)
and obeys the bound

(D.2.16)

\lVert\partial^{\alpha}\psi\partial^{\beta}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}+\lVert\psi\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

(4)

Suppose that
\psi\in W^{k,\infty}(U)
. Then the product
\psi\varphi
belongs to
H^{k}(U)
and obeys the bound

(D.2.17)

\lVert\psi\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}+\lVert\psi\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

The second and fourth items follow directly from the Leibniz rule and the first and third items, respectively. The third item follows as a direct application of Theorem
D.6
in the case that
\ell=2
,
m=0
,
r=2
,
p_{1}=2
,
p_{2}=\infty
,
q_{1}=2
,
q_{2}=\infty
. It remains to prove the first item. In the case
1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor<k
, the estimate of the first item again follows from Theorem
D.6
, applied with
\ell=2
,
m=0
,
r=2
,
p_{1}=p_{2}=2
,
q_{1}=q_{2}=\infty
, combined with the supercritical Sobolev embedding
H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}(U)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(U)
.

Now consider the remaining case,
k\leqslant\lfloor d/2\rfloor
. We consider first what happens when
k<d/2
. In this case we can invoke the subcritical Sobolev embedding
H^{k}(U)\hookrightarrow L^{\frac{2d}{d-2k}}(U)
to see that
\varphi\in(W^{k,p_{1}}\cap L^{q_{1}})(U)
for
p_{1}=2
and
q_{1}=\frac{2d}{d-2k}
. On the other hand, the supercritical Sobolev embedding, Corollary
D.5
, and the estimate
1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor>d/2>k
show that we have the embeddings

(D.2.18)

H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}(U)\hookrightarrow H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}(U)\cap L^{\infty}(U)\hookrightarrow W^{k,2(1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor)/k}(U)\hookrightarrow W^{k,d/k}(U).

This implies that
\psi\in(L^{\infty}\cap H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor})(U)
. We set
p_{2}=d/k
and
q_{2}=\infty
and note that the hypotheses of Theorem
D.6
are satisfied with
r=2
,
\ell=2
, and
m=0
again; the bound (
D.2.14
) when
k<d/2
then follows from the product estimate provided by the theorem.

Finally, we consider the case
k=d/2
. Thanks to Corollary
D.5
again, we obtain that
\psi\in H^{1+d/2}(U)\hookrightarrow W^{k,(4+2d)/d}(U)
, which motivates setting
p_{2}=\frac{4+2d}{d}
and
q_{2}=\infty
. The critical Sobolev embedding implies that
\varphi\in H^{k}(U)\hookrightarrow L^{2+d}(U)
, which dictates that we set
p_{1}=2
,
q_{1}=2+d
, and
r=2
. Using these parameters, we again apply Theorem
D.6
to get (
D.2.14
) when
k=d/2
, which completes the proof of the first item.
∎

Next we consider tame bounds on commutator expressions.

Corollary D.8

(Tame estimates on commutators)
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a Stein extension domain (see Definition
A.2
),
k,m\in\mathbb{N}
with
m\geqslant 1
,
j\in\{{1,\dots,d}\}
, and
\varphi\in H^{k+m-1}(U)
. The following hold.

(1)

Suppose that
\psi\in H^{\max\{{2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,k+m}\}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
satisfies
\partial_{j}\psi\in H^{\max\{{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,k+m-1}\}}(U)
. Then the commutator
[\partial_{j}^{m},\psi]\varphi
belongs to
H^{k}(U)
and obeys the bound

(D.2.19)

\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},\psi]\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k+m-1}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }k+m\leqslant 2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{H^{k+m-1}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor<k+m.\end{cases}

(2)

Suppose that
\psi\in W^{k+m,\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
satisfies
\partial_{j}\psi\in W^{k+m-1,\infty}(U)
. Then the commutator
[\partial_{j}^{m},\psi]\varphi
belongs to
H^{k}(U)
and obeys the bound

(D.2.20)

\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},\psi]\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k+m-1}}+\lVert\partial_{j}\psi\rVert_{W^{k+m-1,\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leqslant k
. By the Leibniz rule we have

(D.2.21)

\partial^{\alpha}([\partial_{j}^{m},\psi]\varphi)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\sum_{\beta\leqslant\alpha}c_{\ell,m,\beta,\alpha}\partial^{\beta}\partial_{j}^{\ell}\partial_{j}\psi\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{j}^{m-1-\ell}\varphi,

and hence

(D.2.22)

\lVert[\partial_{j}^{m},\psi]\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|\leqslant k}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\sum_{\beta\leqslant\alpha}\lVert\partial^{\beta}\partial_{j}^{\ell}\partial_{j}\psi\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{j}^{m-1-\ell}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

To prove the first item, we now apply the first conclusion of Corollary
D.7
and use that
|\alpha|\leqslant k
. To prove the second item, we instead use the third conclusion of the aforementioned corollary and that
\ell\leqslant m-1
in these sums.
∎

Now we derive tame bounds on superposition multipliers.

Corollary D.9

(Tame estimates on superposition multipliers)
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a Stein extension domain (see Definition
A.2
),
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, and
V
be a finite dimensional real vector space. Suppose that
f\in W^{k,\infty}(V)
,
g\in W^{k,\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;V)
is such that
Dg\in W^{k-1,\infty}(U;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V))
, and
\psi\in H^{k}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;V)
is such that
D\psi\in(L^{\infty}\cap H^{k-1})(U;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V))
. Let
R\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
S\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\cup\{\infty\}
satisfy

(D.2.23)

\langle\lVert f\rVert_{W^{1,\infty}},\lVert Dg\rVert_{L^{\infty}},\lVert D\psi\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}\rangle\leqslant R\text{ and }\langle\lVert f\rVert_{W^{2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\infty}},\lVert Dg\rVert_{W^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\infty}},\lVert D\psi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}\rangle\leqslant S.

Then the following hold.

(1)

If
\varphi\in(L^{\infty}\cap H^{k})(U)
, then the product
f(g+\psi)\varphi
belongs to
H^{k}(U)
and obeys the estimate

(D.2.24)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim R^{k+1}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}+R^{k}\lVert f,Dg,D\psi\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}\times W^{k-1,\infty}\times H^{k-1}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}.

(2)

If
\varphi\in H^{k}(U)
and we assume additionally that
S<\infty
, then the product
f(g+\psi)\varphi
belongs to
H^{k}(U)
and obeys the estimate

(D.2.25)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim S^{k+1}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}+S^{k}\begin{cases}0&\text{if }k\leqslant 1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert f,Dg,D\psi\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}\times W^{k-1,\infty}\times H^{k-1}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}&\text{if }1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor<k.\end{cases}

(3)

Suppose that
\varphi\in H^{k}(U)
,
S<\infty
, and there is a Stein-extension domain
O\subseteq V
such that
f\in W^{k,\infty}(O)
. If the image of
g+\psi
is a subset of
O
, then the inclusion and estimate from the second item hold.

Proof.

We begin by proving the first item. We start by estimating

(D.2.26)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\lVert f(g+\psi)\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert D^{k}[f(g+\psi)\varphi]\rVert_{L^{2}}

and
\lVert f(g+\psi)\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}
. For the second term in (
D.2.26
) we have to work harder. By the differentiation rules for products and compositions, we have that

(D.2.27)

D^{k}[f(g+\psi)\varphi]=f(g+\psi)D^{k}\varphi+\mathrm{sym}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{j+1}\sum_{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{\ell}=j+1}c_{j+1,k,\ell,j_{1},\dots,j_{\ell}}\\
\cdot D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\{{D^{j_{1}}(g+\psi),\dots,D^{j_{\ell}}(g+\psi)}\}\otimes D^{k-1-j}\varphi=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II},

where
\mathrm{sym}
denotes symmetrization of the multilinear map. We will estimate the
L^{2}
-norm of
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
separately. We handle
\mathbf{I}
trivially:

(D.2.28)

\lVert\mathbf{I}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}}.

For
\mathbf{II}
, we use the triangle inequality and study each term in the series:

(D.2.29)

\lVert\mathbf{II}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{j+1}\sum_{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{\ell}=j+1}\lVert D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\{{D^{j_{1}}(g+\psi),\dots,D^{j_{\ell}}(g+\psi)}\}\otimes D^{k-1-j}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

By the
\ell
-multilinearity of
D^{\ell}f
and symmetry considerations, we have the upper bound

(D.2.30)

\lVert\mathbf{II}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{j+1}\sum_{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{\ell}=j+1}\sum_{\upnu=0}^{\ell}\lVert D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\{{D^{j_{1}}\psi,\dots,D^{j_{\upnu}}\psi,D^{j_{\upnu+1}}g,\dots,D^{j_{\ell}}g}\}\otimes D^{k-1-j}\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

We would like to apply Theorem
D.6
to the summands in this expression, but due to the appearance of
D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)
terms we cannot verify the theorem’s hypotheses. Instead, we modify the argument used to prove the theorem. Given
j\in\{{0,\dots,k-1}\}
,
\ell\in\{{1,\dots,j+1}\}
,
j_{1}+\cdots+j_{\ell}=j+1
, and
\upnu\in\{{0,\dots,\ell}\}
, we set
\alpha=k-1-j+\sum_{\nu=1}^{\upnu}(j_{\nu}-1)
,
\beta=\ell-1+\sum_{\nu=\upnu+1}^{\ell}(j_{\nu}-1)
,
\alpha+\beta=k-1
,
r=s=2
,
t=a_{1}=b_{1}=\cdots=a_{\ell-\upnu+1}=b_{\ell-\upnu+1}=\infty
,
p_{1}=\cdots=p_{\upnu+1}=2
,
q_{1}=\cdots=q_{\upnu+1}=2\frac{(k-1)(\upnu+1)-\alpha}{k-1-\alpha}
,
u_{1}=\cdots=u_{\upnu}=D\psi
,
u_{\upnu+1}=\varphi
,
w_{1}=Df
, and
w_{2}=\cdots=w_{\ell-\upnu+1}=Dg
. The argument used to prove Theorem
D.6
then pushes through for the summands in (
D.2.30
) with these parameters, thanks to the trivial bound
\lVert D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant\lVert D^{\ell}f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
; this results in the estimate

(D.2.31)

\lVert\mathbf{II}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\langle\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}\times(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty})}\rangle^{k-1}\big(\lVert Df\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}\times(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty})}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k-1}}\\
+\lVert Df\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{W^{k-1,\infty}\times H^{k-1}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}+\lVert Df\rVert_{W^{k-1,\infty}}\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}\times(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty})}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}\big).

Upon combining estimates (
D.2.26
), (
D.2.28
), and (
D.2.31
), we arrive at the desired conclusion, estimate (
D.2.24
), of the first item.

The second item in the case that
1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor\leqslant k
follows from estimate (
D.2.24
) of the first item, the fact that
1\leqslant R\lesssim S
, and the supercritical Sobolev embedding
H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\hookrightarrow L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}
. On the other hand, we have the trivial estimate

(D.2.32)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant S\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}.

This shows that the linear map
\varphi\mapsto f(g+\psi)\varphi
has
S^{2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}
as an upper-bound on the
\mathcal{L}(H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}(U))
operator norm and has
S
as an upper-bound on the
\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(U))
operator norm. Employing operator interpolation (see, for instance, Bergh and Löfström
[
9
]
) with these bounds, we achieve (
D.2.25
) in the cases
k\leqslant 1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor
, which completes the proof of the second item.

The third item follows from the second item applied when
f
is replaced by
\mathfrak{E}_{O}f
, where
\mathfrak{E}_{O}
is a Stein extension operator for
O
, and the observation that the image hypothesis on
g+\psi
ensures that
(\mathfrak{E}_{O}f)(g+\psi)=f(g+\psi)
.
∎

Now we consider superposition on its own, not as a multiplier.

Corollary D.10

(Tame estimates on superposition)
.

Let
\mathbb{N}\ni k\geqslant 2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor
. The following hold.

(1)

Let
V
,
W
be finite dimensional real vector spaces,
O\subseteq V
be a Stein extension domain (see Definition
A.2
) containing
0
that is star shaped with respect to
0
, and
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a Stein extension domain. If
f\in W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{k,\infty}(O;W)
is such that
f(0)=0
and
Df\in W^{k-1,\infty}(O;\mathcal{L}(V;W))
, and
\varphi\in H^{k}(U;V)
is such that
\varphi(U)\subseteq O
, then the superposition
f(\varphi)
belongs to
H^{k}(U;W)
and obeys the estimate

(D.2.33)

\lVert f(\varphi)\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim S^{k}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{k}},

for any
S\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfying
\langle\lVert Df\rVert_{W^{k-1,\infty}},\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\rangle\leqslant S
.

(2)

Suppose that
f\in H^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
g,\psi\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
are such that
Dg\in W^{k-1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
,
D\psi\in H^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
, and
g+\psi
is a bi-Lipschitz and
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The superposition
f(g+\psi)
belongs to
H^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies the estimate

(D.2.34)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim S^{k+1}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{k}}+S^{k}\lVert Dg,D\varphi\rVert_{W^{k-1,\infty}\times H^{k-1}}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}},

for any
S\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfying

(D.2.35)

\langle\lVert\det D(g+\psi)^{-1}\rVert_{L^{\infty}},\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{W^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\infty}\times H^{1+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}\rangle\leqslant S.

Proof.

We begin by proving the first item. Since
f(0)=0
and
O
is star-shaped with respect to the origin of
V
, we can use the fundamental theorem of calculus to obtain the equality
f(\varphi)=\int_{0}^{1}Df(t\varphi)[\varphi]\;\mathrm{d}t
, from which we deduce that
\lVert f(\varphi)\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\lVert Df\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}}
. We next establish an
H^{k-1}
-bound on the derivative of the superposition,
D(f(\varphi))=Df(\varphi)D\varphi
. For this we utilize the third item of Corollary
D.9
and obtain the bound
\lVert D(f(\varphi))\rVert_{H^{k-1}}\lesssim S^{k}\lVert D\varphi\rVert_{H^{k-1}}
. Together, these estimate give the conclusion of the first item.

We next prove the second item, noting initially that

(D.2.36)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\rVert_{H^{k}}\lesssim\lVert f(g+\psi)\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert D^{k}[f(g+\psi)]\rVert_{L^{2}}.

For the first
L^{2}
-norm, we use that
(g+\psi)
is a bi-Lipschitz and
C^{1}
diffeomorphism to estimate

(D.2.37)

\lVert f(g+\psi)\rVert_{L^{2}}=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f|^{2}|\det D(g+\psi)^{-1}|\Big)^{1/2}\leqslant S^{1/2}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}.

For the latter term of (
D.2.36
), we would like to use Theorem
D.6
, but as in the proof of Corollary
D.9
, we cannot quite do so. Instead, we use the differentiation rules for composition and argue in a manner similar to the proof of the first item in Corollary
D.9
. Indeed, we have the formula

(D.2.38)

D^{k}[f(g+\psi)]=\mathrm{sym}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{j_{1}+\dots+j_{\ell}=k}c_{\ell,k,j_{1},\dots,j_{\ell}}D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\{{D^{j_{1}}(g+\psi),\dots,D^{j_{\ell}}(g+\psi)}\},

where
\mathrm{sym}
denotes the symmetrization operator for multilinear maps. By multilinearity and symmetry considerations, we then deduce the upper bound

(D.2.39)

\lVert D^{k}[f(g+\psi)]\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{\ell}=k}\sum_{\upnu=0}^{\ell}\lVert D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\{{D^{j_{1}}\psi,\dots D^{j_{\upnu}}\psi,D^{j_{\upnu+1}}g,\dots,D^{j_{\ell}}g}\}\rVert_{L^{2}}.

To each summand on the right hand side above we apply the argument from the proof of Theorem
D.6
as follows. Given
\ell\in\{{1,\dots,k}\}
,
j_{1}+\cdots+j_{\ell}=k
, and
\upnu\in\{{0,1,\dots,\ell}\}
, we define
\alpha=\ell-1+\sum_{\nu=1}^{\upnu}(j_{\nu}-1)
,
\beta=\sum_{\nu=\upnu+1}^{\ell}(j_{\nu}-1)
,
\alpha+\beta=k-1
,
r=s=2
,
t=a_{1}=b_{1}=\cdots=a_{\ell-\upnu}=b_{\ell-\upnu}=\infty
,
p_{1}=\cdots=p_{\upnu+1}=2
,
q_{1}=\cdots=q_{\upnu+1}=2\frac{(k-1)(\upnu+1)-\alpha}{k-1-\alpha}
,
u_{1}=Df
,
u_{2}=\cdots=u_{\upnu+1}=D\psi
, and
w_{1}=\cdots=w_{\ell-\upnu}=Dg
. The argument used in the theorem pushes through so long as we carry an extra factor of
S
, thanks to the bounds

(D.2.40)

\lVert D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\rVert_{L^{\chi}}\leqslant S^{1/\chi}\lVert D^{\ell}f\rVert_{L^{\chi}}\leqslant S\lVert D^{\ell}f\rVert_{L^{\chi}}\text{ for all }\chi\in[1,\infty].

Therefore, we deduce the estimate

(D.2.41)

\lVert D^{\ell}f(g+\psi)\{{D^{j_{1}}\psi,\dots D^{j_{\upnu}}\psi,D^{j_{\upnu+1}}g,\dots,D^{j_{\ell}}g}\}\rVert_{L^{2}}\\
\lesssim S\langle\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}\times(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty})}\rangle^{\ell-1}\big(\lVert Df\rVert_{H^{k-1}}\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{L^{\infty}\times(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty})}\\
+\lVert Df\rVert_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{W^{k-1,\infty}\times H^{k-1}}\big)\lesssim S^{\ell+1}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{k}}+S^{\ell}\lVert Dg,D\psi\rVert_{W^{k-1,\infty}\times H^{k-1}}\lVert f\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}}.

Upon combining (
D.2.36
), (
D.2.37
), (
D.2.39
), and (
D.2.41
), we acquire the desired bound, (
D.2.34
).
∎

Notation Index
